# Session status & handoff log

> Part of Trakka's modular instructions — see the root [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) for the orientation index. This file is the running, session-by-session historical journal: what's been built, what's been verified (and how), what's known-broken or unverified, and the exact "pick this project back up" framing text to paste at the start of a new session. It is **not** a standing design reference — for that, see [architecture.md](architecture.md), [backend.md](backend.md), [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md), and [ci-security.md](ci-security.md). Update this file at the end of every significant work session rather than letting it drift, exactly as its predecessor section in `CLAUDE.md` used to be updated.

## Project status and session handoff (last updated: 2026-09-30, "Refactor: recurring tasks & reminders cleanup": no behavior, UI or API change — deduplication across `internal/recurrence`/`handlers`/`db` and the recurrence JS, one index-only migration 23 for the every-minute reminder scan; before that, "Custom recurrence: weekdays, interval, start date (RRULE)": recurrence rules become a canonical iCalendar RRULE subset (`FREQ=WEEKLY;INTERVAL=2;BYDAY=MO,WE,FR`), new `internal/recurrence` package and shared `static/js/recurrence.js`, a "Répétition personnalisée" sheet, migration 22; before that, "Recurring tasks, due times & task notifications": a checked-off recurring task now stays in "Terminés" with a `next_due_date` and comes back on its own; due times, an "at the exact due time" reminder mode, custom every-n-days/weeks/months recurrence, a "🔁 Échéance suivante" badge and a "Notifications de tâches" settings section; migration 21; before that, "Fix: Android system back closed the installed PWA": one history entry per open level makes back close the open modal/list first; before that, "Emoji picker for list icons": a searchable, categorized emoji picker replaces the list modal's five fixed preset buttons; new `static/js/emoji-picker.js`, `static/emoji/{fr,en}.json` generated by `tools/genemoji`; before that, "Multi-select / bulk actions in the list detail view" and "Budget summary follows the active filters")

This section is a running log for you (the user) and Claude Code across sessions. It reflects the observed state of the repo as of the date above (build/vet/gofmt verified). Update it at the end of every significant work session rather than letting it drift.

### What's done

- **Complete backend**: `cmd/server`, `internal/config`, `internal/models`, `internal/db` (houses, lists, items, users, sessions, house_members, schema, errors), `internal/auth` (password hashing, session tokens, hand-rolled OIDC client), `internal/handlers` (houses, house_members, lists, items, health, middleware, auth_middleware, auth, rbac, json, scrape, recurrence), `internal/validate`, `internal/scraper` (background price lookup). Builds, `go vet`, and `gofmt -l` are clean.
- **Automatic price & image lookup**: creating an item (or changing an existing one's `url`) kicks off a best-effort fetch of that URL, in a single request, looking for a price (OpenGraph, JSON-LD, or `itemprop="price"` microdata — skipped only if `price` was given explicitly) and a product image (OpenGraph `og:image`, JSON-LD `image`, or `twitter:image`), writing back `items.price_auto = 1`/`items.image_url` for whichever was found — see the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) for the full design (the `UpdateItemPriceIfMissing`/`UpdateItemImageIfMissing` race guards, the SSRF-hardened `http.Client` in `internal/scraper`, the bounded `syncScrapeTimeout` wait, why a slow lookup still can't block the request indefinitely). The frontend (`static/js/list_view.js`) updates the item row and the finance summary bar directly from the create/update/patch response — no `location.reload()` anywhere in the app — showing a small clickable badge next to an auto-detected price, a pulsing "Détection du prix…" badge while `price_status: "pending"` (with a single delayed re-fetch via `scheduleAutoPriceRefresh` to pick the result up), a "Prix en attente de synchro" badge for an item still sitting in the offline sync queue (`isOfflineQueuedItem`, cleared automatically once `sw.js`'s `trakka-sync-complete` message triggers a full list refetch), and — when `image_url` is present — a 48px `buildItemThumbnail` clickable/hoverable thumbnail that opens a lightbox modal (`#image-preview-modal`) rather than navigating; no space is reserved when there's no image. Verified: unit tests for the HTML/JSON-LD/microdata price and image extraction, including relative-to-absolute image URL resolution and unsafe-scheme rejection (`internal/scraper/scraper_test.go`), a DB-layer test for both three-way race guards (`internal/db/items_test.go`), `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt` clean — but **not** against a real e-commerce site with actual price/image markup (no reliable, stable public test fixture was available in the sandbox this was built in; `example.com` has neither, so only the "nothing found" path and the SSRF rejection of `127.0.0.1` were exercised live), and none of the frontend (thumbnail rendering, hover-zoom, lightbox modal, bounded-wait/pending-badge behavior) has been exercised in a browser yet. Try it against a real product page (a Shopify store, an Amazon/Fnac/Darty listing, etc.) at least once before relying on it.
- **Scraper hardening for real-world storefronts (Amazon, Reichelt)**: `internal/scraper` sends a real desktop-Chrome `User-Agent` and `Accept-Language: fr-FR,fr;q=0.9,en-US;q=0.8,en;q=0.7` instead of the old `TrakkaBot/1.0` identifying string, since several storefronts blocked or served a stripped no-price page to it. Amazon hosts (`isAmazonHost`) get dedicated image handling: `#landingImage`/`#imgBlkFront`'s `data-old-hires`/`data-a-dynamic-image` attributes (the real product photo) are tried before anything else, and `og:image` — which on Amazon is frequently the site logo or a nav icon rather than the product — is only used as a fallback, and rejected outright if its URL contains an Amazon-chrome term (`amazon-logo`/`nav2`/`favicon`/`tile`). Price extraction now checks JSON-LD before meta tags (previously the reverse) and gained a Twitter Card `twitter:dataN`/`twitter:labelN`-pair fallback (some storefronts, Reichelt among them, expose a price only that way); a latent bug where `<meta itemprop="price" content="...">`-style microdata was never actually checked (the itemprop check only ran for non-`<meta>` elements) is fixed by checking `itemprop` on every element regardless of tag. Image extraction gained an `itemprop="image"` microdata source. `ProductInfo` gained a `Title` field (JSON-LD `name` preferring an actual `@type: "Product"` object, then `og:title`, then `<title>`) — extracted since it's free from the same fetch, but **not yet wired to anything downstream** (no handler/DB path uses it; `internal/handlers`/`internal/db` are unchanged by this session). None of this changes how a price is stored or displayed — `ProductInfo.Price` is still a bare `float64`, and the frontend's hardcoded EUR formatter still renders it with a currency symbol exactly as before; only the underlying extraction got more sources and better priority ordering. Full design in the "Automatic price & image lookup" bullet above. Verified: `internal/scraper/scraper_test.go` gained tests for the Amazon image path (`data-old-hires`, `data-a-dynamic-image` largest-wins, generic-term rejection, the non-generic og:image fallback), microdata image, meta-tag microdata price, the Twitter Card price fallback, the JSON-LD-over-OpenGraph price reordering, and all three title sources — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: still never exercised against a real Amazon or Reichelt product page (or any real site) — only synthetic HTML fixtures, same caveat the original price/image lookup feature already carried. Try it against a real Amazon and a real Reichelt listing at least once before relying on it.
- **Amazon-specific price selectors**: the generic OpenGraph/JSON-LD/microdata price chain frequently comes up empty on a real Amazon product page even after the scraper hardening above got the image working — Amazon's actual displayed price mostly lives in page-specific markup (`a-offscreen` spans, `priceblock_*` ids from older templates) that none of those generic sources capture, and its JSON-LD price, when present at all, is often stale relative to what's actually shown. `internal/scraper.resolveAmazonPrice` (`internal/scraper/scraper.go`) tries five Amazon-only sources, in priority order, before falling back to anything generic: (1) an `a-offscreen` span's text nested inside `apexPriceToPay`/`#corePrice_desktop` (Amazon's current "buy box" template), (2) an `a-offscreen` span nested inside any other `a-price` element (first one found), (3) `#priceblock_ourprice`/`#priceblock_dealprice`/`#priceblock_saleprice` (older template), (4) the `a-color-price` class, (5) `#price_inside_buybox`. Since `extractProductInfo`'s single tree-walk has no ancestor stack, `walk` now threads two booleans (`inAmazonPrimaryPrice`/`inAmazonAPrice`) down through each recursive call instead, set the moment a node itself matches `apexPriceToPay`/`#corePrice_desktop`/`a-price` and inherited by every descendant — this is what lets a nested `span.a-offscreen` know which priority tier it's in without a separate pass. Only on an Amazon host (`isAmazon`) are these five tried at all, and only *before* JSON-LD — the reverse of the generic (non-Amazon) chain's order, and a deliberate exception to it, for the reason above. If none of the five match, resolution falls through to the existing generic chain unchanged: JSON-LD (`searchJSONPrice`'s key list gained `highPrice` alongside the existing `price`/`priceAmount`/`lowPrice`, since some Amazon `AggregateOffer` blocks carry only a low/high range), then an Amazon `data-asin-price`/`data-price` element attribute (also collected during the same walk), then `og:price:amount`/`product:price:amount`, the Twitter Card fallback, and classic microdata — so a non-Amazon host's behavior, and every previously-passing test, is unchanged. As with every other price source in this file, `ProductInfo.Price` stays a bare `float64` with no currency symbol — `parsePriceString` already tolerates `"14,99 €"`-style text (it strips everything but digits/`,`/`.` before parsing), so no new cleaning logic was needed to get a correct numeric value out of Amazon's `"14,99&nbsp;€"`-shaped markup; the frontend's existing hardcoded-EUR formatter still owns turning the number back into a displayed price, per the documented reasoning in the "Automatic price & image lookup" bullet above. Verified: eleven new cases in `internal/scraper/scraper_test.go` (all five selectors individually, the Amazon-selectors-beat-JSON-LD priority case, the JSON-LD fallback when no selector matches, `highPrice`, and the `data-asin-price` fallback) plus every pre-existing test — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: same caveat as the scraper-hardening entry above — these selectors were written against the Amazon DOM structures described in the request that prompted them, not exercised against a real, live `amazon.*` page (no such page was available in the sandbox this was built in); confirm against a real Amazon listing before relying on it, and revisit if Amazon changes its markup (page-specific CSS-class scraping is inherently more fragile than the structured-data sources it now falls back to).
- **Amazon anti-blocking headers, JS-state price, and raw-body last resort**: a real report against a live Amazon URL (`amazon.fr/dp/...`) found the price still coming back empty even with the DOM selectors above in place — the two suspected causes are Amazon serving its CAPTCHA/"Server Busy" anti-bot challenge page instead of the product page, and/or a page variant that renders its price client-side from embedded JS state rather than in static HTML the selectors can see. Three changes address this, all in `internal/scraper/scraper.go`: (1) `setBrowserHeaders` (split out of `FetchProductInfo` specifically so it's unit-testable against a bare `*http.Request` — the SSRF guard refuses to let a test actually fetch from a loopback `httptest` server) now sets a full realistic Chrome navigation fingerprint on every request, not just `User-Agent`/`Accept`/`Accept-Language` as before: `Device-Memory`, `Sec-Ch-Ua`/`Sec-Ch-Ua-Mobile`/`Sec-Ch-Ua-Platform` (Client Hints), and `Sec-Fetch-Dest`/`Mode`/`Site`/`User` (Fetch Metadata) — `userAgent`'s Chrome version was bumped to `124` to match `secChUa`'s, since a mismatched pair is itself a bot signal some anti-bot checks look for. (2) `amazonScriptStatePrice`, tried only on an Amazon host and only after every DOM/JSON-LD/data-attribute source already tried above comes up empty: it scans the raw text of *every* `<script>` tag on the page (not just `application/ld+json` ones, which is all `ldjsonBlocks` ever collected) for a `priceAmount`/`displayPrice`/`buyingPrice`/`price` key immediately followed by a number — covering an Amazon widget payload like `<script type="a-state" data-a-state='{"key":"..."}'>{"priceAmount":14.99,...}</script>`, whose price would otherwise be invisible to every structured source above since it's inline JS state, not markup. (3) `amazonRawBodyPrice` is the absolute last resort — tried only on an Amazon host and only if literally everything else (selectors, JSON-LD, data-attribute, script-state, and even the generic OpenGraph/Twitter/microdata chain) found nothing at all: three direct regexes (`"priceAmount":\s*([0-9]+\.?[0-9]*)`, `"price":\s*"([0-9]+[.,][0-9]{2})"`, `class="a-offscreen">([0-9]+[.,][0-9]{2})\s*€`) run against the raw, unparsed response body — this is why `extractProductInfo` now reads its input fully into a `[]byte` up front (`io.ReadAll` then `html.Parse(bytes.NewReader(body))`) instead of streaming straight into `html.Parse`, so that raw byte slice is still available for this tier after DOM parsing. Separately, `FetchProductInfo` gained a `logger *slog.Logger` parameter (threaded from `app.Logger` at both call sites, `internal/handlers/scrape.go` and `internal/handlers/price_alerts.go`) purely to log one `Debug` line (`logIfAmazonBlocked`) when a fetched Amazon page's body contains `"Server Busy"` or `"Type the characters you see in this image"` — Amazon's own anti-bot challenge page text — since that failure mode otherwise looks identical to "genuinely no price on this page" from every caller's point of view, and being able to tell the two apart in the logs is the whole point of the addition; `logger` may be `nil` (checked before use) since not every future caller necessarily has one. None of this touches how a price is stored/displayed — every new source still ultimately funnels through `parsePriceString`, and `ProductInfo.Price` is still a bare `float64` — see the "Amazon-specific price selectors" bullet above and "Automatic price & image lookup" further above for that reasoning, which is unchanged. Verified: 13 new test cases in `internal/scraper/scraper_test.go` (`setBrowserHeaders`' full header set including the UA/Client-Hints version match, the script-state fallback via two different script shapes and its non-Amazon-host no-op, the raw-body fallback via an HTML-comment-hidden price its non-Amazon-host no-op, a priority-ordering test confirming the DOM selector still wins when all three tiers could match, and `logIfAmazonBlocked`'s four cases — logs on each marker, silent on an ordinary page, silent on a non-Amazon host, and a nil-logger no-panic case) plus every pre-existing test in the package — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: still not exercised against the actual reported URL or any other live Amazon page (no network access in the sandbox this was built in) — the anti-bot headers and both new fallback tiers were written from the failure description and Amazon's known page structures, not confirmed against a real response; if the price is still empty after this, the next things to check against a real fetch are (a) whether Amazon's response body actually now differs from a blocked page (the new debug log should say so directly) and (b) capturing a real page's HTML to see whether the price lives somewhere none of these three tiers anticipated.
- **Complete REST v1 API** documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md): CRUD for `houses` (RBAC-scoped), `house_members` (roster + invite-by-email + remove), `lists` and `items`, filtering (including lists by `?house_id=`), partial `PATCH`, `/api/v1/me`, `/healthz`.
- **Authentication & authorization**: local email+password (bcrypt) and generic OIDC/OAuth2 (Authelia/Authentik/Keycloak/Google, ...) both establish an opaque, server-side session via HTTP-only `SameSite=Lax` cookie. Every `/api/v1/...` request requires a valid session (`RequireSession` middleware); `house_members` gives every house one or more members with a role (`owner`/`member`), and a house's lists/items are only visible to its members. New users get a personal "Ma Maison" house automatically. Login page at `templates/login.html` (`html/template`, the first server-rendered page in this app). See the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) for the full design (import-cycle reasoning for keeping this in `internal/handlers` rather than a separate `internal/middleware` package, the hand-rolled OIDC/JWT verification, the `sql.Tx` in `CreateHouseWithOwner`, the CSRF reasoning). Full manual smoke test (register/login/logout, RBAC isolation between two users, invite flow, wrong-password/duplicate-email handling) verified end-to-end via curl and a headless-Chromium (Playwright) browser script — no dedicated test file was added to the repo since there's no test tooling set up yet (see "What's left" below).
- **FR/EN multilingual UI**: `static/locales/fr.json`/`en.json` + `static/js/i18n.js` — see the "UI language" bullet in .claude/frontend-pwa.md's Conventions section for the full mechanism. Covers the dashboard/header/modals/item-form static markup plus the network-status text, the "N en attente" badge, and the delete/remove-member `aria-label`s in JS. **Not yet covered**: `templates/login.html` (still French-only, server-rendered, never touches `i18n.js`) — the JS-generated strings in `list_view.js`/`app.js` this originally flagged (item counts, "Terminés (N)", API error messages, empty-state text) were tracked down and fixed in a later session; see the "Fix: dynamic JS-built strings across `app.js`/`list_view.js` stayed hardcoded French even in English mode" bullet further down this log for the full account — this sentence is kept only for history. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` caches `/js/i18n.js` and both locale JSON files (cache bumped to `v8` for this). Verified end-to-end in a real headless-Chrome session (register → create list/item → toggle FR/EN → confirm both static and dynamic strings update, no console errors).
- **Purchase planning / budget forecasting**: items can carry an optional `target_month` (`TEXT`, `YYYY-MM`, validated by `internal/validate.Month`, added to the `items` table the same `addColumnIfMissing`-guarded way as `price`/`image_url` — see the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md)). Deliberately modeled on `items`, not `lists`, since the goal is scheduling individual planned purchases, not whole lists. `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH /api/v1/items` all accept it (see [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#post-apiv1items)); `PATCH` follows the same "absent = untouched, empty string = clear" convention already used for `url`. The frontend adds a second dashboard tab, "Budget & Prévisions Achats" (`static/js/planning.js`, `#tab-planning`/`#planning-view` in `index.html`), scoped to the current house like the existing lists tab: it fetches every shopping list's items (the same `?house_id=` + per-list fan-out `loadDashboard` already does), keeps only the ones with a `target_month` set, and groups them by month over a selectable 3/6/12-month horizon (the current calendar month always counts as the first of "the next N", not skipped), showing a running total and a per-month subtotal. Each item row has a `<select>` (options: unschedule, or any of the next 12 months regardless of the active horizon filter) that `PATCH`es the item's `target_month` immediately — this is the "report a purchase to another month" UI the feature was built for. Scheduling an item in the first place happens from the ordinary item create/edit UI (`static/js/list_view.js`): a shopping-list-only (`data-shopping-only`) `<input type="month">` next to the price field, plus a small violet "target month" badge on the item's row in the normal list view once set. Fully i18n'd (horizon labels, month names via `Intl.DateTimeFormat` in the active UI language, totals) and integrated with the offline-queue/house-switch/language-switch refresh hooks already in `app.js`. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `/js/planning.js` (cache bumped to `v9`). Verified end-to-end in two real headless-Chrome sessions: create-with-month via the item form, badge rendering, tab switching, 3/6/12 horizon filtering, correct per-month/total sums, rescheduling and unscheduling via the `<select>`, and the edit-modal round-trip (open → prefilled month → change → save → re-open shows the new value) — no console errors in any of it. **Not yet done**: no Go unit test for `internal/validate.Month` (the existing `internal/validate` package has none for `URL` either, so this matches current — imperfect — convention rather than a regression) and no dedicated `internal/db`/`internal/handlers` test for `target_month`, only the live browser/curl verification above.
- **Recurring items**: an item can carry `recurrence_rule`/`due_date`/`recurrence_end_date` (`items.is_recurring` is derived, never a separate input) — see the "Recurring items" bullet in the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) for the full design (`internal/handlers.applyRecurrenceCompletion`/`nextDueDate`, the "shift in place rather than clone a row" choice, the offline JS mirror in `sw.js`). The frontend's recurrence `<select>` ("Ne pas répéter"/"Tous les jours"/"Toutes les semaines"/"Tous les mois"/"Tous les ans") is in both the create-item form and the edit-item modal in `static/index.html`, applies to both list types, and a recurring item's row shows a 🔄 badge with the translated frequency (fully i18n'd, new `items.recurrence*`/`modals.editItem.recurrenceLabel` keys in `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`). Verified: Go unit tests for `internal/validate.Date`/`Recurrence` (`internal/validate/date_test.go`/`recurrence_test.go`), the completion/advance logic including the recurrence-end-date cutoff and month/year rollover (`internal/handlers/recurrence_test.go`), a DB-layer persistence test (`internal/db/items_test.go`'s `TestRecurringItemPersistence`) — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt`/`go test ./...` all clean — a live curl walkthrough of the full API (create with `WEEKLY`, complete twice watching `due_date` advance by 7 days each time, set a `recurrence_end_date` in the past and confirm the item then stays `done`, clear `recurrence_rule` and confirm `due_date`/`recurrence_end_date` clear with it), and three real headless-Chromium (Playwright) sessions covering the create form, the edit-modal round-trip, and — with `BrowserContext.setOffline(true)` — the offline check-off-and-advance path in `sw.js`. `EVERY_X_DAYS:<n>` is accepted by the API and both the Go and JS advance logic, but there's no UI control to set it (the dropdown only offers the four fixed cadences), so it's currently reachable only via a direct API call. **Not yet done**: no due-date display anywhere in the UI (it's tracked purely as backend bookkeeping for now, per the feature request's own scope) — a future session could surface it if that's wanted. *Superseded 2026-09-30 by "Recurring tasks, due times & task notifications" below: a checked-off recurring item now stays done with a `next_due_date` instead of advancing in place, the UI has custom intervals and shows the next due date, and `applyRecurrenceCompletion`/`nextDueDate` became `applyRecurrenceLifecycle`/`nextOccurrence`/`stepDueDate`.*
- **Urgent items**: an item can carry a plain `is_urgent` boolean (`INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, `addColumnIfMissing`-guarded the same way as `price`/`target_month`) flagging it as needing attention right away (e.g. "out of toilet paper") — unlike `is_recurring` it's not derived from anything else, and unlike `price`/`target_month` it applies to both list types. `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH /api/v1/items` all accept it (see [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#urgent-items)); `PUT` resets it to `false` if omitted (full replace), `PATCH` leaves it untouched if omitted. The frontend adds a checkbox ("Marquer comme urgent", with an "Ex : Papier toilette épuisé" hint) to both the create-item form and the edit-item modal in `static/index.html`; an item flagged urgent shows a 🚨 "Urgent" badge on its row (`buildUrgentBadge` in `static/js/list_view.js`) and, while still unfinished, a distinctive rose border, and `renderItems`' active-item list is stably sorted so unfinished urgent items float to the top without disturbing the relative order of everything else. A third dashboard tab, "Urgent" (`static/js/urgent.js`, `#tab-urgent`/`#urgent-view`), lists every unfinished urgent item across every list (shopping and todo alike) in the current house, fanning out over `?house_id=` the same way `planning.js` does, with a one-click checkbox to mark an item done straight from the widget (a direct optimistic `PATCH`, not `list_view.js`'s undo-toast `toggleDone`, since the item isn't part of `state.currentList`) and a click-through back into that item's list (`selectList`). The three dashboard tabs' mutual-exclusion switching (`setActiveTab`) was generalized from two-way to three-way directly in `planning.js`, since it already owned that logic; `app.js`'s `refreshVisibleView` (used by the offline-sync-complete/language-switch/house-switch hooks) now also checks `isUrgentTabActive()`/`refreshUrgentIfActive()` the same way it already did for planning. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `/js/urgent.js` (cache bumped to `v12`); no offline-mirror logic needed touching since `is_urgent` is a plain field that already passes through `sw.js`'s generic `{...existing, ...body}` optimistic-edit spread, unlike recurrence's hand-kept JS port. Verified: a DB-layer persistence test (`internal/db/items_test.go`'s `TestUrgentItemPersistence`), `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt`/`go test ./...` all clean, and a live curl walkthrough of the full API (create with `is_urgent: true`, `PATCH` to `false` and back, `PUT` full-replace behavior). **Not yet done**: no browser/Playwright verification of the frontend (checkbox, badge, sort-to-top, the urgent tab's fan-out/mark-done/click-through) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in; try it in an actual browser before relying on it.
- **Price alerts / deal notifications**: a new `price_alerts` table (`id`, `item_id`, `original_price`, `found_price`, `source_url`, `status` ∈ `pending`/`accepted`/`rejected`, `created_at` — a brand-new table added directly in `schema.sql`, not `addColumnIfMissing`-guarded) records a lower price found for an item's `url` than its current `price`. Rows are created by `internal/handlers.checkItemForBetterPrice` (`internal/handlers/price_alerts.go`), which reuses the existing `internal/scraper.FetchProductInfo` (no changes needed in `internal/scraper` itself — the "comparator" is just a `<` on the two prices) and guards against duplicate alerts via `internal/db.CreatePriceAlertIfNonePending`'s `WHERE NOT EXISTS (... status = 'pending')`. Two triggers call it: a periodic background scan (`RunPriceAlertScan`, iterating `internal/db.ListItemsForPriceScan`'s not-done/has-url/has-price items, run on a `time.Ticker` wired up in `cmd/server/main.go`'s `runPriceAlertScanLoop`, interval configurable via the new `PRICE_CHECK_INTERVAL_HOURS` env var — default 24h, `<= 0` disables it) and an on-demand endpoint (`POST /api/v1/items/{id}/price-check`, bounded by the same `priceCheckTimeout` = 10s the periodic scan uses per item). `GET /api/v1/price-alerts?house_id=&status=` lists alerts (RBAC-scoped via `authorizeHouseAccess`); `PATCH /api/v1/price-alerts/{id}` with `{"status": "accepted"}` atomically applies `found_price` to the item (`price_auto: true`, inside a `sql.Tx` in `internal/db.AcceptPriceAlert` — same "must not leave inconsistent state" reasoning as `CreateHouseWithOwner`) or `{"status": "rejected"}` just dismisses it (`RejectPriceAlert`); both guard on `WHERE status = 'pending'` so an alert can only ever be actioned once, returning `409` on a repeat attempt. Full design in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#price-alerts) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#price_alerts). The frontend adds a 🔔 button to the header (next to the language switcher) with a badge showing the current house's pending-alert count, opening a bottom-sheet drawer (`static/js/notifications.js`, `#notifications-modal`) listing each alert as an old-price/new-price comparison with "Appliquer le nouveau prix"/"Ignorer" buttons (optimistic, with rollback on failure, the same pattern `urgent.js`'s mark-done button uses) — unlike the planning/urgent dashboard tabs this isn't a tab, so it refreshes from its own hook points wired into `app.js` (house switch, `trakka-sync-complete`, a language switch, the page regaining visibility, and once on initial load) rather than through `refreshVisibleView`'s tab-switch logic. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `/js/notifications.js` (cache bumped to `v13`). Verified: `internal/db/price_alerts_test.go` (the duplicate-pending guard, accept applying the price + flipping status atomically, reject leaving the item untouched, the accept/reject-twice `ErrNotFound` guard, and the periodic scan's eligibility filter), `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt`/`go test ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: no live curl walkthrough or browser verification of the new endpoints/UI (no e-commerce site with an actual price drop was available to trigger the scraper end-to-end in the sandbox this was built in), and the frontend (bell badge, drawer, accept/reject buttons) has not been exercised in a browser — check both before relying on it, the same caveat every scraper-touching feature in this log has carried.
- **Light/dark theme**: the whole PWA (and the server-rendered `templates/login.html`) supports Light, Dark, and Auto (follows the OS `prefers-color-scheme`), chosen from a 🌙/☀️ header dropdown (`#theme-button`/`#theme-menu`, same open/close/Escape/click-outside pattern as the existing FR/EN `#lang-button`/`#lang-menu`) and persisted in `localStorage['trakka:theme']`. The mechanism is a single `data-theme="light"|"dark"` attribute on `<html>`: Tailwind's Play CDN is configured with `darkMode: ['selector', '[data-theme="dark"]']` (`static/js/tailwind-config.js`), so every hardcoded dark-only Tailwind utility class across `static/index.html`, `templates/login.html`, and the DOM-building code in `static/js/*.js` was mechanically paired with a light-mode default plus a `dark:` variant carrying the original (already-verified) dark value — e.g. `bg-slate-900` became `bg-white dark:bg-slate-900` — so dark mode's appearance is pixel-identical to before this feature; only light mode is new. `static/css/tokens.css`'s `--tk-*` custom properties back the handful of things Tailwind utilities don't reach (`color-scheme`, used so native form-control chrome — checkboxes, `<select>`, scrollbars, the `<input type="month">` picker — re-themes itself instead of staying stuck light); `static/css/base.css` adds a scoped background/border/color `transition` for a smooth manual crossfade and a `scrollbar-color`/`::selection` rule. The header lockup logo swaps between the pre-existing `trakka-lockup-light-bg.svg`/`trakka-lockup-dark-bg.svg` assets via two stacked `<img>` tags toggled with `dark:hidden`/`hidden dark:block` (pure CSS, no flash) — this asset pair already existed but only the dark variant was ever wired up before. **Anti-FOUC + a CSP gotcha worth remembering**: the theme has to be resolved and set as the `data-theme` attribute *before* any stylesheet is parsed, which normally argues for an inline `<script>` at the very top of `<head>` — but this app's CSP (`internal/handlers.SecurityHeaders`) sets `script-src 'self' https://cdn.tailwindcss.com` with no `'unsafe-inline'`, so an inline script here is silently blocked by the browser rather than executed (this was actually hit and debugged during this session — the first implementation attempt used inline `<script>` tags for both the anti-FOUC bootstrap and the `tailwind.config` assignment, and dark-mode toggling silently did nothing because Tailwind fell back to its default `media`-query strategy). The fix, and the pattern to follow for anything like this in the future: `static/js/theme-init.js` (the anti-FOUC resolver) and `static/js/tailwind-config.js` (the `darkMode` config) are both tiny external files loaded via ordinary `<script src="...">` tags — same origin, so `'self'` covers them, and a blocking `<script src>` still runs before subsequent `<link rel="stylesheet">` tags are applied, preserving the FOUC guarantee. `static/js/theme.js` (loaded later, at the bottom of `<body>`) owns the interactive parts: the header menu, persistence, updating `<meta name="theme-color" id="theme-color-meta">`, and a `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` change listener that keeps the page live-following the OS for as long as "Auto" stays selected (no reload needed). `static/sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `theme-init.js`/`theme.js`/`tailwind-config.js`/the light-bg logo (cache bumped to `v15`). Verified end-to-end in real headless-Chromium (Playwright) sessions covering: the login/register page respecting OS light and OS dark with zero JS (confirms the `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` fallback in `tokens.css` independently of `theme.js`); the full dashboard, item list (including the urgent-item rose border/badge), edit-item modal, planning tab, urgent tab, admin-settings/members/new-house modals, and the error banner all in explicit Light; explicit Dark; reload-persistence of an explicit choice; and Auto live-following a simulated OS preference change without a reload — zero console errors in any of it. **Not yet done**: `templates/login.html` has no header/toggle of its own (it's unauthenticated and has no header at all) — it only benefits from an explicit choice because it shares `localStorage` with the app on the same origin; a user whose very first visit is the login page still only gets OS-based Auto, which is fine since that's this page's existing precedent (see the pre-existing "login.html is still French-only" gap below).
- **Houses**: a grouping/sharing concept — every list belongs to exactly one house (`lists.house_id`, added via the `addColumnIfMissing` guard since `lists` already had a shipped shape), and every house has one or more members via `house_members`. `ensureDefaultHouse()`'s pre-auth "Maison Principale" seed (`internal/db/db.go`) still runs on a fresh database but is now a harmless, permanently orphaned row (no members, so RBAC hides it from everyone) — see docs/DATABASE.md's Houses section for why it wasn't worth special-casing away.
- **SQLite database** (`modernc.org/sqlite`, pure Go) with idempotent schema, `ON DELETE CASCADE` chains (house → list → item; house → house_members; user → sessions/house_members), single-connection pool — see [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md).
- **Complete, working PWA frontend**: `static/index.html` (+ logout button, members-management modal), `static/js/app.js` (dashboard, house selector, list management, auth-gate via `apiRequest()`'s 401 handling, house-member invite/remove UI), `static/js/list_view.js` ("Google Tasks"-style list detail view), `static/js/db.js` (shared IndexedDB layer, `DB_VERSION` 2 — unchanged by this feature, since the HttpOnly session cookie has nothing to mirror), `static/sw.js` (service worker: app-shell caching, offline write queue, temp-id reconciliation, a `401`-leaves-entry-queued fix so an expired session can't silently drop an offline edit, `/auth/...` bypass; houses are read-mirrored but require connectivity to mutate — see docs/PWA.md), `static/manifest.json`, cross-platform icons. Tailwind via Play CDN, minimal custom CSS. Verified end-to-end with the same headless-Chromium (Playwright) approach used for the houses feature.
- **Offline/PWA support** fully designed and documented in [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md), including the historical `remapTempId`/cascade-delete ordering bug (houses feature) and the newer session-expiry-vs-queue fix (this feature), both found by scripted testing.
- **Deployment**: multi-stage `Dockerfile` (`golang:1.27.0-alpine` builder → `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12` runtime, fixed non-root UID/GID `10001:10001`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `COPY`s `templates/`), `compose.yml` (`trakka` service + optional `radicale` service behind the `calendar` profile), in-process healthcheck. New env vars (`BASE_URL`, `OIDC_*`, `SESSION_*`, `TEMPLATES_DIR`) documented in [docs/DEPLOYMENT.md](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md). The Docker image build itself was **not** verified in the session that added auth, nor in the later session that switched the runtime base to distroless and added `read_only`/`cap_drop` hardening to `compose.yml` — no Docker daemon was available in either sandbox. Both changes are low-risk (the distroless switch mirrors the well-known "prepare passwd/group/data-dir in the builder stage, `COPY --from=build` them into a shell-less runtime" pattern; nothing in the app writes anywhere but `/data`), but build and run it at least once before deploying — especially the `read_only: true` root filesystem, which is the part most likely to surface a startup issue than the base-image swap itself.
- **Documentation**: `README.md` + `docs/API.md`, `docs/DATABASE.md`, `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, `docs/PWA.md`, `docs/AUDIT.md`, all current and cross-linked with this file. The `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` gap that used to be tracked here (missing `list_view.js` in the project layout) is fixed.
- **Custom categories ("Spaces")** — backend only: a new `custom_categories` table (`id`, `user_id`, `name`, `icon`, `color`, `position`, `created_at`) plus `lists.custom_category_id` (nullable, `addColumnIfMissing`-guarded, `REFERENCES custom_categories(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`) — see the dedicated Architecture bullet above for the full design (per-user ownership scoping, the `ON DELETE SET NULL` reasoning, and why the `addColumnIfMissing` call had to be sequenced after `migrateListsTypeCheck()` in `internal/db/db.go`). `models.CustomCategory` (`internal/models/models.go`) and CRUD in `internal/db/custom_categories.go`; `internal/handlers/categories.go` exposes `GET`/`POST /api/v1/custom-categories` and `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/custom-categories/{id}` (no partial `PATCH` — matches houses' own CRUD shape, which also has no `PATCH`); `internal/handlers/lists.go`'s `handleListsCreate`/`handleListsUpdate` accept an optional `custom_category_id` (validated by the new `validateCustomCategoryOwnership` against the *caller's* own categories) and `internal/db.GetList`/`ListListsForUser` embed the attached category via a `LEFT JOIN` (`custom_category` in the JSON response). A new `internal/validate.Color` validates an optional `#RGB`/`#RRGGBB` hex string, following the same "empty is valid, non-empty must match" shape as `Date`/`Month`/`Recurrence`. Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#custom-categories) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#custom_categories). Verified: `internal/db/custom_categories_test.go` (CRUD, cross-user ownership scoping returning `ErrNotFound` rather than leaking existence, list association/embedding via both `GetList` and `ListListsForUser`, dissociation, and `ON DELETE SET NULL` when a category is deleted out from under an attached list), `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt`/`go test ./...` all clean.
- **Custom categories frontend**: a fourth dashboard tab, "Espaces" (`static/js/spaces.js`, `#tab-spaces`/`#spaces-view`), showing a small violet dot on the tab button (`#tab-spaces-badge`) whenever the user has at least one category, updated by `loadCustomCategories()` at startup and after every category create/edit/delete. The tab renders each category as a collapsible `<details>` section (the same chevron/`group`/`group-open:rotate-90` idiom as `#done-section`) with an edit (pencil) and delete (trash) icon-button in its `<summary>`, holding a grid of every list attached to it (`buildListCard`, reused directly from `app.js`, with a per-type badge function — `urlBadges`/`progressBadge`/`noBadges` — chosen per list since a single space can mix `type`s) fetched via the same `?house_id=` + per-list `GET /lists/{id}` fan-out `planning.js`/`urgent.js` already use, filtered to lists carrying a `custom_category_id`. Zero categories shows an empty state with a "+ Créer une catégorie personnalisée" button. The three-way tab switcher in `planning.js` (`setActiveTab`/`activeTab`) was generalized to four; `app.js`'s `refreshVisibleView` gained an `isSpacesTabActive()`/`refreshSpacesIfActive()` branch the same way it already had one each for planning/urgent. A shared create/edit modal (`#category-modal`, `#category-form`) collects a name, a freeform emoji `icon` (a text input plus eight one-click emoji preset buttons), and a `color` (a native `<input type="color">`, which already only ever emits a `#rrggbb` string — satisfying `internal/validate.Color` for free — plus six preset swatch buttons); category deletion goes through the same 5s undo-toast pattern as `removeList` in `app.js` (`TrakkaUndo.schedule`), relying on the backend's `ON DELETE SET NULL` so a deleted category's lists are never themselves touched. The "new list" modal (`static/index.html`'s `#create-list-form`, submit handler in `app.js`) gained a `#list-category` `<select>` (populated by `spaces.js`'s `populateCategorySelect`, called every time the modal opens so it can't go stale) sending `custom_category_id` on `POST /api/v1/lists`; picking its "+ Créer un nouvel espace" sentinel option (`CREATE_CATEGORY_OPTION_VALUE`, mirroring `CREATE_HOUSE_OPTION_VALUE`'s pattern for the house selector) opens the category modal *on top of* the still-open list modal, and its `onCreated` callback selects the freshly created category back in the list modal's picker once saved — the only two places in the app where one modal can currently open on top of another, which needed two small guards: `closeCategoryModal` only releases the shared `overflow-hidden` body class if the list modal isn't still open, and the list modal's own Escape handler skips closing while the category modal is the one on top of it. A dedicated "edit list" modal (reassigning name/type/category, and now icon) was added in a later session — see the "List editing" bullet below; the previous sentence in this bullet claiming no such modal exists is now out of date and kept only for history. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `/js/spaces.js` (cache bumped to `v20`); new `spaces.*`/`modals.newCategory.*`/`modals.newList.category*`/`undo.categoryDeleted` keys added to both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`. **Not yet done**: no live browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in (same caveat as the urgent-items and price-alerts frontends above) — only `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt` (unaffected, since this is a frontend-only change) were checked. Before relying on it: exercise the Espaces tab's empty state, create/edit/delete a category (including the undo toast), the nested "create space from the list modal" flow end to end, and the mixed-type list grouping in a real browser.
- **List editing**: a list's `name`, `type`, `custom_category_id`, and a new freeform `icon` (typically an emoji, e.g. `🛒`/`🖥️`/`📦`) can all be changed after creation, not just at creation time. `lists.icon` (`TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, added the same way as `custom_category_id` — at the time, `addColumnIfMissing`-guarded and sequenced after `migrateListsTypeCheck()` for the "the rebuild would silently drop it" reason; both are now migrations 8 and 6 respectively in the versioned engine described in .claude/architecture.md, where sequencing is enforced by migration order instead) backs `models.List.Icon`; `internal/handlers.handleListsCreate`/`handleListsUpdate` both accept it (trimmed, no further validation, same convention as `custom_categories.icon`), and `PUT /api/v1/lists/{id}` — already a full replace — now also resets `icon` to `""` if omitted, matching how `type`/`custom_category_id` already behave there. The frontend turns the existing "new list" modal (`#new-list-modal`) into a shared create/edit modal exactly the way `spaces.js`'s category modal already does (`editingList` state var in `app.js`'s `openListModal(list)`, `null` meaning create mode; swaps the modal's title/submit button text via new `modals.newList.title{Create,Edit}`/`submit{Create,Edit}` i18n keys), adding an icon text input plus five one-click emoji presets. A ✏️ edit button now sits next to every list card's 🗑️ delete button on the dashboard grids *and* the Espaces tab (both render through the same shared `buildListCard` in `app.js`), and next to the list-detail header (`#edit-list-button` in `list_view.js`, opening the modal prefilled from `state.currentList`); saving an edit calls `refreshCurrentList()` afterward so the open detail view picks up the change immediately (a no-op if a different list, or no list, is open). A list's own `icon` is shown before its name — falling back to a fixed per-`type` default (`listIcon()` in `app.js`, reusing `LIST_TYPE_BADGE_META`'s icons) when unset — on every list card (dashboard grids and Espaces cards alike, since both share `buildListCard`) and in the list-detail header. `icon` is a plain field already covered by `sw.js`'s generic `{...existing, ...body}` optimistic-edit spread for `PUT /api/v1/lists/{id}` (lists are already offline-queueable via `temp-list-*` ids, unlike houses), so no offline-mirror logic needed touching. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` cache bumped to `v21` (content-only change, no new cached paths). Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt`/`go test ./...` clean, plus a live curl walkthrough (register, create a list with `icon`, `GET` reflects it, `PUT` changes name/type/icon, a `PUT` omitting `icon` clears it back to `""`). **Not yet done**: no live browser verification of the frontend (the edit button on cards/header, the modal's prefill and title/submit swap, the icon presets, the icon showing up on cards and the detail header) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as every other frontend-only feature in this log.
- **Offline UX pass on the Budget/Urgent/Espaces tabs**: previously, `planning.js`'s `loadPlanningView`/`urgent.js`'s `loadUrgentView`/`spaces.js`'s `loadSpacesView`/`loadCustomCategories` had no IndexedDB fallback at all — a failed `apiRequest()` (offline, or a transient server error) just emptied `planningEntries`/`urgentEntries`/`spacesLists`/`customCategories` and popped the blocking error banner, so those three tabs went completely blank the moment the network dropped, unlike the dashboard (`loadDashboard`) which already fell back to its `cachedDashboardLists()` mirror. Fixed by giving all three the same fallback: on failure, they now re-derive their entries from `cachedDashboardLists(state.currentHouseId)` (the existing helper in `app.js`, reused as-is rather than duplicated) via new shared extraction helpers (`applyPlanningEntries` in `planning.js`, `extractUrgentEntries` in `urgent.js`) so the live-network and cache-fallback paths can't drift apart. Custom categories themselves had no IndexedDB mirror at all (only the lists/items/houses stores existed) — added a `custom_categories` object store to `static/js/db.js` (`DB_VERSION` bumped `2` → `3`, `getCustomCategories`/`putCustomCategories`/`putCustomCategory`/`deleteCustomCategory`) and wired `static/sw.js` to mirror `GET/POST/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/custom-categories` into it the same way houses/lists/items already are, including an `offlineReadFallback()` branch that now serves a real cached `200` for `GET /api/v1/custom-categories` while offline instead of falling through to a generic `503` (`sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v22` accordingly). Separately, the blocking error banner (`showError()`) was firing on every plain connectivity failure during ordinary read-driven navigation (opening a tab, switching houses, a background refresh) even when a cache fallback had *already* repainted the view with something to show — redundant with the header's discreet "Hors-ligne" badge and, per explicit user feedback, intrusive. Fixed at the source: `apiRequest()` (`app.js`) now tags the `Error` it throws when `fetch()` itself fails (no route to the server) with `err.isNetworkError = true`, distinct from the untagged `Error` thrown for a non-2xx HTTP response; a new `isNetworkError(err)` helper is checked before every `showError()` call in every GET-driven loader (`loadHouses`/`loadDashboard`/`loadMembers` in `app.js`; `selectList`/`refreshCurrentList` in `list_view.js`; the three tabs' loaders above) so a plain offline navigation stays silent while a genuine server-side error (a `5xx`, a bug) still raises the banner. List/item metadata needed for card rendering while offline (`icon`, `type`, `custom_category_id`, badge-driving fields) required no changes — `sw.js`'s existing `withoutItems()` spread already preserves every list field verbatim into the mirror, and `buildListCard`/`listIcon`/`typeBadge` in `app.js` already read from whatever list object they're given (live or cached) identically. Full design rationale documented in [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md) (new "Custom categories are read-mirrored but not offline-writable" section, mirroring the pre-existing houses one, plus a new paragraph under "How offline reads and writes work" explaining the `isNetworkError` convention). **Not yet done**: no live browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in (same caveat as most other frontend work in this log). Before relying on it: go offline mid-session (DevTools' Network throttling → Offline, or airplane mode) with each of the three tabs already visited once online, confirm they keep showing their last-synced data with no error banner, and confirm the banner still appears for an actual server error (e.g. temporarily stop the backend and hit a `5xx`/connection-refused case if that's distinguishable from a network error in your test setup). Also note: creating/editing/deleting a *custom category itself* while offline is still not a supported flow (only reading the last-synced set is) — see the new PWA.md section for why.
- **Offline reload follow-up: skip the doomed fetch instead of just catching it**: the offline UX pass above already made every GET-driven loader fall back to IndexedDB on failure, but it still *attempted* `fetch()` first every time, even when the browser already knew there was no connectivity — without a controlling service worker (or on the very first request after a fresh `F5` before one takes over), a doomed `fetch()` can take several seconds to time out rather than reject instantly, so a reload while offline could sit stalled before its cache fallback ever kicked in. Fixed centrally in `apiRequest()` (`app.js`): when called for a `GET` and `navigator.onLine` is already `false`, it now throws the same `isNetworkError`-tagged `Error` immediately, without ever calling `fetch()` — this automatically speeds up *every* GET-driven caller (`loadHouses`/`loadDashboard`/`loadMembers` in `app.js`; `selectList`/`refreshCurrentList` in `list_view.js`; `loadPlanningView`/`loadUrgentView`/`loadSpacesView`/`loadCustomCategories` in `planning.js`/`urgent.js`/`spaces.js`) with no per-file changes needed, since they already funnel through this one function. Deliberately scoped to `GET` only: a write (`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`) still always calls `fetch()` even while offline, because that's what lets the service worker's `handleApiWrite` intercept it and queue it in IndexedDB for later replay (`sw.js`'s `queueOfflineWrite`) — short-circuiting before `fetch()` for a write would silently drop it instead of queuing it. `navigator.onLine` reporting `true` on a connection that can't actually reach the server (a captive portal, a downed backend) is unaffected and still runs the normal `fetch()`-then-catch path exactly as before. Separately, `hydrateFromCache()` (`app.js`, the cache-only paint `init()` runs before any network call) previously only warmed houses/lists/items — it now also reads the `custom_categories` IndexedDB store directly and calls `updateSpacesTabBadge()` (both defined in `spaces.js`), so the "Espaces" tab's highlight dot and the "new list" modal's category picker are correct immediately on boot rather than only after `loadCustomCategories()`'s own network-first call fails over further down in `init()`. Also hardened `db.js`'s `open()`: it memoizes its connection promise (`dbPromise`) so every store helper shares one `indexedDB.open()` call, but previously never cleared that memo on `onerror` — meaning one transient failure (a browser hiccup, a version-upgrade conflict with another open tab) would have permanently wedged the entire IndexedDB mirror, and every offline fallback built on it, for the rest of the page's lifetime; it now resets `dbPromise = null` before rejecting so the next call retries with a fresh request. **Not yet done**: same caveat as the pass above — no browser automation tooling was available to actually click through an offline `F5` reload landing on each tab; verify with DevTools' Network → Offline before relying on it.
- Verified on 2026-08-28: `go build`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .`, and `go test ./...` are clean on the current state of the repo (this session's changes were frontend-only — `static/js/{app,db,list_view,planning,urgent,spaces}.js`, `static/sw.js` — so none of these commands touch the changed files, but re-running them after a frontend-only change is still cheap confirmation nothing else regressed).
- **Granular sharing (Spaces & Lists)**: extends the existing House-only sharing model with two new tables, `space_shares`/`list_shares`, so a Space or an individual List can be shared directly with one other user (by email, `read` or `write`) without adding them to the whole parent House — see the dedicated "Granular sharing" bullet in the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) for the full design (`internal/db.AccessLevelForList`'s three-source combined verdict, `internal/handlers.authorizeListAccess`/`authorizeItemAccess`'s new `requireWrite` parameter, why List-sharing management stays House-membership-only while Space-sharing stays owner-only, `ListSharedListsForUser` backing the new `?shared_with_me=true` query and the "Partagé avec moi" tab). New backend files: `internal/db/shares.go`, `internal/handlers/shares.go`; new frontend file: `static/js/shares.js` (the share modal + the fifth dashboard tab, `#tab-shared`/`#shared-view`); a 👥 share button was added to every list card (`buildListCard` in `app.js`), every space section (`spaces.js`), and the list-detail header (`#share-list-button` in `list_view.js`). `sw.js` blocks POST/DELETE on `.../share` while offline (same "needs an immediate round-trip, queuing it would surface a confusing later failure" reasoning as houses/admin-settings) and excludes `?shared_with_me=true` results from the general lists IndexedDB mirror (`SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v23`). Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#sharing) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#space_shares-and-list_shares). Verified: `internal/db/shares_test.go` (share CRUD/upsert for both tables, `AccessLevelForList` exercising all three sources including the "higher of two shares wins" case, `ListSharedListsForUser`'s house-membership exclusion) — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt`/`go test ./...` all clean — plus a full live curl walkthrough of the backend end-to-end: sharing a list (read) and a space (write) with a second registered user, confirming `?shared_with_me=true` lists both with the right `access_source`/`access_permission`, confirming read-only enforcement (renaming/adding an item to the read-shared list both correctly `403`), confirming write-via-space access (adding an item to the space-shared list succeeds), the roster endpoint being House-membership-gated (`403` for the non-member recipient), self-share/unknown-email/non-owner-sharing-a-category-they-don't-own all rejected with the right status codes (`400`/`404`/`404`, the last never leaking the category's existence), revoke working and being non-idempotent (`404` on a repeat call), and a shared list correctly dropping out of `?shared_with_me=true` once the recipient becomes an actual House member (already visible the ordinary way). **Not yet done**: no browser verification of the frontend (the 👥 buttons, the share modal's roster/grant/revoke flow, the "Partagé avec moi" tab's card rendering and empty state) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as every other frontend-only feature in this log.
- **Versioned SQLite migration engine, pre-migration backups, service worker update banner, IndexedDB upgrade hardening**: replaces the single idempotent `internal/db/schema.sql` + `addColumnIfMissing()`/`migrateListsTypeCheck()` mechanism with a real versioned migration engine — see the dedicated Architecture bullet above and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#evolving-the-schema) for the full design. `internal/db/migrations/0001_init.sql` through `0009_granular_sharing.sql` (migration 6, widening `lists.type`'s `CHECK` constraint, is Go code in `internal/db/migrate_list_types.go` instead, since it needs `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` set outside any transaction) reconstruct the schema's *actual* history one step at a time, reverse-engineered from `git log` on `internal/db/schema.sql`/`db.go` rather than guessed, so a fresh install replays the same sequence of changes a long-lived deployment actually went through. `internal/db/migrate.go`'s `hasExistingSchema` adopts a pre-existing database (tables present, `PRAGMA user_version` still `0`) directly at the latest version without re-running any migration SQL, which the pre-migration-engine code had already applied unconditionally on every prior startup — the load-bearing invariant this relies on is that `Open()` always refused to start on any failure, so no database that ever started successfully can be missing part of the schema. `backupBeforeMigration` snapshots an already-versioned, non-empty database via `VACUUM INTO` (confirmed to accept a bound parameter through `modernc.org/sqlite`, kept as an ordinary parameterized statement) to `<DB_PATH's directory>/backups/` before applying any pending migration — `/data/backups/`, for the default `DB_PATH`, already writable under `compose.yml`'s `read_only: true` root filesystem since it's inside the `/data` named volume; nothing prunes old backups automatically yet. `internal/db.Open` now takes a `*slog.Logger` (only used to record which migrations ran — the common "already up to date" case logs nothing), so every call site (`cmd/server/main.go`, and the `openTestDB` helpers in `internal/db`'s and `internal/settings`'s test files) was updated to pass one. On the frontend: `static/js/app.js`'s `registerServiceWorker()` now calls `watchForServiceWorkerUpdate(registration)`, which listens for a new service worker reaching `'installed'` while `navigator.serviceWorker.controller` is already set (the standard "this is a genuine update, not the first-ever install" signal) and shows a new discreet `#update-banner` ("Une nouvelle version est disponible." / "Mettre à jour", new `update.*` i18n keys) rather than reloading automatically — reload only happens if the user clicks the button, so nothing forces a loss of in-progress form input. This works specifically because `sw.js`'s existing `self.skipWaiting()` (install) and `self.clients.claim()` (activate) calls — already present before this session, not new — mean the new worker actually takes over almost immediately once installed, so a click on the banner's button really does pick up the new assets rather than reloading into the same old ones; `sw.js` gained a couple of comments tying this together but no behavior changes. `static/js/db.js`'s `open()` gained `request.onblocked` (logs a warning) and, on every successful connection, `db.onversionchange` (proactively closes that connection and clears the memoized `dbPromise`) — the standard multi-tab IndexedDB upgrade pattern, so a `DB_VERSION` bump in a future deployment can't leave an older tab's still-open connection blocking a newer tab's `onupgradeneeded` from ever running; `onupgradeneeded` itself was already purely additive (`objectStoreNames.contains` guards before every `createObjectStore`, never dropping a store) from earlier work, unchanged here beyond a clarifying comment. Verified: three new Go tests in `internal/db/migrate_test.go` (`TestMigrateFreshDatabase` — a brand new database ends at the latest `user_version` with a schema whose `lists.type` CHECK is already widened; `TestMigrateAdoptsPreExistingDatabase` — a simulated pre-migration-engine database is adopted without data loss and without attempting to back it up; `TestBackupBeforeMigrationCreatesSnapshot` — the `VACUUM INTO` snapshot actually contains the pre-migration data; plus `TestHasExistingSchema`/`TestLoadMigrationsSequential`), `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of the update banner (no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most frontend work in this log) — check that a real deployment (bump `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` in `sw.js`, redeploy, reload an already-open tab) actually surfaces the banner and that clicking it reloads onto the new assets. The `hasExistingSchema`-adoption path was only exercised against a synthetic minimal seed (a `houses`/`lists` pair) in the test above, not against a real pre-existing `/data/trakka.db` carrying the full current schema and real data — do that once before relying on it in a real upgrade. No backup-pruning/retention policy exists yet for `/data/backups/`.
- **Hardened GitHub Actions CI/CD pipeline**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` replaces the previous ad-hoc workflow with a four-job pipeline (`lint` → `test` → `security-scan` → `build-scan-push`) built around two supply-chain rules — every action pinned to a full commit SHA (never a floating tag) and `permissions: read-all` at the workflow level with each job granting back only what it actually needs — see the "CI/CD & Sécurité" section in .claude/ci-security.md for the complete design (job responsibilities, the permissions table, the SHA-pinning rule and how to apply it to a new action, local-equivalent commands). Turning `golangci-lint` and `gosec` into real CI gates for the first time surfaced pre-existing findings that had to be triaged rather than ignored: a new `.golangci.yml` excludes `errcheck`'s check on the `Close`/`Rollback` deferred-cleanup pattern already used throughout `internal/db` (13 pre-existing hits, all the same harmless idiom — see .claude/ci-security.md for the exact exclusion list), `internal/db/migrate.go`'s pre-migration backup directory now uses `0o750` instead of `0o755` (gosec G301 — the directory holds full SQLite snapshots, so it shouldn't be world-readable, and this was a real, if minor, gap worth fixing outright), and five `#nosec` comments were added at genuine gosec false positives rather than being silenced globally: two `G101` ("potential hardcoded credentials") on a settings-table key name and a map of UI error-message strings, and three `G124` (missing/insecure cookie attributes) on `http.Cookie` literals whose `Secure` field is already correctly wired to `SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE` via a variable (`s.CookieSecure`/`app.Auth.CookieSecure`) rather than a literal `true`, which is all gosec's G124 check actually looks for. One of the four originally-flagged cookie sites — `internal/handlers/auth.go`'s OIDC callback clearing its single-use flow cookie — turned out to be a genuine gap rather than a false positive (it set no `HttpOnly`/`Secure`/`SameSite` at all) and now mirrors the attributes of the cookie it's clearing. `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...`/`golangci-lint run ./...`/`govulncheck ./...`/`gosec ./...` were all re-run locally after these fixes and are clean. **Not yet done**: the workflow has never actually run on GitHub's runners — see the dedicated bullet under "What's left" below.

- **Item quantity is now correctly multiplied into every price total**: `items.quantity` (and `PATCH /api/v1/items/{id}`'s existing `{"quantity": N}` support, validated positive server-side — already present, no backend change was needed) previously had no effect on any displayed total — `updateFinanceSummary` in `static/js/list_view.js` summed raw `item.price` across a list's items, and `planning.js`'s budget-forecast view did the same, both silently ignoring `quantity`, so a "×3" item only ever counted once. Fixed by introducing `lineTotal(item)` in `list_view.js` (`price * (quantity > 0 ? quantity : 1)`, since `item.price` is stored and entered as a *per-unit* price, never a line total) and routing every total through it: `updateFinanceSummary`'s Total estimé/Déjà dépensé/Reste à dépenser trio, `planning.js`'s per-month and grand budget totals (both the one-off `target_month` entries and the recurring-item occurrence projections, which now multiply `price * quantity * occurrenceCount`), and a new estimated-total badge on every purchase-type list card (`static/js/app.js`'s `urlBadges`, shown on the dashboard's "Achats & Sourcing" grid and, since it's shared, the "Espaces" tab too) that sums `price * quantity` across a list's priced items. An item's row in the list detail view now shows its line subtotal as the primary price figure, with a small secondary `(unit price/u)` label once `quantity > 1` so the per-unit price used to compute it stays visible. Quantity itself is now directly editable from the list detail view — a `[-]`/number-input/`[+]` stepper (`buildQuantityStepper`/`changeQuantity` in `list_view.js`, replacing the old read-only "title × N" title suffix for any list type that shows quantity at all) fires an optimistic `PATCH /api/v1/items/{id}` with `{"quantity": N}` on every change, immediately re-rendering the row's/list's totals from local state; a `pendingQuantityUpdates` map (mirroring the existing `pendingToggles` pattern `toggleDone` already uses) tracks an in-flight request's last-known-good value plus a `Symbol` request id per item, so a slower, now-stale response from an earlier click can never clobber a value the user has since moved past with further clicks, and a failed request rolls back to that last-known-good value with the existing error banner. No `sw.js`/IndexedDB changes were needed for either the read or write side: `quantity` is a plain field already covered by the service worker's generic `{...existing, ...body}` optimistic-edit spread (offline) and its generic `mirrorWriteResult` (online) — the same generic paths `is_urgent`/`icon` already ride on — so the IndexedDB mirror and every cache-fallback view already stay in sync automatically. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean (no Go files were touched — the bug and its fix were entirely in the frontend's price-summing logic) and `node --check` on all three edited JS files. **Not yet done**: no live browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend work in this log. Before relying on it: open a shopping list, set an item's price and bump its quantity via the new stepper (buttons and typing directly into the field), confirm the row's subtotal, the finance summary bar, the dashboard/Espaces card badge, and the Budget/Prévisions tab's totals all update consistently and immediately; also click the stepper rapidly (or throttle the network) to sanity-check the request-coalescing rollback behavior described above.

- **Mobile/PWA login loop fix**: local-password and OIDC login both appeared to succeed (`POST /auth/login` → `302`) but the very next call, `GET /api/v1/me`, immediately came back `401` and bounced the user back to `/auth/login` — a real report from mobile browsers, most consistently reproducible in iOS Safari's "Add to Home Screen" standalone/PWA mode. Root cause: the session cookie (`internal/auth.Service.SetSessionCookie`) was `SameSite=Strict`, and mobile WebKit has a well-documented quirk where a `Strict` cookie set on the response of a same-site redirect (`finishLogin`'s `302` to `/`) isn't reliably attached to the page's very next request — this is exactly the reasoning the pre-existing OIDC flow cookie (`oidcFlowCookieName` in `internal/handlers/auth.go`) already used to justify `SameSite=Lax` for itself, just not yet applied to the session cookie. Fixed by changing the session cookie to `SameSite=Lax` in both `SetSessionCookie` and `ClearSessionCookie` (`internal/auth/service.go`) — see the updated CSRF bullet in .claude/backend.md's Security rules section for why this doesn't weaken CSRF protection (every mutating request is `fetch`/XHR, never carried cross-site under Lax either; every `GET` route is a pure read). Also added an explicit `Max-Age` alongside the existing `Expires` on the session cookie (older WebKit has been inconsistent about `Expires`-only `Set-Cookie` headers), and made `credentials: 'same-origin'` explicit on both `apiRequest()`'s `fetch()` in `static/js/app.js` and the offline-queue replay `fetch()` in `static/sw.js`'s `flushQueue()` — `'same-origin'` has been the spec default since 2017, but embedded/WebKit mobile browsers have a history of being unreliable about implicit fetch defaults, so this is now explicit as defense in depth rather than relied upon implicitly. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean, `node --check` on both edited JS files. **Not yet done**: no verification on an actual mobile device or iOS Safari standalone/PWA session — no mobile browser or device was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before considering this closed: install the PWA to an iOS home screen (or at minimum test in mobile Safari/Chrome dev tools device emulation), log in, and confirm `/api/v1/me` succeeds on the first call after the post-login redirect, both for local-password login and (if a provider is available) OIDC.

- **List detail view redesigned Mobile-First (Todoist/Notion-style)**: `static/js/list_view.js`, `static/index.html`, and `static/css/base.css` reworked the item-list UI end to end, replacing the old always-expanded, all-fields-visible create-item form and single dense desktop-only row with a layout that's the same shape at every breakpoint rather than reflowing via CSS `order` on a narrow screen. Four pieces:
  - **Quick-add bar with a collapsible advanced panel**: `#create-item-form` is now just a name `<input>` plus a `[⚙️]` toggle and a `[+]` submit button by default; `[⚙️]` (`#quick-add-toggle`) expands `#quick-add-advanced` (URL/quantity/price/target month/recurrence/urgent — exactly the fields `data-item-field` already gated per list type) in place, collapsing again automatically after a successful add (`setQuickAddAdvancedExpanded`, called from the form's submit handler). The toggle itself hides outright for a `custom` list, which has none of those fields to reveal (`hasAdvancedFields`, shared between `applyListTypeVisibility` and the FAB flow below so the two can't drift apart). A `[+]` **FAB** (`#add-item-fab`, `md:hidden` — mobile only, and only ever visible at all while a list is open since it's a child of `#items-section`) opens `#create-item-form` as a bottom sheet (`#add-item-sheet`) instead of a second, duplicated form: `openAddItemSheet`/`closeAddItemSheet` simply relocate the one real `<form>` (and its already-attached listeners) between its inline slot (`#create-item-form-anchor`) and the sheet's body via plain `appendChild` on an already-attached node — moves it, doesn't clone it — with the advanced panel force-expanded while it's the dedicated add flow. An add made from the FAB sheet closes the sheet afterward (the "add one item" mobile pattern, matching this file's own "Google Tasks"-style framing); an add from the always-visible inline bar just collapses the panel back for the next entry.
  - **Item cards restructured**: `buildItemRow` now builds a top `.item-card__row` (checkbox/line-marker + optional thumbnail + bold title + inline 🔗 icon-link when the item has a safe URL, replacing the old standalone "lien ↗" pill badge — folding it into the title line is what keeps the row's one-glance hierarchy simple) with the price prominently trailing on the right (`buildPriceBlock`: the line subtotal, the auto-detected-price sparkle badge, and, once quantity > 1, the small `(unit price/u)` label — or a pending-price badge when there's a URL but no price yet), plus edit/delete (`.item-card__actions`) or a `[⋮]` kebab (`.item-card__kebab`) at the end — both are always built, and which one is actually visible is a pure `hidden md:flex`/`flex md:hidden` Tailwind concern (Tailwind's CDN default `md` breakpoint is 768px, matching every other breakpoint already hand-rolled in `base.css`). A second line, `.item-card__secondary` (only appended when it has anything in it), carries the already-existing `[-]`/N/`[+]` quantity stepper (no native `<input type=number>` spin arrows — it was already a custom styled control before this redesign) plus the month/recurrence/urgent badges, indented to align under the title.
  - **Actions simplified via a bottom sheet on mobile**: tapping an item's title, or its `[⋮]` kebab button, opens `#item-actions-sheet` (populated per-item by `openItemActionsSheet`) with ✏️ Modifier/🔗 Ouvrir le lien (hidden when the item has no safe URL)/🚨 Basculer en urgent (label flips to "Retirer l'urgence" when already urgent)/🗑️ Supprimer (in rose). "Basculer en urgent" is a new `toggleUrgent` optimistic PATCH, coalesced against concurrent toggles the same way `changeQuantity` already coalesces quantity clicks (a `pendingUrgentUpdates` map keyed by item, tracking the last-confirmed value and an in-flight request id so a slower stale response can never clobber a value the user has since moved past). The pencil/trash buttons stay visible and directly clickable on wider screens (`md:flex`) — the sheet exists specifically because two more 44px tap targets on an already-busy row crowd the price/title on a narrow phone.
  - **Collapsible budget summary**: `#finance-summary` is now a plain `<details>`/`<summary>` (the same collapsible idiom `#done-section` already used) rather than a permanently-expanded `<div>`, showing a compact always-visible total next to the chevron even while collapsed (`#finance-total-compact`, updated alongside `#finance-total` in `updateFinanceSummary`) so collapsing it to free up screen space on a small phone doesn't hide the one number most worth keeping at a glance. The open/closed choice persists across visits via `localStorage['trakka:financeSummaryCollapsed']`, the same per-browser-only persistence convention `HOUSE_STORAGE_KEY`/theme/language already use.

  New i18n keys (`items.financeSummaryLabel`/`advancedToggleAriaLabel`/`addFabAriaLabel`/`addSheetTitle`/`openLinkAriaLabel`/`moreActionsAriaLabel`, `modals.itemActions.*`) added to both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`; `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v24` (content-only change to already-cached `index.html`/`list_view.js`/`base.css`, no new cached paths). Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt` clean (no Go files touched), `node --check` on the edited JS, a Python `html.parser` pass confirming `index.html`'s tags still balance, and — unlike most other frontend-only work in this log — **a real headless-Chromium (Playwright) session** covering both an iPhone 13 emulated viewport and a 1280px desktop viewport: register → create a shopping list → quick-add a plain item → expand the advanced panel and add a priced/quantified item (confirmed the finance summary's total, `price × quantity` math unchanged) → collapse/expand the budget summary via tap → open the item-actions sheet via the kebab and via a title tap → toggle urgent from the sheet (badge appears, rose border applied) → delete via the sheet (undo toast still fires) → open the FAB's bottom sheet, add an item, confirm it auto-closes and the form moves back inline → add an item with a URL and confirm the inline 🔗 icon renders with the right `href` and that "Ouvrir le lien" is un-hidden in the actions sheet for it → confirm on the desktop viewport that the FAB and kebab are both hidden and the pencil/trash buttons are visible instead. Zero console errors/page errors/failed requests across the whole run. **Not yet done**: no real mobile device or physical touch/gesture testing (Playwright's viewport emulation only); todo-list and custom-list card rendering (no checkbox/no quantity/no price fields) and a recurring item's badge alongside the new secondary row weren't specifically exercised in this pass, only reasoned about from the unchanged `FIELD_VISIBILITY_BY_TYPE`/`buildRecurrenceBadge` logic; and the product-thumbnail case (`buildItemThumbnail`, which the redesign didn't otherwise touch) wasn't re-verified since it needs a real scraped image, the same pre-existing gap noted elsewhere in this log.

- **"Keep last page on launch" preference**: Trakka previously always landed on the dashboard's "Lists" tab on every reload/relaunch, regardless of what was last open. `users.keep_last_page` (`INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`, migration `internal/db/migrations/0010_keep_last_page_preference.sql`) is a per-user, per-account (not per-browser) on/off preference for this, defaulting to enabled; `models.User.KeepLastPage` carries it through `GET /api/v1/me` (both `internal/db.GetUser` and `getUserWithCredentials`'s queries were extended to select it, so it's present via every path that resolves the authenticated user — including `RequireSession`'s per-request `ValidateSession` → `GetUser` call), and a new `PATCH /api/v1/me` (`internal/handlers.handleMeUpdate`, `internal/db.UpdateUserKeepLastPage`) sets it, following the same `*bool`/"absent = untouched" partial-update convention as `PATCH /api/v1/items/{id}`.
