Deliberately **not** modeled as a `system_settings` row (that table is instance-wide, admin-only — see the "Custom categories" bullet's `user_id`-scoping reasoning above for why a genuinely per-user preference doesn't belong there) and deliberately **not** used to store the actual last-visited page itself — only the on/off choice lives server-side; which tab or list to reopen is tracked purely client-side, per the existing `HOUSE_STORAGE_KEY`/theme/language `localStorage`-only convention (see "Frontend (PWA, `static/`)" in .claude/frontend-pwa.md), since it's meaningless to sync across devices/browsers the way an actual account preference is.

  The frontend has two pieces. First, a new user-facing "Paramètres" panel (distinct from the admin-only "Paramètres du Système" one in `admin.js`, and visible to every signed-in user rather than gated) — a new header button (`#user-settings-button`, a cog SVG rather than an emoji so it doesn't visually collide with the admin panel's ⚙️) opens `#user-settings-modal` (`static/js/settings.js`, mirroring `admin.js`'s open/close/Escape/click-outside/status-message wiring almost verbatim), currently holding just one checkbox toggle that reads from and PATCHes `/api/v1/me`. Second, the actual save/restore machinery lives in `static/js/app.js`: `LAST_VIEW_STORAGE_KEY` (`'trakka:lastView'`, JSON — either `{ type: 'tab', tab: 'planning' }` or `{ type: 'list', id: 42 }`) is written by `saveLastView`, called from the three places that change what's visible — `list_view.js`'s `selectList`/`showDashboard` and `planning.js`'s `setActiveTab` — and read back by `restoreLastView`, called once at the very end of `init()` after `state.currentUser`/`state.currentHouseId`/the dashboard are all resolved. `isKeepLastPageEnabled()` prefers `state.currentUser.keep_last_page` (the authoritative server value) but falls back to a `KEEP_LAST_PAGE_STORAGE_KEY` (`'trakka:keepLastPage'`) `localStorage` mirror — kept in sync by `setKeepLastPagePreference`, called both right after `/me` resolves in `init()` and right after a successful `PATCH` in `settings.js` — so the very first paint of a reload can decide whether to even attempt a restore without blocking on a network round-trip that might not resolve yet (e.g. while offline), the same reasoning `apiRequest`'s existing `navigator.onLine` short-circuit already uses elsewhere. `selectList` gained an `opts.silent` flag (used only by `restoreLastView`) so reopening a list that's since been deleted or made inaccessible fails quietly back to the dashboard instead of greeting a relaunch with an error banner; every ordinary caller (clicking a list card) is unaffected. No `sw.js`/IndexedDB changes were needed — this is pure client-side state layered on already-existing pieces (the `/me` response, `localStorage`), not a new offline-sync concern; `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` were bumped to `v25` purely to pick up the new `/js/settings.js` file and the modal/button markup added to the already-cached `index.html`.

  Verified: a new `internal/db/users_test.go` test (`TestKeepLastPagePreference` — defaults to `true` for a new user, `UpdateUserKeepLastPage` round-trips through both `GetUser` and `GetUserByEmail`, and updating a nonexistent user returns `ErrNotFound`), `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean; a live curl walkthrough of `GET`/`PATCH /api/v1/me` (default `true`, toggling to `false` and back, an empty-body PATCH being a no-op, an unknown-field PATCH correctly `400`-rejected by the existing strict-JSON decoder, and unauthenticated `401`); and — unlike many entries in this log — **two real headless-Chromium (Playwright) sessions** exercising the actual restore behavior end-to-end: logging in, opening the new settings modal and confirming the toggle reflects the server default, navigating to the "Urgent" tab and confirming `localStorage['trakka:lastView']` updates, reloading and confirming the app reopens directly on the Urgent tab (not the dashboard), creating and opening a list and confirming a reload reopens directly into that list's detail view, then disabling the preference via the modal and confirming a subsequent reload goes back to ignoring the saved view and lands on the dashboard as before — plus a second session confirming the modal's title/label/hint and the header button's `aria-label` all render correctly in both French and English via the language switcher. Zero console errors/page errors/failed requests across both runs. **Not yet done**: no verification on a real mobile device (same caveat as most frontend work in this log — only Playwright's desktop-viewport emulation was exercised, though the modal reuses the same responsive modal/toggle patterns already shipped and verified elsewhere); no way yet to reset this preference from the admin panel if a user gets stuck (not requested, and `is_admin` intentionally can't reach another user's own preferences); and restoring into a list that belongs to a house other than the currently-selected one was reasoned about (`selectList` fetches `/lists/{id}` directly, independent of `state.currentHouseId`) but not specifically clicked through with two houses in play.

- **Pinning shared lists to the dashboard**: a recipient of a directly-shared list can now pin it so it shows up on their own dashboard alongside their house's own lists, instead of only in the "Partagé avec moi" tab — see the dedicated "Pinning shared lists to the dashboard" bullet in the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) for the full design (`list_shares.is_pinned_to_dashboard`, migration 11, `PATCH /api/v1/lists/{id}/share/pin`, why it's scoped to `list_shares` only and not `space_shares`, the client-side dashboard merge in `app.js`'s `loadDashboard`/`shares.js`'s `loadPinnedSharedLists`). New/changed backend: `internal/db/migrations/0011_pin_shared_lists.sql`, `internal/db/shares.go` (`SetListSharePinned`, and every existing `list_shares` read/write updated to carry the new column), `internal/handlers/shares.go` (`handleListSharePin`), one new route in `internal/handlers/app.go`. Frontend: `app.js` gained a `PIN_ICON_SVG` constant and a 📌/📌-filled pin/unpin button in `buildListCard` (shown only for `list.access_source === 'list_share'`), `toggleListPin`, and the dashboard merge in `loadDashboard`; `shares.js` gained `loadPinnedSharedLists` and now also carries `is_pinned_to_dashboard` through `loadSharedView`'s per-list merge so the "Partagé avec moi" tab's own pin button reflects the right state. `sw.js`'s existing share-endpoint regex (`^\/api\/v1\/(custom-categories|lists)\/[^/]+\/share(\/[^/]+)?$`) already matched `/share/pin` with no changes needed, so the new endpoint correctly returns a `503` "requires connectivity" response while offline, same as granting/revoking a share (`SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v26` for the content-only changes to already-cached files). Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#patch-apiv1listsidsharepin) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#space_shares-and-list_shares). Verified: two new `internal/db/shares_test.go` cases (`TestSetListSharePinned` — defaults to unpinned, toggles on and back off, `ErrNotFound` for a user with no `list_shares` row at all; and `TestListSharedListsForUser` extended to confirm a pinned direct share comes back with `IsPinnedToDashboard: true` while a Space-only share never does) — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean — plus a full live curl walkthrough: sharing a list, confirming it defaults to `is_pinned_to_dashboard: false`, pinning it and confirming `?shared_with_me=true` reflects `true`, unpinning back to `false`, a `404` when the caller (the list's own owner, who holds no `list_shares` row on their own list) tries to pin it, and a `400` on a request body missing `pinned`. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of the frontend (the 📌 button's appearance/toggle on a card in the "Partagé avec moi" tab, a pinned card actually appearing on the dashboard in the right grid with the 👥 badge, unpinning from the dashboard card itself) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log.

  A follow-up bug report claimed this endpoint rejected a share recipient with `403 "not a member of this house"`. Re-verified from scratch against the code as shipped above (fresh database, a recipient in a completely unrelated House, `UserCanAccessHouse` asserted `false` before pinning) and the report did not reproduce: `handleListSharePin` never called `authorizeHouseAccess` to begin with, exactly per the design described above. Added `internal/handlers/shares_test.go`'s `TestHandleListSharePinDoesNotRequireHouseMembership` as a permanent regression guard either way — it calls the handler directly (bypassing `Routes()`'s mux, since only the handler's own authorization logic is under test) with a caller who has zero House relationship to the list, asserts `200`, and separately asserts the House *owner* (full membership, but no `list_shares` row of their own) gets `404` — proving the check really is "holds a share", not "is a House member" in either direction. Also strengthened `db.ListSharedListsForUser`'s doc comment to spell out that its one House-related SQL condition (`lists.house_id NOT IN (...)`) is a dedup exclusion, never an access requirement — every row is sourced by a `shared_with_user_id = ?` match.

  **A second report, with actual server logs, pointed at a real bug — but not the endpoint above.** The logs showed `GET /api/v1/lists` and `GET /api/v1/price-alerts` both `403`ing immediately on an ordinary dashboard reload (no `PATCH .../share/pin` anywhere in them), followed a few milliseconds later by `GET /api/v1/houses` `200` and the *same* two endpoints succeeding — a transient race, not a permanent authorization bug, but `loadDashboard()`'s error handling treats a `403` as a genuine failure (unlike a network error) and calls `showError()`, so it briefly flashed the same "not a member of this house" text as a banner right on the dashboard. Root cause: `state.currentHouseId` is only *authoritative* once `loadHouses()` has validated it against a live `GET /api/v1/houses` — before that it can hold whatever `hydrateFromCache()` derived from the IndexedDB mirror (itself possibly stale, e.g. left over from a different account previously signed into the same browser). Several independent triggers can call `loadDashboard()`/`loadNotifications()` — the initial `init()` sequence itself, `i18n.js`'s `trakka:lang-changed` event (dispatched on every `loadLang()` call, **including the very first, page-bootstrap one** — not just a genuine user-initiated switch), the `visibilitychange`/`online` listeners, and a `trakka-sync-complete` message from the service worker — and none of them coordinated with each other about whether `loadHouses()` had actually resolved yet.

  Fixed with two complementary changes, both in this session: (1) `static/js/i18n.js`'s `loadLang()` now only dispatches `trakka:lang-changed` from its *second* call onward (a new `hasLoadedOnce` flag) — the very first call, at page-load bootstrap, has nothing dynamic to re-render yet (`applyTranslations()` already handles the static `data-i18n` markup unconditionally), so reacting to it was pure downside; a genuine mid-session switch via the header dropdown is completely unaffected, since `hasLoadedOnce` is already `true` by the time a user can click it. (2) The deeper, structural fix: `static/js/app.js` gained `ensureHousesLoaded()` — a promise-memoized gate (`housesLoadedOnce`/`housesLoadingPromise`) that `loadDashboard()` now awaits at its very top, and that `init()` calls instead of `loadHouses()` directly — so `state.currentHouseId` is *guaranteed* validated against the live server before any house-scoped fetch fires, no matter which of the independent triggers calls it first or how they're timed relative to each other. `notifications.js`'s `loadNotifications()` (the other `state.currentHouseId`-scoped loader, called from the same set of triggers) awaits the same gate. `planning.js`/`urgent.js`/`spaces.js`/`shares.js`'s own tab loaders needed no equivalent change: none of them can become the active tab before `restoreLastView()` runs at the tail of `init()`, by which point `ensureHousesLoaded()` has already resolved via `loadDashboard()`'s own call earlier in the same sequence.

  Verified with real headless-Chromium (Playwright) sessions rather than just reasoning about the code — this bug specifically motivated reaching for actual browser automation instead of static analysis, given how much timing determines whether it manifests. Three separate reproduction attempts against the *unfixed* code (switching accounts in the same browser storage; sharing+pinning a list then reloading 25 times in a row with `fetch` instrumented to capture a stack trace on every call; and literally killing and restarting the server process mid-session before reloading, matching the report's own description) all failed to trigger the exact race even once — headless Chromium's IndexedDB/fetch scheduling on localhost is apparently fast and consistent enough that the specific interleaving the logs showed doesn't reproduce this way, so the precise trigger among the four candidates remains unconfirmed. The `ensureHousesLoaded()` gate doesn't depend on pinning down which one it was, though: it's correct by construction (a single-flight promise every caller awaits) and eliminates the entire class of "house-scoped fetch fires before `state.currentHouseId` is validated" races regardless of which trigger or timing caused this specific report. Separately confirmed via Playwright that a genuine, user-initiated language switch (clicking the header's FR/EN dropdown) still correctly re-renders dynamic content and shows no banner, i.e. the `i18n.js` change didn't regress the feature it's part of. `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean (no Go files touched), `node --check` on every edited JS file. `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v27` for the content-only changes to already-cached `app.js`/`i18n.js`/`notifications.js`. **If this resurfaces**: capture the exact `fetch` call (URL including query string — the server's own request log only records `r.URL.Path`, not the query string, so a bare `"path":"/api/v1/lists"` log line can't by itself distinguish the house-scoped call from the shared-with-me one) and, ideally, a stack trace (the instrumentation approach in this session's Playwright scripts — monkey-patching `window.fetch` via `context.addInitScript` before any page script runs — is the fastest way to get one) to finally identify which of the four trigger points is actually responsible.

- **Mobile-accessible pin/unpin option for shared lists**: a follow-up request asked for the pin/unpin toggle above to also be reachable as a dedicated, text-labeled option — not just a bare icon — and specifically to work well on a narrow screen, mirroring `list_view.js`'s existing item-row pattern (a `[⋮]` kebab opening a bottom sheet on mobile, direct icon buttons on desktop) rather than introducing a different interaction. `buildListCard`'s `list.access_source === 'list_share'` branch (`static/js/app.js`) now splits its pin button in two: the existing 📌 icon button stays but is now `hidden md:flex` (desktop only), and a new `[⋮]` kebab button (`flex md:hidden`, `KEBAB_ICON_SVG` — defined in `list_view.js`, referenced here the same way `PENCIL_ICON_SVG` already was, since it's only read at render/click time after every script has loaded) opens a new `#list-card-actions-sheet` bottom sheet on mobile, styled and wired exactly like `#item-actions-sheet` (a dialog with a title set to the list's name and one text-labeled action row). The sheet's single option relabels itself based on state — `📌 Épingler sur le tableau de bord` when unpinned, `📍 Retirer du tableau de bord` when already pinned (`openListCardActionsSheet` swaps both the emoji and the `modals.listActions.pin`/`unpin` i18n text) — and its click handler just closes the sheet and calls the same `toggleListPin(list)` the desktop icon button already used, so both entry points share one code path and one `PATCH /api/v1/lists/{id}/share/pin` call; `refreshVisibleView()` (unchanged) is what re-renders the dashboard/"Partagé avec moi" tab in place afterward, with no full page reload either way. Since `shares.js`'s "Partagé avec moi" tab renders through this same `buildListCard`, the kebab/sheet/desktop-icon split and the new badge below are automatically present there too, not just on the dashboard grids — satisfying the "works in both the shared-lists view and the dashboard, mobile and desktop" requirement without a second implementation. Separately, a card now also carries visible feedback independent of the button's own state: a small `📌 Épinglée` badge (new `amber` entry in `badge()`'s color map) appears in the card's top badge row, next to the existing `👥 Partagé` one, whenever `list.is_pinned_to_dashboard` is true — so a pinned card is recognizable at a glance even before hovering/tapping its action control. New `common.listActionsAriaLabel`, `shares.pinnedBadge`, and `modals.listActions.{pin,unpin}` i18n keys added to both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`. No backend or `sw.js` write-path changes were needed (the endpoint and its offline `503` behavior already existed); `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v32` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`app.js`/the locale files. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), `node --check` on the edited JS, and a Python `html.parser` pass confirming `index.html`'s tags still balance. **Not yet done**: same as the base pinning feature above — no browser verification of either the kebab/sheet on a narrow viewport or the new badge, no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: on a narrow/mobile viewport, confirm the 📌 icon is hidden and the `[⋮]` kebab is shown instead on every shared-list card (dashboard grids and the "Partagé avec moi" tab alike), tapping it opens the sheet with the list's name as its title and the correctly-worded pin/unpin option, tapping that option closes the sheet and toggles the pin (card moves on/off the dashboard, badge appears/disappears) without a page reload; on a wide/desktop viewport confirm the kebab is hidden and the icon button alone still works as before.

- **Pinning fixed and extended to cover shared Spaces, not just directly-shared Lists**: a bug report found that a List reached *only* through a shared Space (no `list_shares` row of its own — see "Pinning shared lists" above) could not be pinned at all: `PATCH /api/v1/lists/{id}/share/pin` (`SetListSharePinned`) only ever `UPDATE`d an existing `list_shares` row, so it returned `404` for exactly this case, and there was no way to pin a shared Space as a whole either. Both are fixed/added in this session — see the rewritten "Pinning shared lists (and shared Spaces) to the dashboard" bullet in the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) for the full design; this entry just records what changed and how it was verified. Backend: `internal/db/migrations/0012_pin_shared_spaces.sql` adds `space_shares.is_pinned_to_dashboard`; `internal/db/shares.go` gained `spaceSharePermissionForList` (a `lists JOIN space_shares` lookup) and `SetSpaceSharePinned`, and `SetListSharePinned` now falls back to an `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` auto-create when the plain `UPDATE` affects zero rows; `ListSharedListsForUser`'s `space_share` branch now selects `ss.is_pinned_to_dashboard` instead of a hardcoded `0`; `ListSpacesSharedWithUser` is a new query (Space-level equivalent of `ListSharedListsForUser`) backing a new `?shared_with_me=true` mode on `GET /api/v1/custom-categories` (`internal/handlers/categories.go`); `internal/handlers/shares.go` gained `handleSpaceSharePin`, routed at `PATCH /api/v1/custom-categories/{id}/share/pin` in `internal/handlers/app.go` — this path (nesting under the existing `/custom-categories/{id}` resource) was chosen over the `/api/v1/spaces/shares/:id/pin` shape floated in the request that prompted this, to stay consistent with every other Space-sharing route already living under `/custom-categories/{id}/share...` rather than introducing a second URL root for the same resource. Frontend: `static/js/app.js`'s `buildListCard` now shows the pin icon/kebab for `list.access_source === 'space_share'` too (previously `'list_share'` only), so an individual List reached via a shared Space can be pinned the same way a directly-shared one already could; `static/js/spaces.js` gained `sharedCategories`/`sharedCategoryLists` state, `loadSharedCustomCategories`/`loadSharedCategoryLists`, a new "Espaces partagés avec moi" section (`buildSharedCategorySection`) in the "Espaces" tab listing every Space shared with the caller regardless of pin state (there has to be *some* place to discover and pin one for the first time), and a `[⋮]` kebab opening a new `#space-card-actions-sheet` bottom sheet (mirroring `#list-card-actions-sheet` almost exactly) for the pin/unpin toggle (`toggleSpacePin`) — unlike `toggleListPin`'s `refreshVisibleView()`, this explicitly calls both `refreshSpacesIfActive()` and `loadDashboard()` regardless of which tab is active, since `setActiveTab('dashboard')` doesn't itself trigger a refetch (only `showDashboard()` does) and the whole point of pinning a Space is for its lists to appear the moment the user next looks at the dashboard, not only after some unrelated trigger refreshes it. `static/sw.js` excludes `?shared_with_me=true` custom-categories from the plain categories IndexedDB mirror (mirroring the existing lists exclusion) and answers it with an empty array while offline; `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v33`. New `modals.listActions.{pinSpace,unpinSpace}` and `spaces.{sharedSectionTitle,actionsAriaLabel}` i18n keys in both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`. A documented, intentional interaction rather than a bug: pinned state is combined via OR across the two levels, so unpinning one List individually while its parent Space is still pinned as a whole leaves the card on the dashboard regardless — there is currently no "pin the Space but exclude this one List" option, and clicking that List's own unpin control in that situation will look like a no-op from the dashboard's point of view even though the underlying `list_shares` row did flip. Verified: five new `internal/db` tests (`TestSetSpaceSharePinned`, `TestSetListSharePinnedViaSpaceAccess` — the core regression case, confirming the auto-created row mirrors the Space's permission and leaves `AccessLevelForList` unchanged — `TestListSpacesSharedWithUser`, `TestListSharedListsForUserSpacePinPropagates`, plus the pre-existing `TestListSharedListsForUser`/`TestAccessLevelForList` re-run unchanged) and a new `internal/handlers` test (`TestHandleSpaceSharePinDoesNotRequireOwnership`, mirroring `TestHandleListSharePinDoesNotRequireHouseMembership`) — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean. Additionally verified with a full live two-user curl walkthrough against a real running server (a throwaway `DB_PATH`, register two accounts, share a category with the second one): confirmed `PATCH /api/v1/lists/{id}/share/pin` on a list reached *only* via the shared Space — previously `404`, the exact bug this session fixes — now returns `200` and auto-creates a `list_shares` row with the Space's own `write` permission; confirmed pinning the whole Space via `PATCH /api/v1/custom-categories/{id}/share/pin` makes *both* of its lists (one individually unpinned-but-directly-shared, one reachable only via the Space) come back `is_pinned_to_dashboard: true` from `?shared_with_me=true` with no per-list action; confirmed unpinning the Space reverts both; and confirmed the edge cases — `404` for the Space's own owner (holds no `space_shares` row of their own), `400` for a request body missing `pinned`, `404` for a nonexistent category id, `401` unauthenticated. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of the frontend (the "Espaces partagés avec moi" section, the space kebab/sheet, a pinned Space's lists actually appearing on the dashboard grids) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log.

- **Rename house**: `PUT /api/v1/houses/{id}` (owner-only, `internal/handlers.handleHousesUpdate`/`internal/db.UpdateHouse`) already existed and was already documented ([docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#put-apiv1housesid)) from the original houses feature — every session touching this has only ever needed a frontend surface for it, no backend change. The UI went through two iterations in the same handoff window, worth recording because the first one was explicitly rejected as poor UX: a first pass tucked the rename control inside the "Membres de la Maison" modal (a pencil next to a house-name row there), which a follow-up request called out as too hidden — renaming isn't a membership operation and shouldn't require opening that modal first. It was replaced outright (not layered on top of) by an inline, one-click control directly on the dashboard header, which is what ships now: `#house-toolbar` (the `Maison` label + `#house-select` + a ✏️ pencil button, `#rename-house-inline-button` — same pencil path as `list_view.js`'s `PENCIL_ICON_SVG`/the list-detail header's edit button — + the "Membres" button) and a sibling `#rename-house-inline-form` are mutually exclusive siblings in `static/index.html`; toggling one `hidden` and the other visible (`openRenameHouseInline`/`closeRenameHouseInline` in `app.js`) swaps the row between "browse" and "edit" in place, no modal at all. The pencil is visible only when `currentHouseRole() === 'owner'` (`updateRenameHouseButton()`, called everywhere `updateManageMembersButton()` already was — `loadHouses`, `selectHouse`, `hydrateFromCache` — so it never lags behind whatever role state the rest of the header reflects); clicking it prefills `#rename-house-inline-input` with the current name and focuses/selects it, and the form carries a green ✓ "confirm" button and a rose ✕ "cancel" one (Feather-style check/X paths) rather than text buttons, keeping the whole interaction to a single click plus Enter (or a tap on ✓). Escape while the form is open closes it back to browse mode (a dedicated `keydown` listener guarded by `isRenameHouseInlineOpen()`), and `selectHouse()` also force-closes it defensively (switching houses mid-edit isn't reachable through the UI since the toolbar, including `#house-select`, is hidden while editing, but this keeps state consistent regardless). Submitting `PUT`s the trimmed name, patches `state.houses` in place and re-populates `#house-select` so the dropdown reflects the new name immediately (no full `loadHouses()` round trip), then shows `static/js/undo.js`'s `TrakkaToast.success(message)` (a sibling to the existing `TrakkaUndo.schedule` countdown/undo toast added in the first pass and kept as-is — reuses the same `#toast-container`, no undo button or countdown bar, since a rename is already committed by the time it shows). New `dashboard.rename{HouseAriaLabel,ConfirmAriaLabel,CancelAriaLabel,Success}` i18n keys in both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json` (the first pass's now-dead `modals.members.rename*` keys were removed rather than left stale). No `sw.js` write-path changes were needed either time — `PUT /api/v1/houses/{id}` was already generically mirrored into the IndexedDB houses store and already correctly returns a `503` "requires connectivity" response while offline (see "Houses are read-mirrored but not offline-writable" in [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md)); `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v29` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`app.js`/the locale files. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), `node --check` on the edited JS, and a Python `html.parser` pass confirming `index.html`'s tags still balance. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of either iteration — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: as a house's owner, confirm the pencil appears next to the selector, click it, edit the name, and confirm the header selector updates immediately along with the success toast; as a non-owner member, confirm the pencil stays hidden; confirm Escape and the ✕ button both cancel cleanly back to the selector without saving; and confirm a `PUT` attempt while offline surfaces the existing "requires connectivity" error rather than silently queuing.

- **Pinning: dashboard didn't refresh without a reload, and a House-visible (not owned, not explicitly shared) Space couldn't be pinned at all**: two more bug reports against the pinning feature above. (1) **No dynamic refresh**: pinning/unpinning a shared List or Space correctly wrote to the database, but the Dashboard tab often kept showing stale content until a full page reload. Root cause: of the five dashboard tabs, only `setActiveTab('dashboard')` (`static/js/planning.js`) skipped refetching on click — `planning`/`urgent`/`spaces`/`shared` all called their own loader when clicked, but clicking back to "Tableau de bord" via the tab button just toggled visibility, relying on `showDashboard()` (used only by the logo link) or some earlier, possibly-stale `loadDashboard()` call. Fixed by adding `if (tab === 'dashboard') loadDashboard();` alongside the other four, and a new `isDashboardTabActive()` helper (mirroring `isPlanningTabActive`/etc.) so `app.js`'s `toggleListPin` can eagerly re-run `loadDashboard()` after a pin/unpin without double-fetching when already on that tab (`toggleSpacePin` in `spaces.js` already unconditionally called `loadDashboard()`, since it's only ever invoked from the Espaces tab). Both toggle functions also gained a `TrakkaToast.success(...)` confirmation (new `shares.{pinnedToast,unpinnedToast}`/`spaces.{pinnedToast,unpinnedToast}` i18n keys), mirroring the existing house-rename toast, since pinning from a card that isn't even on screen (e.g. the "Partagé avec moi" tab) previously gave no feedback at all. (2) **A Space visible only through House membership couldn't be pinned**: the existing pin endpoint (`handleSpaceSharePin`) only ever recognized an explicit `space_shares` grant — a fellow House member who could see a Space only because someone else in the *same* House tagged one of its own lists with their personal Space (no share, no ownership) got a `404` and had no way to discover the Space as a first-class object at all, even though `GET /api/v1/lists`/`{id}` already embedded its `custom_category` on that shared list per the "Custom categories" design. Fixed with a new access path, parallel to `space_shares` rather than replacing it: `internal/db/migrations/0013_pin_house_spaces.sql` adds `space_house_pins` (`custom_category_id`, `user_id`, `UNIQUE` on the pair) — no `permission` column, since House membership already fully determines the underlying lists' access via `AccessLevelForList`, this table exists purely to record the pin preference; row presence means pinned, so `db.SetSpaceHousePinned` unpins via `DELETE` rather than flipping a flag. `db.spaceAccessibleViaHouse(categoryID, userID)` is the new "is this category tagged on a list in one of userID's Houses" check — it explicitly excludes the category's own owner (`cc.user_id != ?`), since an owner is typically also a House member of wherever their own Space is used and would otherwise be able to "pin" their own already-visible Space for no purpose (caught by a db-layer and a handler-layer regression test, not by the original implementation — see [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#space_house_pins)). `handleSpaceSharePin` now tries `SetSpaceSharePinned` first and only falls back to `SetSpaceHousePinned` on `ErrNotFound`, returning a new lightweight `models.SpacePinStatus` (`{custom_category_id, is_pinned_to_dashboard, access_source: "house_member"}`) in the fallback case since there's no `SpaceShare` row to return. `db.ListSpacesSharedWithUser` was renamed `ListSpacesVisibleToUser` and now `UNION ALL`s a second source into its query — every category the caller doesn't own that's tagged on a list in one of their Houses, `LEFT JOIN`ed against `space_house_pins` for the pin flag — so `GET /api/v1/custom-categories?shared_with_me=true` (unchanged endpoint, broadened meaning) now returns both `access_source: "space_share"` and `"house_member"` rows; `CustomCategory` gained an `AccessSource` field to carry it (mirroring `List.AccessSource`). A new `db.ListPinnedHouseSpaceLists` + `GET /api/v1/lists?pinned_house_spaces=true` (a third, separate mode from `?shared_with_me=true` — deliberately not unioned into that query, since `ListSharedListsForUser`'s own House-membership exclusion would filter every `house_member`-sourced row straight back out, being the exact opposite condition) is what actually surfaces a pinned House Space's lists on the dashboard, including from a House other than the one currently selected. Frontend: `static/js/app.js`'s `loadDashboard` now merges three sources (own-House listing, pinned shares, pinned House-space lists) deduped by list id (a `house_member`-sourced list is often already present via the caller's own currently-selected House, in which case pinning's only visible effect is the 📌 badge — its cross-House value shows once a *different* House is selected instead), and `buildListCard`'s pin/kebab branch now also covers `access_source === 'house_member'`; `static/js/spaces.js`'s "Espaces" tab gained a second heading, "Espaces de la Maison" (new `spaces.houseSectionTitle`/`houseBadge` i18n keys, a 🏠 badge distinct from the 👥 "Partagé" one), sourced from the same broadened `sharedCategories` array split by `access_source`, with its lists drawn from `spacesLists` (current House) plus a new `houseSpaceLists` fetch (cross-House pinned ones) rather than `sharedCategoryLists` (which, per the exclusion above, never contains a `house_member`-sourced list). `static/sw.js` gained the same offline exclusion/empty-array-while-offline treatment for `?pinned_house_spaces=true` that `?shared_with_me=true` already had (`SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v34`). Verified: nine new/changed `internal/db` tests (`TestSpaceAccessibleViaHouseMemberPin` including the owner-exclusion case, `TestListSpacesVisibleToUserHouseMember`, `TestListPinnedHouseSpaceLists`, `TestListSpacesVisibleToUser` renamed/extended) and two new `internal/handlers` tests (`TestHandleSpaceSharePinFallsBackToHouseMembership` including its own owner-exclusion assertion) — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean. Unlike most pinning-related entries in this log, **this one was verified with real headless-Chromium (Playwright) sessions against the actual built server**, not just curl/unit tests, specifically because the "no reload needed" claim is exactly the kind of timing-dependent behavior static analysis can't confirm: three scripted browser runs (a two-user same-House scenario; a three-user cross-House scenario — the fellow member's own House selected in the dropdown, the pinned Space's list living in a completely different House they also belong to; and a plain direct-List-share pin from the "Partagé avec moi" tab) all confirmed, via the real tab buttons and kebab/bottom-sheet UI, zero console/page errors, a toast on pin, the "Espaces de la Maison" section rendering with the correct badge, a House-visible Space's pin/unpin correctly toggling its cross-House lists on and off the dashboard with **no page reload**, and the same for the pre-existing List-share pin flow this session's tab-refresh fix also covers. **Not yet done**: no verification on a real mobile device/touch input (Playwright viewport emulation only, and only a desktop viewport was exercised this time — the narrow-viewport kebab/sheet variant for a House-visible Space's card wasn't specifically re-checked, though it reuses the identical `#space-card-actions-sheet` already verified for the `space_share` case above).

- **Touch gesture layer for mobile**: a new `static/js/gestures.js` adds two independent, touch-only gestures on top of the existing tap/click UI, neither of which removes or replaces any existing control — every action either gesture triggers already had (and keeps) an ordinary accessible button. (1) **Left-edge swipe-back**: a touch starting within `EDGE_BACK_ZONE_PX` (30px) of the screen's left edge, dragged rightward past `EDGE_BACK_THRESHOLD_PX` (90px), calls a new `goBack()` — it closes the topmost open modal/bottom sheet if one is open, or (if none is) returns from the list detail view to the dashboard via a real `.click()` on the existing `#back-button`, or is a no-op at the dashboard root. `goBack()`'s modal-closing half is fully generic: every modal/sheet in `index.html` already closes itself via a `click` listener checking `event.target === <itself>` (the "click the backdrop" pattern, used by all 14 of them — `new-list-modal`, `category-modal`, `new-house-modal`, `members-modal`, `share-modal`, `notifications-modal`, `admin-settings-modal`, `user-settings-modal`, `edit-item-modal`, `add-item-sheet`, `item-actions-sheet`, `list-card-actions-sheet`, `space-card-actions-sheet`, `image-preview-modal`), so `closeTopOverlay()` just queries `[id$="-modal"]:not([hidden]), [id$="-sheet"]:not([hidden])` and dispatches a synthetic `click` on each match — reusing every modal's own close logic without gestures.js needing to import or name a single `closeXModal` function, and automatically covering any future modal/sheet that follows the same id convention. During the drag, a small circular chevron pill (`getBackIndicator()`, `aria-hidden`, page-level chrome created once and reused) slides in from the edge and `<main>` itself nudges right by up to 28px (`EDGE_BACK_MAIN_PULL_PX`) as visual feedback — both purely decorative, since the action they precede already has its own accessible control. A touch starting on an item card (`.swipe-item`, see below) or on `#dashboard-tabs` (the existing horizontally-scrollable mobile tab strip, `base.css`) is explicitly excluded from edge-back tracking so the two gesture layers, and this gesture and that pre-existing native scroll strip, can never both react to the same touch. (2) **Item card swipe actions**, wired into the list detail view only via a single new call at the end of `buildItemRow` (`list_view.js`): `attachItemSwipeGestures(li, item, { canToggleDone: showCheckbox })` restructures the already-built `<li>` so it can slide — every child `buildItemRow` appended is moved into a `.swipe-item__foreground` div carrying the card's original classes (so it's pixel-identical while not being swiped), with a green `✓` background (swipe right) and/or a red `🗑️` background (swipe left) sitting behind it, revealed as the foreground slides away. Swiping right past `ITEM_SWIPE_THRESHOLD_PX` (80px) calls the exact same `toggleDone(item)` the row's checkbox already calls; swiping left past the same threshold calls the exact same `removeItem(item)` the row's trash button already calls — including its own 5s `TrakkaUndo` grace period, so a swipe-to-delete is never permanent without a chance to undo it, for free. `canToggleDone` mirrors `buildItemRow`'s own `showCheckbox` parameter, so a `custom` list (no "done" concept, per `FIELD_VISIBILITY_BY_TYPE`) simply clamps a rightward drag to zero, exactly like its checkbox column already being replaced by a line-number marker. Below `ITEM_SWIPE_THRESHOLD_PX` the card snaps back to neutral (the "aimant" requirement); at or past it, the foreground finishes sliding fully off (`translateX(±100%)`, a 180ms transition) and only *then* — via `settleThenRun`, keyed off `transitionend` with a 220ms fallback timer in case the element gets detached by an unrelated re-render first — does the actual `toggleDone`/`removeItem` call fire, so the visual completion and the state mutation are sequenced rather than simultaneous. Both gestures share the same anti-conflict design: every listener is `{ passive: true }` and none of them ever calls `preventDefault`; instead, the first ~10px of movement decide whether a touch is horizontal or vertical (mirroring `attachLongPress`'s own `LONG_PRESS_MOVE_TOLERANCE_PX` in `list_view.js`), and the moment it's vertical, the gesture stops touching that element at all and native scrolling proceeds exactly as if gestures.js didn't exist. Item cards additionally get `touch-action: pan-y` (set via inline style on the foreground, not a CSS class, to avoid any dependency on the Tailwind Play CDN's version) so the browser itself never treats a horizontal drag there as a scroll/navigation candidate in the first place. A module-level `IS_TOUCH_DEVICE` check (`'ontouchstart' in window || navigator.maxTouchPoints > 0`) skips *all* of gestures.js's DOM restructuring and listener attachment on a device with no touchscreen, so a desktop/mouse-only session carries zero overhead for a feature it could never trigger — the existing `attachLongPress` doesn't need the equivalent check since it only ever adds listeners, never restructures DOM, but `attachItemSwipeGestures` does the latter (wrapping every item row's children, adding two extra background divs) on every render, which is only worth doing where the gesture can actually fire. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `/js/gestures.js` (`SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v35`); no new i18n keys were needed since both action backgrounds are `aria-hidden` decoration behind an already-translated, already-accessible control. Verified: `node --check` on the new and edited JS files, a Python `html.parser` pass confirming `index.html`'s tags still balance, `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched, unaffected by a pure-frontend change). **Not yet done**: no real-device or browser-automation verification at all — no browser automation tooling (Playwright or similar) was available in the sandbox this was built in, so none of the following has actually been clicked/tapped through yet: the edge-swipe-back gesture actually closing a modal/sheet or returning to the dashboard, the visual pill/`<main>`-nudge feedback, the item-card swipe-right/swipe-left thresholds and snap-back behavior on both a `custom` list (right-swipe should do nothing) and every other list type, that a genuine vertical scroll through a long item list is never interrupted or visually disturbed by either gesture, and that ordinary taps on every control inside an item row (checkbox, quantity stepper, thumbnail, edit/delete/kebab buttons) still work unaffected now that they sit inside the new `.swipe-item__foreground` wrapper. Try all of the above on a real touchscreen (or at minimum Chrome DevTools' touch-emulation mode) before relying on it.

- **PWA install guide + in-app help modal**: a new [docs/INSTALLATION.md](../docs/INSTALLATION.md) walks an end user through adding Trakka to their home screen — separate from [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md), which documents the underlying service-worker/manifest mechanism for contributors rather than the click-by-click steps a user follows, and cross-linked from it and from `README.md`'s Documentation list. Per the "Documentation" convention below, the file itself is written in English like every other file under `docs/`, even though its subject (getting a non-technical user's phone set up) is naturally something a French-speaking user would otherwise expect in French — the in-app modal described next is where the actual end-user-facing, i18n'd copy lives. The in-app counterpart is a new "📱 Comment installer l'application sur mon téléphone ?" button appended below the existing form in `#user-settings-modal` (`static/index.html`), opening a new `#install-help-modal` (`static/js/install-help.js`, a new file, loaded after `settings.js`) that shows four numbered, icon-illustrated steps for either iOS/iPadOS Safari or Android Chrome — matching the two platforms `docs/INSTALLATION.md` covers — switchable via a small iOS/Android tab pair styled like `planning.js`'s dashboard tabs. `detectMobileOS()` preselects the tab matching the visiting device (iPadOS 13+'s "reports itself as a Mac" quirk is handled with the standard `navigator.platform === 'MacIntel' && navigator.maxTouchPoints > 1` check, same reasoning already used for Trakka's own device-class detection elsewhere) but both tabs stay one tap away regardless, since sniffing is only ever a hint — reading the steps from a desktop browser before trying on a phone, or an in-app browser reporting a generic UA, are both real cases where the detected default would be wrong. Because this modal opens on top of the already-open settings modal, it follows the exact "modal stacked on modal" convention `spaces.js`'s category-modal-on-top-of-list-modal already established: `closeInstallHelpModal` only releases the shared `overflow-hidden` body class if `userSettingsEls.modal` isn't still open behind it, and `settings.js`'s own Escape handler was updated to skip closing while `installHelpEls.modal` is the one currently on top, so a single Escape press closes just the topmost modal rather than both at once. All step text is static markup with `data-i18n`/`data-i18n-aria-label` attributes (new `modals.userSettings.installHelpButton` and `modals.installHelp.*` keys in both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`), not JS-built strings, matching every other modal's own convention rather than needing any DOM-building code. `sw.js`'s `APP_SHELL` now also caches `/js/install-help.js` (`SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v39`). Verified: `node --check` on the new and edited JS files, a Python `html.parser` pass confirming `index.html`'s tags still balance, and a JSON parse check on both edited locale files — `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched, unaffected by a pure-frontend-and-docs change). **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: open Settings, click the new button, confirm the modal opens on top with the right tab preselected for your device, switch tabs manually, confirm Escape and clicking the backdrop each close only the install-help modal (settings stays open, still scrollable-locked) and a second Escape/backdrop-click then closes settings too, and confirm both French and English render correctly via the language switcher.

- **Manual item reordering (drag & drop)**: `items.position` already existed from the very first schema (used to order `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}`'s embedded `items` and settable per-item via `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH /api/v1/items`, see the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics)) — this session's actual gap was a way to *reorder in bulk* from a drag gesture rather than issuing one `PATCH` per moved item. `PUT /api/v1/lists/{id}/reorder` (`internal/handlers/reorder.go`'s `handleListsReorder`, `internal/db.ReorderItems` in `internal/db/items.go`) takes the request body's `item_ids` as the *complete* new ordering and assigns each one a position matching its index, inside a single transaction (`tx.ExecContext` per id, the same "no `PrepareContext`, just loop `tx.ExecContext`" convention `SetSettings`/`CreateHouseWithOwner` already use) so a failure partway through can never leave positions in a mixed old/new state. `item_ids` must be exactly a permutation of the list's current items — every current item named once, nothing else — or the whole request is rejected with the new `db.ErrInvalidReorder` (`400`, "item_ids must list every item currently in this list exactly once"): a partial list would leave the omitted items' positions ambiguous relative to the reordered ones, and silently accepting a foreign id would let a write-access holder on one list reorder items out from under a different list they don't control. Authorization is `authorizeListAccess(..., requireWrite: true)` — the same bar `PUT`/`PATCH` on an [item](../docs/API.md#items) already require, since rearranging a list's contents is an editing action, not house-management-level like deleting the list outright. Returns `200` with the list's items in their new order (same shape as `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}`'s embedded `items`). Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#put-apiv1listsidreorder) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md).

  The frontend adds a "⇅ Réordonner" button (`#reorder-list-button`, hidden whenever the list has fewer than two items or any item still carries an offline-queued `temp-item-*` id — `reorderAvailable()` in the new `static/js/reorder.js`) next to the existing share/edit buttons in the list detail header. Clicking it enters reorder mode: `list_view.js`'s `renderItems()` gained a one-line branch at its very top (`if (isReorderModeActive()) { renderReorderList(); return; }`) that hands rendering over to `reorder.js` for as long as the mode is active, flattening active+done items into one draggable column (each row a `☰` handle + title, built by `buildReorderRow`) and hiding the quick-add bar/FAB/finance/done sections so nothing else can be touched mid-drag; a fixed bottom bar (`#reorder-actions-bar`, safe-area-aware like the FAB) offers "Annuler" and "✓ Valider l'ordre". Nothing is sent to the server until "Valider" is pressed — a `reorderDraftOrder` array holds the in-progress order separately from `state.currentList.items`, so "Annuler" (or any request failure) has nothing to undo. Desktop dragging uses the native HTML5 Drag and Drop API (`draggable="true"` on each row, `dragstart`/`dragover`/`dragend` delegated once on the shared `<ul>` container rather than re-attached per row); touch devices get a hand-rolled equivalent gated behind `IS_TOUCH_DEVICE` (from `gestures.js`), deliberately starting only from the `☰` handle (`touch-action: none`, unlike the desktop path which allows a drag to start anywhere on the row) so the rest of a long reorder list stays natively scrollable. Both paths converge on the same "closest sortable target" algorithm (`reorderRowAfter`/`moveReorderRowTo`): the dragged row is physically moved in the live DOM the moment the pointer crosses a neighboring row's vertical midpoint, and `reorderDraftOrder` is only resynced from the DOM's actual order once the drag ends (`syncReorderDraftFromDOM`, called from `dragend`/`touchend`/`touchcancel`). `gestures.js`'s left-edge swipe-back zone now also excludes a touch starting on `.reorder-row` (mirroring its pre-existing `.swipe-item` exclusion), since a card's own handle can sit close enough to the screen edge on a narrow phone to otherwise be mistaken for an edge-back gesture. `showDashboard`/`selectList`/`refreshCurrentList` (`list_view.js`) all call the new `exitReorderModeIfActive()` so leaving the list, switching lists, or a background sync landing mid-drag can never strand the UI in reorder mode with stale item references. `sw.js` originally treated `PUT /lists/{id}/reorder` the same as houses/admin-settings/shares: it needed an immediate round-trip and reported success/failure right back to the UI, so it was added to the "requires connectivity" `503` block in `queueOfflineWrite` rather than being queued (queuing it through the generic per-list optimistic-edit path would have silently handed `commitReorder` a `{queued:true}` object instead of the item array it expects, corrupting `state.currentList.items`) — **this turned out to be the wrong call and was replaced by real offline queueing in a later session; see the "Fix: reorder (and the rest of the app) is now fully Offline-First" bullet further down this log.** `mirrorWriteResult` mirrors a successful *online* response's item array into IndexedDB via the existing bulk `TrakkaDB.putItems` helper. `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v41` for the new `/js/reorder.js` file. New `items.reorder{List,Hint,Cancel,Confirm,Success}` i18n keys in both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`. Verified: `internal/db/items_test.go`'s `TestReorderItems` (happy path plus rejecting a partial list, a duplicated id, and an id from a different list) and `internal/handlers/reorder_test.go` (the handler happy path, a read-only List share correctly getting `403`, and a partial `item_ids` list correctly getting `400`) — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: no browser or touch-emulation verification of the frontend at all — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: on desktop, open a list with several items, click "⇅ Réordonner", drag a card via native HTML5 DnD to a new position, confirm the live reordering feels smooth, click "✓ Valider l'ordre" and confirm the list re-renders sorted by the new order with a success toast; separately confirm "Annuler" (and navigating back to the dashboard mid-drag) discards the draft and leaves the original order untouched. On a touchscreen (or Chrome DevTools' touch-emulation mode), confirm the drag only starts from the `☰` handle, confirm ordinary vertical scrolling through a long reorder list away from any handle is unaffected, and confirm a touch starting near the left screen edge on a handle doesn't accidentally trigger the edge-swipe-back gesture instead. Also confirm the button stays hidden for a list with fewer than two items, and reappears correctly once an offline-queued item finishes syncing.

- **Fix: `/auth/login`/`/auth/register` rejected with "cross-origin request rejected" in every real browser**: a genuine regression from the security-audit commit (`13cdb01`, 2026-08-30), reported live against a running local instance and root-caused from the browser's own raw request headers rather than guessed at. `requireSameOriginWrite` (`internal/handlers/csrf.go`) rejected any request whose `Origin` header wasn't a real matching host — including the literal string `"null"`, treated identically to an attacker-controlled origin. That's correct for the case it was written for (a sandboxed iframe forging a CSRF POST — an opaque origin, serialized as `"null"`), but incomplete: per the [Fetch spec](https://fetch.spec.whatwg.org/#origin-header)'s Origin-header algorithm, a **navigational** (non-`fetch()`) request — an HTML `<form method="post">` submission — whose page has `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` in effect *also* serializes `Origin` as `"null"`, even when the request is genuinely same-origin. `SecurityHeaders` (`internal/handlers/middleware.go`) sets `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` on every response, and `/auth/login`/`/auth/register` (`templates/login.html`) are the *only two* navigational, non-`fetch()` POSTs anywhere in this app — every `/api/v1/...` write goes through `apiRequest()`'s `fetch()`, whose response tainting is always `"cors"`, which the same spec algorithm exempts from the no-referrer nulling (the real `Origin` is always sent for a `fetch()` call regardless of referrer policy). That's exactly why this broke login/register specifically, in literally every standards-compliant browser (reproduced live in Brave, Edge, and a fresh Firefox install — the browser was never the variable), while the rest of the app kept working. Fixed by treating an `Origin` of `""` (absent) or `"null"` identically: both now fall back to the `Sec-Fetch-Site` check instead of rejecting outright. This doesn't reopen the sandboxed-iframe case the `"null"` handling was originally written for: an opaque origin can never be "same-site" with anything by definition, so a genuine cross-site attacker (sandboxed iframe or otherwise) still gets `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` and is still rejected — verified by a new test case asserting exactly that alongside the fixed one. A third new test case documents the one narrow residual gap this leaves (`Origin: null` with no `Sec-Fetch-Site` at all, i.e. a browser old enough to predate Fetch Metadata support entirely): accepted deliberately, since it's the same "no usable signal → allow" trade-off this codebase already made for a bare `curl` request, and because it grants nothing usable anyway — `/auth/login`/`/auth/register` are additionally protected by their own independent double-submit `csrf_token` (`checkCSRFToken`, unrelated to this header), and every `/api/v1/...` write still requires the `SameSite=Lax` session cookie, which such a request wouldn't carry cross-site regardless of what this check decides. Verified: two new `TestRequireSameOriginWrite` cases in `internal/handlers/security_test.go` (the fixed false-positive, and the still-rejected attack) plus a third documenting the accepted edge case, `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` all clean — and, unlike most entries in this log, this fix was verified against the user's actual real-world failure: reproduced via `curl -H "Origin: null"` matching their exact browser 403 byte-for-byte, and confirmed the fix's logic against the raw header dump they captured (`sec-fetch-site: same-origin` alongside `origin: null`) before writing a single line of the fix. **Confirmed by the user's own restart**: the `cross-origin request rejected` error is gone — the next thing they hit was a completely separate `500` (see the next bullet), which only happens *after* `requireSameOriginWrite` already let the request through, proving this fix works.

- **Fix: legacy-database adoption silently skipped every migration added after the versioned engine's introduction, surfacing as `500 Internal Server Error` / `no such column: keep_last_page` on login**: reported live right after the CSRF fix above was confirmed — login got past the cross-origin check and then failed inside the handler itself, with the server log showing `"querying user: SQL logic error: no such column: keep_last_page (1)"`. Root-caused by inspecting the user's actual local `trakka.db` directly (via a throwaway Go program, since no `sqlite3` CLI was available in the sandbox): `PRAGMA user_version` read `14` (today's latest migration) but the real schema stopped exactly at migration `0009_granular_sharing.sql` — `lists.icon`/`custom_category_id`, `space_shares`/`list_shares` all present, but `users.keep_last_page` (0010), `list_shares`/`space_shares.is_pinned_to_dashboard` (0011/0012), `space_house_pins` (0013), and `pending_invitations` (0014) all missing. The bug lived in `hasExistingSchema`'s adoption branch in `migrate()` (`internal/db/migrate.go`): a database that predates the versioned engine (tables exist, `PRAGMA user_version` still its SQLite default of `0`) was stamped at `migrations[len(migrations)-1].version` — i.e. whatever the *current codebase's* highest migration number happens to be — and the function returned immediately, never running the apply loop at all. That's only correct if the engine's first-ever run against a given legacy database happens at the exact moment the engine was introduced (when "latest" meant migration 9, the last migration that reconstructs the pre-engine `schema.sql`/`addColumnIfMissing()` history — see the versioned-migration-engine bullet earlier in this log). Here, this local `trakka.db` was last touched by pre-engine code back when granular sharing (0009) was the newest feature; by the time a migration-aware binary finally ran against it, migrations 0010–0014 already existed in the tree, so adoption jumped straight to `user_version = 14` and silently skipped all five of them — leaving the schema genuinely five migrations behind while claiming to be fully current. This is a real, general bug in the engine's design (anyone whose local/deployed database predates the engine and whose *first* run under an engine-aware binary happens after further migrations have been added since would hit the identical thing), not a one-off corrupted file. Fixed in `migrate.go` by adding a `legacySchemaVersion = 9` constant — a fixed historical fact, never bumped — and adopting a legacy database at that constant instead of at `latest`; the adoption branch no longer returns early, so if `latest > legacySchemaVersion` (as it always will be from now on), execution falls straight through into the normal apply loop for everything newer, preceded by the usual `backupBeforeMigration` safety snapshot exactly as it would for any other already-versioned database catching up. `internal/db/migrate_test.go`'s `TestMigrateAdoptsPreExistingDatabase` (which used to assert adoption landed at `latest` with *no* backup — encoding the buggy behavior) now seeds a database at exactly the migration-9 shape (`seedLegacyDatabase`, which actually runs migrations 1–9 for real rather than hand-rolling a partial schema, so the subsequent catch-up migrations have real tables to act on) and skips itself when `latest > legacySchemaVersion`, deferring to the new `TestMigrateAdoptsLegacyDatabaseWithPendingMigrations`, which is the direct regression test for this exact bug: it asserts the database ends at `user_version = latest` (not just `legacySchemaVersion`), that `users.keep_last_page` genuinely exists afterward (proof the pending migrations actually ran, not just that the counter moved), that pre-existing data survived, and that a backup was taken. Because the buggy code had *already* mis-stamped the user's real `trakka.db` at `user_version = 14` before this fix was written, the code fix alone couldn't repair that already-existing file — a database already (incorrectly) stamped at `latest` short-circuits `migrate()`'s very first check (`current >= latest`) regardless of how adoption itself is fixed. The user's actual `trakka.db` needed a one-time manual correction (`PRAGMA user_version = 9`, matching its true schema shape) so that the *next* `Open()` — now running the fixed code — would fall through and genuinely apply 10–14. Verified end-to-end against a scratch copy of the user's real database first (dry run), then against the real file itself: `PRAGMA user_version` correctly re-lands at `14`, `keep_last_page`/`is_pinned_to_dashboard`/`space_house_pins`/`pending_invitations` are all now genuinely present, the pre-existing house/user rows survived untouched, and a `backups/trakka-v9-to-v14-<timestamp>.db` safety snapshot was created exactly as the engine is designed to do — plus `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean. **Not yet done**: the user has not yet restarted their server against the now-repaired `trakka.db` and confirmed login actually succeeds end-to-end (only the migration/schema layer was verified directly, not a real HTTP login round-trip against the running server). If anyone else's local or deployed database predates the versioned migration engine and was first opened by an engine-aware binary at some point after migrations 10–14 already existed in the tree, it likely has the identical `user_version = 14`-but-actually-behind mis-stamp and needs the same one-time `PRAGMA user_version = 9` correction before its next startup — there is no automatic detection/self-healing for this in the codebase (deliberately not added — a real, live-reproduced instance of this exact bug is a one-time, easily-diagnosed event, not something worth adding permanent runtime complexity to guard against).

- **Fix: dashboard/tab counters stuck stale after checking an item off (or deleting one) and navigating away before the 5s undo window closed**: reported live — checking an item done in the list detail view, then tapping back to the dashboard, left that list's card showing its pre-toggle count until a full page reload. Root cause: `toggleDone` (`static/js/list_view.js`) applies the checkbox optimistically but only fires the real `PATCH` from inside `TrakkaUndo.schedule`'s `onCommit`, deferred by the module's `DEFAULT_DELAY_MS` (5000ms — see `static/js/undo.js`'s own doc comment on why: it's what gives the "Annuler" toast something to undo before the real request goes out). `showDashboard()`'s back-button handler already calls `loadDashboard()` — a genuine refetch — but if the user navigates back inside that 5s window, the refetch runs *before* the server has received the change, and nothing re-triggers a second refresh once the deferred commit actually lands, so the stale snapshot from that first fetch just sits there until something else (a reload, a later unrelated navigation) happens to refetch again. Fixed by having `toggleDone`'s `onCommit` check `state.currentListId` once the (now-resolved) request settles: still on a list (this one or a different one navigated to in the meantime) → the existing lightweight `renderItems()` (local re-render, no network call — deliberately *not* replaced by a refetch here, since that could clobber some *other* item's own still-pending optimistic toggle with the server's not-yet-updated view of it); left the list entirely (`state.currentListId === null`, e.g. back on the dashboard or a tab) → `refreshVisibleView()` (`app.js`, the same dispatcher already used by `removeList`'s analogous deferred commit and by the sync-complete/language-switch/house-switch hooks), which refreshes whichever view is actually on screen. While tracing this, found and fixed a closely related, more severe bug in `removeItem`'s `onCommit` right next to it: it read `state.currentList.items` unconditionally to splice the item out, which throws outright if the user has navigated away by the time the deferred `DELETE` fires (`state.currentList` is `null`, or points at a different list by then) — silently aborting the real server-side delete before the `try`/`apiRequest` call it's supposed to guard ever runs, while the item stays soft-hidden client-side only. Fixed by capturing the list reference (`const list = state.currentList;`) at `removeItem`'s call time instead of re-reading it inside the deferred callback — `removeItem` is only ever invoked from a row rendered for the currently-open list, so this capture is always the correct list regardless of what's on screen 5 seconds later — with the same `renderItems()`-vs-`refreshVisibleView()` split as `toggleDone`, gated on `state.currentList === list` instead of `state.currentListId !== null` (since by the time the commit fires, `state.currentList` might now be a *different* list, not just `null`). Neither `changeQuantity` nor `toggleUrgent` needed the same fix — both already commit their `PATCH` immediately on click (no artificial delay), so the equivalent race window is just ordinary network latency, not a deliberate 5s gap. Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a manual brace/paren balance check on both edited files (no JS runtime was available in the sandbox this was built in to run a real syntax check). **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: check an item done, immediately tap back to the dashboard (well within 5s), and confirm the card's remaining-count updates on its own a few seconds later with no reload; do the same navigating to the Urgent/Budget/Espaces/Partagé tabs instead; and confirm deleting an item, then quickly switching to a *different* list before its own 5s elapses, doesn't throw in the console and does correctly remove it server-side.

- **Fix: dynamic JS-built strings across `app.js`/`list_view.js` stayed hardcoded French even in English mode**: reported live (the dashboard's remaining-item count read "3 restants" while the rest of the UI was in English) and turned into a full audit rather than a single-string patch, since CLAUDE.md's own "What's left" already flagged this as a known, larger gap ("item counts, 'Terminés (N)', API error messages, empty-state text… still hard-coded French"). Found by grepping every `.js` file for accented-French characters and known French stopwords inside string/template literals not already routed through `t()`, then manually classifying every hit (comments and dev-only `console.warn` diagnostics excluded — the latter stay English-only per this codebase's own "code/comments/logs are English" convention and were already tagged as out of scope, not touched). Confirmed the later, more mature frontend files (`urgent.js`/`planning.js`/`spaces.js`/`shares.js`/`notifications.js`/`admin.js`/`settings.js`/`install-help.js`/`reorder.js`/`gestures.js`) were already fully clean — every hardcoded string was concentrated in the two oldest files, `app.js` and `list_view.js`, exactly matching the documented gap. Fixed roughly a dozen call sites: `remainingItemsLabel`'s "N restant(s)" (now `items.remainingCountOne`/`remainingCountOther` — a hand-rolled plural, since the `t()` engine has no ICU plural support, so the caller picks the key based on the actual count, the same pattern this fix uses everywhere a count needed pluralizing); `list_view.js`'s `typeLabel` (the parenthetical "(courses)"/"(tâches)"/etc. in the item-list header — `app.js` turned out to have a **second**, dead-code copy of the same function, shadowed at runtime by `list_view.js`'s later script-load-order definition and never actually called from anywhere in `app.js` itself, so it was deleted outright rather than translated); `progressBadge`'s "N/M terminées" pill on todo-list dashboard cards; the three dashboard grids' empty-state messages ("Aucune liste de courses…"/"Aucun espace tâches…"/"Aucune liste libre…"); the network-unreachable `Error` message `apiRequest()` throws (both the `!navigator.onLine` short-circuit and the `fetch()`-itself-failed catch) — this one is more than cosmetic: most GET-loaders suppress it via `isNetworkError()`, but `toggleDone`'s own error-rollback path calls `showError(err.message)` unconditionally, so a failed done-toggle while offline really could have shown this raw French string over an English UI; the item-list "Terminés (N)" done-count label (`doneSummaryLabel.textContent`, which — unlike the *static* placeholder `#done-summary-label` already had a `items.doneDefault` key for — was rebuilt with a hardcoded template literal on every render, ignoring `t()` entirely); the empty/all-done item-list messages; the auto-detected-price badge's title/aria-label and both pending-price badges ("Prix en attente de synchro"/"Détection du prix…"); the enlarge-image lightbox button's title/aria-label; the item row's edit/delete button aria-labels; all three quantity-stepper aria-labels; the price-validation error message; and the item-done checkbox's aria-label (deliberately *reused* `urgent.markDoneAriaLabel` — identical text and purpose to what `list_view.js` needed, so no new duplicate key). `#done-summary-label`'s static HTML placeholder (`static/index.html`) gained the `data-i18n="items.doneDefault"` attribute it was missing (a table-stakes fix, though moot in practice since JS always overwrites it before the element is ever visible). All ~20 new keys were added to *both* `static/locales/{fr,en}.json` with the French side matching the original hardcoded text verbatim (so FR-mode behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged) — verified with a throwaway Go program (no `node`/`python` available in the sandbox) doing three things: confirming both files still parse as valid JSON, a full key-parity diff (flattening every dotted key path in both files and diffing the sets — came back with zero keys missing on either side, 269/269), and a `{placeholder}` consistency check (extracting every `{var}` token per key and diffing fr vs en per key — zero mismatches). **Deliberately not touched**: the three `console.warn` diagnostics in `app.js` (`purgeLocalUserData`'s cache-clear failures, the session-check warning) — dev-console-only, never reach the UI, and per this file's own convention dev-facing text should be English rather than translated via `t()` at all, but that's a separate, pre-existing style gap unrelated to the FR/EN toggle and out of scope for this fix; `i18n.js`'s own bootstrap fallback object (the hardcoded French mirror of `fr.json` used only for the few-millisecond window before the real fetch resolves) — already significantly out of sync with `fr.json` before this session (missing most of `urgent.*`/`spaces.*`/`shares.*`/`notifications.*`/most of `modals.*`, among others), reconciling it fully is a separate, much larger cleanup with negligible practical benefit given how short that window is, so this session didn't attempt it; and `templates/login.html`, already a documented, separate gap (server-rendered, never loads `i18n.js` at all). `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v43` for the content changes to `app.js`/`list_view.js`/`index.html`/both locale JSON files, all of which are cached app-shell assets. Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), the JSON-validity/key-parity/placeholder-consistency checks above, and a manual re-grep confirming zero remaining hardcoded-French hits (accented-character scan, French-stopword scan, and a full aria-label/title inventory cross-checked against `t(`) in either `app.js` or `list_view.js`. **Not yet done**: no browser verification that switching to English and exercising every one of these code paths (checking an item, viewing an empty/all-done list, an offline done-toggle failure, a scraped-price-pending badge, the quantity stepper, the enlarge-image lightbox, a todo-list's progress pill, an empty dashboard grid) actually renders the new English strings correctly on screen rather than just resolving through `t()` without a runtime error — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. `templates/login.html`'s French-only gap and `i18n.js`'s incomplete bootstrap object remain open, unrelated follow-ups (see "What's left" below).

- **Fix: reorder (and the rest of the app) is now fully Offline-First**: reported live — dragging items into a new order while offline (or on a flaky connection) surfaced a blocking "Cette action nécessite une connexion réseau." error banner instead of applying the new order. Root cause: when manual item reordering (`PUT /api/v1/lists/{id}/reorder`) was built (see the "Manual item reordering" bullet above), `sw.js`'s `queueOfflineWrite` deliberately special-cased it into the same hard `503` block used for houses/admin-settings/shares — resources whose offline-queueing is genuinely unsafe (houses need a second level of temp-id chaining nothing else in this app supports; admin settings and shares need an immediate round-trip to report success/failure into an already-open modal). Reordering doesn't share any of those constraints: it's a plain per-list item mutation, no different in kind from a `PATCH` on a single item's `position` — which *is* already queued — so exempting it was an oversight, not a deliberate trade-off, and it's what regressed the requirement (stated back in the very first line of `queueOfflineWrite`'s own "Optimistic mutations" doc comment in `list_view.js`) that every mutation resolves as a plain 2xx while offline with the optimistic state simply becoming final.

  Fixed by adding `queueOfflineReorder` (`static/sw.js`), following the exact same pattern the rest of the app's item/list writes already use: it re-derives each item's `position` from its index in the request's `item_ids` (matching `db.ReorderItems`'s own server-side assignment), writes the updated rows straight into the IndexedDB mirror via the existing bulk `TrakkaDB.putItems` helper — so the new order survives a reload even before the request ever reaches the server — enqueues the request for replay via the existing `TrakkaDB.enqueueRequest`/`scheduleSync` machinery, and synthesizes a response carrying every item in the list in its new order, i.e. the exact shape a live `200` response already carries. `commitReorder` (`static/js/reorder.js`) needed no changes at all: it already just does `state.currentList.items = updated` on a successful `apiRequest()` call and only reaches its `catch`/`showError` branch on an actual non-2xx response — since the synthesized response is a `202` (an ordinary success status), the existing code path already does the right thing once the service worker stops manufacturing a `503` for it. No temp-id resolution was needed the way pending item/list creates require elsewhere in `queueOfflineWrite`: `reorder.js`'s own `reorderAvailable()` already keeps the "⇅ Réordonner" button hidden whenever any item in the list still carries an offline-queued `temp-item-*` id, so by the time `commitReorder` can even fire, `item_ids` is guaranteed to be a set of real, numeric ids already present in the mirror.

  Confirmed this was the *only* actual gap, not just the reported symptom of a wider one: item creation/edit/delete/toggle-done/quantity-change/urgent-toggle were all re-audited against `apiRequest`'s actual control flow (`static/js/app.js`) and are — and, per `git log`, already were — genuinely Offline-First already. The reason is that `handleApiWrite` (`sw.js`) only ever falls through to `queueOfflineWrite` when the *real* `fetch(request)` inside the service worker itself throws (i.e., actually offline); `queueOfflineWrite`'s generic path (used by every item/list write except the houses/admin-settings/shares/reorder exemptions above) always answers with a `202`, which `apiRequest` treats as an ordinary success — no exception is ever thrown, so none of `toggleDone`/`removeItem`/`changeQuantity`/`toggleUrgent`/the create-item and edit-item form handlers' `catch` blocks are reachable at all while genuinely offline; their optimistic state simply becomes final, exactly as the "Optimistic mutations" doc comment at the top of that section in `list_view.js` already promises. Automatic reconnection was likewise already fully wired before this session and needed no changes: `window.addEventListener('online', ...)` (`app.js`) posts a `flush-queue` message to the controlling service worker, real Background Sync (`self.addEventListener('sync', ...)`, Chrome/Android) fires independently of any open tab, and every same-origin fetch made while `navigator.onLine` is `true` opportunistically retries the queue too (`sw.js`'s `fetch` listener) — `flushQueue()` itself already replays the queue strictly in order (a `for...of` loop with `await`, not `Promise.all`), stopping at the first entry that still fails so ordering and dependent temp-ids hold, and special-cases a `401` reply (session expired mid-queue) by leaving that entry and everything after it queued rather than discarding it. None of that needed touching — this session's fix was scoped entirely to the one broken code path.
