  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched — this is a pure `static/sw.js` change), a manual brace/paren balance check on the edited file (no `node`/`python` was available in the sandbox this was built in to run a real syntax check — same limitation noted elsewhere in this log), and re-tracing `apiRequest`'s/`handleApiWrite`'s/`queueOfflineWrite`'s control flow line by line for every one of the other listed actions (adds/edits/deletes/toggle-done) to confirm none of them share the reorder bug or any equivalent gap. **Not yet done**: no browser or touch-emulation verification — same caveat as the original "Manual item reordering" feature itself, which has never been exercised in a browser either (no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox that built either it or this fix). Before relying on it: open a list with several items, go offline (DevTools' Network → Offline, or airplane mode), drag items into a new order, click "✓ Valider l'ordre", and confirm the new order applies immediately with a success toast and *no* error banner; reload the page while still offline and confirm the new order persisted (proving the IndexedDB mirror actually picked it up, not just the in-memory `state.currentList`); then go back online and confirm the reorder silently replays to the server (e.g. via a fresh `GET` after reconnecting, or the Network tab) without the user having to do anything.

- **Offline sync visual indicators (header badge + per-list/per-item "unsynced changes" dots + a public event bus)**: requested to make the offline queue's state visible everywhere it affects, rather than only via the pre-existing bare "N en attente" text badge — full design in the new "Offline sync indicators" section of [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md), added in this same session; this entry just records what changed. `static/sw.js`'s `queueOfflineWrite`/`queueOfflineReorder`/`resolveAgainstPendingCreate` now tag every queue entry they write with a `listId` (and, for an item-scoped write, an `itemId`), resolved by the new `resolveQueueTargetIds()` *before* `applyOptimisticEdit()` runs (a queued `DELETE`'s optimistic apply removes the row from the mirror, so it has to be looked up first). A new `broadcastQueueState(syncing, pendingOverride?)` posts a `{ type: 'trakka-sync-status', pending, syncing }` message to every open tab after every enqueue/cancel and at the start/end of `flushQueue()` — guarded behind a `hadEntries` flag so it doesn't fire on `flushQueue()`'s own pre-existing opportunistic per-fetch call once the queue is already empty (it runs on *every* same-origin fetch while online; without the guard, an ordinary fully-synced session would broadcast — and trigger a repaint — on every API call). `notifyClients()` (`sw.js`) grew a `payload` parameter (defaulting to the pre-existing `{ type: 'trakka-sync-complete' }` shape) so both message types share one sender.

  `static/js/app.js`'s `handleSyncStatusMessage()` (new, wired into the existing `navigator.serviceWorker.addEventListener('message', ...)` listener in `registerServiceWorker()`) is the single place that turns those messages into: (1) the header's `#pending-badge` cycling through `idle`/`pending` (☁️⏳, amber, pulsing)/`syncing` (🔄, spinning)/`synced` (☁️✓, shown 2.5s then auto-hidden) via `setSyncIndicatorState()` — Tailwind's built-in `animate-spin`/`animate-pulse` utilities provide the motion, no custom CSS was needed; (2) the public `trakka:sync-pending`/`trakka:sync-complete` `window` `CustomEvent`s (`detail: { count, syncing }` / `detail: {}`) the request asked for; and (3) a network-free repaint of whatever's on screen (`repaintPendingChangeIndicators()`, calling `list_view.js`'s `renderItems()` or this file's own `renderDashboardFromCache()`) so a list's/item's dot updates immediately rather than waiting for that view's next full network refresh. `refreshPendingChangeIndicators()` rebuilds two `Set`s, `pendingListIds`/`pendingItemIds`, from one read of the queue; `hasPendingListChanges(id)`/`hasPendingItemChanges(id)` are what `buildListCard` (a new "⏳ Non synchronisé" badge in its `typeRow`, alongside the existing 👥/📌 ones), `list_view.js`'s `renderItems()` (a new `#list-sync-indicator` badge next to `#items-heading`) and `buildSecondaryRow` (a new `buildUnsyncedItemBadge`, shown whenever `hasPendingItemChanges(item.id)` or the pre-existing `isOfflineQueuedItem(item)` is true) all consult. Separately, `apiRequest()` now checks every response for the `X-Trakka-Queued: true` header `queueOfflineWrite`/`resolveAgainstPendingCreate` already set on every response they return (previously unread anywhere) and, when present, awaits `refreshPendingChangeIndicators()` *before* returning — this is what makes the dot for the exact list/item an action just affected already correct by the time that action's own success path re-renders, rather than only catching up once the service worker's `postMessage` round-trip arrives a moment later. `refreshPendingBadge()` (the pre-existing entry point, still called from several ordinary-mutation success paths across `app.js`/`list_view.js`/`urgent.js`/`planning.js`) now defers to `handleSyncStatusMessage`'s transitions whenever one is already showing (`syncing`/`synced`), so it can't cut a spin or a confirmation short. `hydrateFromCache()` also now calls `refreshPendingChangeIndicators()` before its first render, so a leftover, never-flushed queue from a previous session already shows its dots on the very first paint rather than only once something next changes. New `header.syncing`/`header.synced` and a new `sync.{listBadgeLabel,listBadgeAriaLabel,itemBadgeAriaLabel}` section added to both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json` (key parity and `{placeholder}` consistency verified with a throwaway Go program, the same technique the "dynamic JS-built strings" fix above used since no `node`/`python` was available). `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v44` for the content changes to `app.js`/`list_view.js`/`index.html`/`sw.js`/both locale files.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), brace/paren balance checks on every edited JS file, a throwaway Go program parsing `index.html` with `golang.org/x/net/html` to confirm it's still well-formed (no `node`/`python` available in the sandbox — same limitation as most frontend-only work in this log) plus a duplicate-`id` attribute scan, and the locale JSON validity/key-parity/placeholder-consistency check mentioned above. **Not yet done**: no browser verification at all of any of the three pieces (the header badge's four-state animation, a list card's/detail header's/item row's dot appearing and clearing in step with real offline actions, or the `trakka:sync-pending`/`trakka:sync-complete` events actually firing) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: go offline, add/edit/delete/reorder/check off an item or a list, and confirm (a) the header badge shows ☁️⏳ with the right count immediately, (b) the affected list's dashboard/Espaces card and its detail header (if open) show the "⏳ Non synchronisé" badge, and the specific item's row shows its own badge, (c) reconnecting shows a brief 🔄 spin then a ☁️✓ confirmation that fades back to "En ligne", and the dots clear on every affected list/item without a reload; also confirm via the browser console that `window.addEventListener('trakka:sync-pending', ...)`/`'trakka:sync-complete'` actually fire with the expected `detail`.

- **Web Push notifications**: full design in the dedicated "Web Push notifications" bullet in the relevant section in .claude/backend.md (or .claude/architecture.md for package-boundary/migration-engine topics) — this entry just records what was built and how it was verified. New: `internal/webpush` (VAPID + `aes128gcm` encryption + delivery, hand-rolled per the "standard library wherever possible" convention), `internal/db/migrations/0015_push_notifications.sql` (`push_subscriptions` table; `items.recurrence_lead_minutes`/`items.due_reminder_sent_for`), `internal/db/push_subscriptions.go`, `internal/db/notify_recipients.go` (`ListNotificationRecipients`), `internal/db/recurring_notify.go` (`ListItemsForRecurringNotifyScan`/`MarkRecurringReminderSent`), `internal/handlers/push.go` (`GET /api/v1/push/vapid-public-key`, `POST`/`DELETE /api/v1/push/subscribe`, `notifyListChange`, `RunRecurringDueScan`), `static/js/push.js` (the settings-modal opt-in toggle), and the `push`/`notificationclick` listeners in `static/sw.js`. Changed: `internal/config` (`VAPID_*`/`NOTIF_RECURRING_*` env vars, the `d`-suffix duration parser), `internal/models.Item` (`RecurrenceLeadMinutes`) and `internal/models.PushSubscription` (new), `internal/db/items.go` (`CreateItem`/`UpdateItem` both gained a `recurrenceLeadMinutes *int` parameter — every call site across the codebase, including every pre-existing test, was updated), `internal/handlers/items.go` (threading `recurrence_lead_minutes` through create/update/patch, and the two `notifyListChange` call sites — item creation, and a `done` `false→true` transition captured *before* `applyRecurrenceCompletion` can flip it back for a recurring item), `internal/handlers/app.go` (three new routes), `cmd/server/main.go` (`runRecurringNotifyScanLoop`, the `-generate-vapid-keys` flag), `static/index.html` (the toggle's markup, `<script src="/js/push.js">`), `static/js/app.js` (`handleDeepLinkOrRestore`/`handleNotificationClickMessage`, wired into `init()` and the service worker message listener respectively), `static/js/settings.js` (`refreshPushToggleUI()` called every time the settings modal opens), both `static/locales/*.json` (five new `modals.userSettings.push*` keys), and [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#push-notifications)/[docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#push_subscriptions).

  Verified: `internal/webpush/webpush_test.go`'s `TestEncryptPayloadMatchesRFC8291Example` matches RFC 8291 Appendix A's own published test vector byte-for-byte (every intermediate HKDF value and the final ciphertext), plus VAPID JWT sign/self-verify and validation-edge-case tests; `internal/db` tests for the subscription upsert/batch lookup, the three-source recipient `UNION` (excluding the acting user), and the recurring-scan query's filter and "re-arms on due_date change" behavior; `internal/handlers` tests for the vapid-public-key response shape (enabled/disabled), subscribe validation (missing/non-https endpoint, missing keys, not-configured `503`), unsubscribe idempotency, and `sendToUsers`' no-op paths (no subscriptions; push disabled) — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean across the whole repo. Frontend: brace/paren balance checks on every edited/new JS file, a throwaway Go program confirming `index.html` still parses cleanly with no duplicate ids, and the same JSON-validity/key-parity/placeholder-consistency check this log's other i18n entries used, since no `node`/`python` was available in the sandbox. **Not yet done**: never exercised against a real browser or a real push service — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in (same caveat as nearly every frontend-touching feature in this log), and the async delivery path specifically can't be integration-tested even with tooling, since `webpush.Send`'s own SSRF guard correctly refuses to dial a local `httptest` server's loopback address (see the Architecture bullet for why). Before relying on it: configure `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`/`VAPID_SUBJECT` (via `trakka -generate-vapid-keys`), open the app in a real browser, enable the toggle in Paramètres and grant the permission prompt, then from a second account/session add or check off an item in a list shared with the first account and confirm a real system notification arrives and clicking it opens/focuses Trakka on the right list; separately, set a recurring item's `due_date` within its lead time and confirm the periodic scan (or a shortened `NOTIF_RECURRING_SCAN_INTERVAL_MINUTES` for testing) delivers a reminder.

- **Fix: gitleaks blocked the Web Push notifications commit on 3 false-positive "leaks", all in `internal/webpush/webpush_test.go`**: attempting to commit the feature above tripped the local pre-commit hook (`gitleaks protect --staged`, mirroring the `security-scan` job's `gitleaks detect` in CI). Installed `gitleaks v8.28.0` (the version pinned in `ci.yml`/`docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`) and ran it directly to get the exact findings rather than guessing: all three were the `generic-api-key` rule firing on `rfcASPrivateKeyB64` (line 27), `rfcAuthSecretB64` (line 29), and `rfcECDHSecretB64` (line 31) — three of the named constants in `TestEncryptPayloadMatchesRFC8291Example`'s RFC 8291 Appendix A test-vector block (see the "Web Push notifications" bullet above). These are **not real secrets**: they're RFC 8291's own published example values, fixed by the RFC's authors specifically so any implementation can verify its output byte-for-byte, and no live VAPID identity, session, or subscription this app has ever used is exposed by them — gitleaks flagged them purely because their Go identifier names contain `...PrivateKey`/`...Secret` and their values are high-entropy base64. Confirmed by a full audit of every other file changed in this branch (`internal/config`, `cmd/server/main.go`, `compose.yml`, docs, `static/js/push.js`) that the actual VAPID key material is never hardcoded anywhere: `internal/config.Load()` already reads `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`/`VAPID_SUBJECT` exclusively from the environment (all-or-nothing, see `Validate()`), and the only way a real key pair is ever produced is `internal/webpush.GenerateVAPIDKeys()`/`trakka -generate-vapid-keys` at deploy time — this was already correct before this fix and needed no code change.

  Fixed by marking exactly the three flagged lines `// gitleaks:allow` with a one-line justification each, the same narrowly-scoped, documented-false-positive convention .claude/ci-security.md already documents for gosec's `#nosec G101`/`#nosec G124` findings (see "Running the same checks locally" above) — chosen over deleting the test (this RFC-conformance check is, per its own doc comment, "the strongest correctness signal available for this code short of a live push service") and over a blanket `.gitleaks.toml` path exclusion (which would also blind the scanner to a real secret accidentally pasted into the same file later). Also hardened the repo against this class of accident going forward, independent of the false positive itself: `.gitignore` now excludes `.env`/`.env.*` (with `.env.example` explicitly un-ignored), `*.pem`, and `*.key`; a new `.env.example` documents every `internal/config` env var (including `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`/`VAPID_SUBJECT`) with placeholder, non-functional values for local `go run`/`docker compose --env-file` use. A fresh real VAPID key pair was generated via `trakka -generate-vapid-keys` and written to a local, gitignored `.env` (not committed) purely so local Web Push testing has a working identity to use — there was nothing to "rotate" in the security-incident sense, since the flagged strings were never this app's own credential to begin with. Verified: `gitleaks protect --staged --redact` (the exact pre-commit-hook invocation) now reports zero leaks, `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`go mod verify`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race -cover ./...` all clean (including `TestEncryptPayloadMatchesRFC8291Example` itself, unaffected by the added trailing comments).

  **Deliberately not done**: did not add `godotenv`/any `.env`-file-reading code to the Go binary itself, and did not add "auto-generate a VAPID key pair in memory at startup if unset" behavior — both were suggested alongside this fix but both would cut against this app's own documented design (see "Config is env-var only" above): `PushEnabled()`'s all-or-nothing gate deliberately treats "no VAPID vars set" as "push disabled", not an error to work around, and a browser's `PushManager.subscribe()` ties a subscription to a specific `applicationServerKey` — silently minting a new key pair on every restart would invalidate every existing subscriber's registration and throw `InvalidStateError` on their next subscribe attempt. `trakka -generate-vapid-keys` (already existed) remains the one sanctioned way to get a pair, once, into whatever env-var mechanism (`.env`, compose, a secrets manager) the deployment already uses.

- **Fix: `gosec ./...` findings from the Web Push notifications feature, plus SARIF-upload robustness in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: the `security-scan` job's `gosec` step (see .claude/ci-security.md's "CI/CD & Sécurité" section) had never actually been run against the Web Push code before, since gosec's own CI gate only started failing once that feature landed. Installed `gosec v2.29.0` (the version pinned in `ci.yml`) and ran it directly: exactly three findings, all newly reachable through `internal/webpush`/`internal/handlers/push.go`/`internal/db/push_subscriptions.go`, all verified false-positives-or-worth-hardening rather than blanket-silenced:
  - **G115** (`internal/webpush/encrypt.go`, `byte(len(asPub))`) — a false positive: `asPub` is `asPriv.PublicKey().Bytes()` from an `ecdh.P256().GenerateKey()` call a few lines above, always exactly 65 bytes (the fixed uncompressed-point size for that curve) and never derived from anything user-controlled, so this conversion can never actually overflow a byte. Marked `#nosec G115` with that reasoning rather than adding a dead "if len > 255" branch the app's own "don't validate what can't happen" convention would reject.
  - **G201** (`internal/db/push_subscriptions.go`, `ListPushSubscriptionsForUsers`'s `fmt.Sprintf`-built `IN (%s)` clause) — also a false positive, and already explained by the function's own pre-existing doc comment: only the placeholder count (structural, derived from `len(userIDs)`) is interpolated into the SQL text, every actual id is still bound as a `?` parameter. Marked `#nosec G201` referencing that same doc comment.
  - **G118** (`internal/handlers/push.go`, `sendToUsers`'s per-subscription goroutine) — flagged because the goroutine calls `context.Background()` (to delete a gone subscription) instead of reusing the function's own `ctx` parameter. This one was a genuine, if minor, gap worth actually fixing rather than just silencing: the delete used a fully *unbounded* `context.Background()` with no timeout at all, deliberately avoiding `ctx` because `ctx` may already be at or past `sendToUsers`' own `pushSendTimeout` (30s) deadline by the time an individual delivery's cleanup runs — but "unbounded" wasn't the right alternative to "reuse a possibly-expired context" either. Fixed by giving the cleanup its own short `subscriptionCleanupTimeout` (5s) derived from `context.Background()`, so it's bounded without being tied to the batch's own deadline; gosec still flags the literal `context.Background()` call regardless of the wrapping, so a `#nosec G118` documents why reusing `ctx` isn't the fix here.

  Separately, hardened all four `github/codeql-action/upload-sarif` steps in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (gosec, Trivy filesystem, gitleaks, Trivy image) against a report file simply not existing — e.g. if the underlying scan tool itself crashes before writing one (Trivy failing to pull its vulnerability DB is a well-known real-world case of this), rather than running cleanly and just finding an issue, which is the *only* case those steps' pre-existing `if: always()` was actually written to handle. Each upload step's `if:` now also checks `hashFiles('<report>.sarif') != ''`, so a missing report skips the upload cleanly instead of failing on "Path does not exist" and cascading into a second, unrelated-looking job failure; each also gained `continue-on-error: true` as a second line of defense so a transient upload failure (a GitHub API hiccup, a permissions issue) can't fail the whole job either — the underlying scan tool's own exit code, not this upload step, is what's supposed to gate the pipeline on a genuine finding. The Trivy/gitleaks steps' own `format`/`report-format`+`report-path` flags that actually produce these SARIF files in the first place were already correctly set before this session (confirmed by re-reading the workflow, not assumed) and needed no change.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean; `gosec -fmt=sarif -out=gosec-results.sarif ./...` (the exact `security-scan` job invocation) now exits `0` with zero issues and a well-formed SARIF file; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` re-validated as well-formed YAML after editing. **Not yet done**: same as every other CI/CD entry in this log — the workflow itself has never actually run on GitHub's runners (see "What's left" below), so the SARIF-upload robustness fix is verified by re-reading the YAML's logic, not by observing a real Trivy-DB-download failure actually get handled gracefully in a live run.

- **Loading skeletons & stale-while-revalidate view loaders (mobile blank-screen fix)**: a reported multi-second blank screen at launch/view-change on mobile, root-caused to two independent things rather than one. First: `/js/tailwind.js` (the vendored Play CDN runtime, ~400KB — see its own header comment for why it's vendored rather than loaded from the CDN) was a plain, non-`defer`red `<script>` in `<head>` in both `static/index.html` and `templates/login.html`, directly before `<body>` even begins parsing — on a slow mobile connection or a low-end phone's CPU (Tailwind's runtime does real work: a JIT compiler scanning the whole DOM's class attributes and generating CSS on the fly), this alone can block the very first paint for seconds, and it's genuinely blank rather than just unstyled because nothing after it in `<head>`/`<body>` gets parsed until it finishes. Second: three of the five dashboard tabs (`loadUrgentView`/`loadPlanningView`/`loadSpacesView` in `urgent.js`/`planning.js`/`spaces.js`) and `list_view.js`'s `selectList` (opening a list) were network-first with no proactive cache paint — each first-ever tab open in a session did a full `GET /lists?house_id=` + per-list fan-out round trip against a container that started out completely empty, before rendering anything at all; `app.js`'s `loadDashboard()` had the same gap on every call *after* the initial boot (a house switch, returning from a list, clicking back to "Listes"), even though the dashboard's own `hydrateFromCache()`/`renderDashboardFromCache()` already existed and made the very first paint of a session fine.

  Fixed with four independent pieces, none of which touches the service worker's actual caching *strategy* (see below for why): (1) `defer` added to every `<script>` tag in both files except `theme-init.js` (which must stay synchronous/blocking — it sets the anti-FOUC `data-theme` attribute before any stylesheet is parsed) — this lets the parser reach `<body>`'s real HTML immediately instead of stalling on ~750KB of JS first; `defer` preserves every script's existing document-order execution guarantee (several files, sharing one global scope, already depend on this — see the new comment above the tail `<script>` block in `index.html`), so this is a load-timing change only, not a behavior change. (2) A new `html, body { background-color: var(--tk-bg); color: var(--tk-text); }` rule at the top of `static/css/base.css` — reusing the same `--tk-bg`/`--tk-text` tokens `theme-init.js`'s synchronous `data-theme` attribute already drives, so the correct theme's colors apply from the very first paint (no white/black-default flash) even before Tailwind's own utility classes exist; Tailwind's class selectors (higher specificity) take over seamlessly once `tailwind.js` finishes, with no visible change, since the token values were already chosen to match Tailwind's own `bg-white`/`dark:bg-slate-900`/`text-slate-900` palette. (3) A small `.tk-skeleton`/`.tk-skeleton-card`/`.tk-skeleton-row`/`.tk-skeleton-block` shimmer system (also in `base.css`, `prefers-reduced-motion`-aware) — placed as real, static `aria-hidden="true"` markup directly inside `index.html`'s `#shopping-lists`/`#todo-lists`/`#custom-lists`/`#planning-months`/`#urgent-items`/`#spaces-list`/`#shared-list` containers, needing zero JS to remove: every one of these containers' real render function already does an unconditional `container.replaceChildren()` (`renderGrid`, `renderPlanning`, `renderUrgent`, `renderSpaces`, `renderSharedList`) the first time it runs, which silently wipes the skeleton the moment real content is ready. `list_view.js` gained an equivalent `renderItemsSkeleton()` for the item-list detail view (which doesn't exist in the DOM before a list is opened, so it can't be pre-seeded statically), called by `selectList` only when there's nothing cached to paint instead. (4) `loadDashboard`/`loadUrgentView`/`loadPlanningView`/`loadSpacesView`/`selectList` were all changed to paint immediately from the IndexedDB mirror (`cachedDashboardLists`/`cachedListDetail`, both pre-existing helpers) *before* the network fetch even starts, not only as a fallback after it fails — true stale-while-revalidate at the call-site level: real (if possibly slightly stale) content appears instantly on a repeat view, falling through to the shimmer skeleton only when there's genuinely nothing cached yet (a cold session, or a list/tab never opened on this device before). `loadSharedView` (`shares.js`) was deliberately left as-is — `?shared_with_me=true` has no offline mirror by design (see docs/PWA.md), so there's no cache to paint from; it still gets the static skeleton markup in its container for the cold-start case. This was a deliberate choice over changing `handleApiRead`'s strategy in `sw.js` itself (still network-first, unchanged) — a mutation response needs to reflect a live RBAC/price/quantity check, never a stale cache, so true SWR was implemented one layer up, at each view's own already-existing cache helpers, rather than widening what the SW itself treats as cacheable; `sw.js`'s API-handling comment block now explains this split. `list_view.js`'s `buildItemThumbnail` (scraped product images) already had `loading="lazy"`; added `decoding="async"` too so a thumbnail's image decode never blocks the main thread. Emoji glyphs (used throughout for badges/icons) need no "compression" — they're plain Unicode text rendered by the system font, not image assets. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v46` for the content changes to `index.html`/`base.css`/`app.js`/`list_view.js`/`urgent.js`/`planning.js`/`spaces.js`/both locale files (a new `items.loadingHeading` key). Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a throwaway Go program parsing `static/index.html` with `golang.org/x/net/html` confirming it's still well-formed with no duplicate ids, a JSON-validity + key-parity check on both locale files (280/280, zero missing either direction), and brace/paren balance checks on every edited JS/CSS file — no `node`/`python` was available in the sandbox this was built in to run a real syntax check, same limitation noted elsewhere in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser or real-device verification at all — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: on an actual mobile device (or at minimum Chrome DevTools' network throttling set to a slow 3G/4G profile plus 4-6x CPU slowdown), confirm first paint of the header/nav/skeleton cards happens well under a second on a cold load of both `/` and `/auth/login`, confirm there's no visible flash of the wrong theme's colors before Tailwind's stylesheet injects, confirm each dashboard tab's skeleton→real-content transition looks reasonable rather than jarring, and confirm opening a previously-seen list now shows its (possibly momentarily stale, then refreshed) content instantly instead of a shimmer.

- **Price drop alerts (per-item target price)**: an item can carry a user-set `target_price` (`REAL`, nullable) and `alert_on_price_drop` (`INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`) — migration `internal/db/migrations/0016_target_price_alerts.sql`, added to `models.Item`, `internal/db.CreateItem`/`UpdateItem` (two more positional parameters, every call site across the codebase and its tests updated), and accepted by `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH /api/v1/items` (see [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#price-drop-alerts)). Deliberately a *separate* feature from the pre-existing "Price alerts / deal notifications" (`price_alerts` table, the periodic scan comparing an item's price against a *lower price found on its page*) rather than an extension of it — this one compares against a number the user themselves chooses, and notifies unconditionally the moment `price` reaches it, with no accept/reject workflow.

  `internal/handlers.priceAlertCondition`/`checkPriceDropAlert` (`internal/handlers/price_target_alerts.go`) is the single entry point every price-mutating code path calls: `handleItemsCreate`/`Update`/`Patch` (`items.go`, checked against the manually-supplied price only, *before* `scrapeProductInfo` runs), the background scraper's initial price fill-in (`scrapeProductInfo`'s own goroutine in `scrape.go`, both when it resolves within the request's bounded wait and when it lands fully asynchronously after the response is already gone), and accepting an auto-detected `price_alerts` row (`handlePriceAlertsUpdate` in `price_alerts.go`). Firing is a **false→true transition**, not a level check re-evaluated on every request — every caller computes `wasActive := priceAlertCondition(...)` from the item's state *before* that request's changes apply (the same pattern `justCompleted`/`previousDone` already use for recurring-item completion), so a price that stays under the threshold across several unrelated edits only ever notifies once, and re-raising the threshold above the current price and back down again correctly re-arms it. On a transition it sets `item.PriceAlertTriggered` (`models.Item`, a transient response-only field, the `target_price` counterpart to `PriceStatus`) so the frontend can show an immediate in-app toast, and fires a push notification to every user with access to the item's list (`db.ListNotificationRecipients`, no actor excluded — a price drop isn't the result of any one person's action even when a manual edit is what triggered the check) via the same `sendToUsers`/`pushPayload` `notifyListChange` already uses.

  A real risk during design: the scraper's own background goroutine (which does the actual `UpdateItemPriceIfMissing` DB write, unconditionally, regardless of whether the request's synchronous wait ends up "catching" the result in time or times out) and the handler's own post-scrape check could easily have double-fired — one push from each — for the common case of a brand-new item whose price the scraper resolves within the ~2.5s bounded wait. Avoided by splitting responsibility cleanly: the handler only ever checks the *manually-supplied* price, before `scrapeProductInfo` is called at all; a scraped price is checked exactly once, inside the goroutine itself (which is what makes the fully-async case — the scraper finishing after the response has already gone out — notifiable in the first place, since nothing else ever sees that update). The handler still needs the *toast* to show up on a response whose price the scraper happened to resolve synchronously, so it separately (and without re-sending a push) sets `PriceAlertTriggered` on the outgoing item after the scrape, whenever the price went from absent to present-and-under-threshold during that exact call.

  Verified: `internal/db/items_test.go`'s `TestTargetPriceAlertPersistence` (create/update/clear round-trips), a pure-function test (`TestPriceAlertConditionPure`) and three handler-level tests calling `handleItemsCreate`/`Update`/`Patch` directly (`internal/handlers/price_target_alerts_test.go`) confirming the false→true transition semantics end-to-end — including that a repeat update still under the same threshold, or an unrelated field edit, does *not* re-trigger — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean. Frontend: a single "Déclencher une alerte si le prix descend en dessous de (€)" number input (no separate opt-in checkbox — typing a value implicitly sets `alert_on_price_drop: true` client-side, clearing it clears both together) added to both the quick-add advanced panel and the edit-item modal (gated by the same `data-item-field="price"` visibility group the price field itself uses, since a target price is meaningless wherever price itself isn't shown), a highlighted amber 🔥 "Bonne affaire" badge (`buildPriceAlertBadge`, `static/js/list_view.js`) shown on an item's row whenever `priceAlertCondition` holds (a JS mirror of the Go function of the same name), and a `TrakkaToast.success` confirmation (`notifyPriceAlertIfTriggered`) fired from both the create-item and edit-item form submit handlers whenever the response carries `price_alert_triggered: true`. `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v47` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`list_view.js`/both locale files — no other `sw.js` changes were needed, since `target_price`/`alert_on_price_drop` are plain fields already covered by the service worker's generic `{...existing, ...body}` optimistic-edit spread, the same as `is_urgent`/`icon`. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of the frontend (the input, the badge, the toast) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: set a target price on an item whose current price already qualifies and confirm the badge/toast/push all fire on save; lower an existing item's price below its own already-set threshold and confirm the same; confirm a *second* save that leaves the price still under the same threshold does *not* re-toast; and — the one path that couldn't be exercised at all without a real subscribed browser and a real product page — confirm the background scraper filling in a price that happens to already qualify (an item created with just a URL and a pre-set threshold, whose price the scraper resolves after the response has already gone out) still delivers a push notification with no toast possible for that specific request.

- **Target-price background scan**: the "Price drop alerts" feature above only ever checked `alert_on_price_drop`/`target_price` at *request* time (creating/editing an item, or the scraper's own in-flight/async price fill-in) — nothing re-scraped a tracked item that nobody happened to touch, so a price drop on an item left alone could go undetected indefinitely. `internal/handlers.RunTargetPriceScan` (`internal/handlers/price_target_scan.go`) closes that gap: a periodic worker, wired to a ticker in `cmd/server/main.go` (`runTargetPriceScanLoop`, mirroring `runPriceAlertScanLoop`'s "run once immediately, then on `SCRAPE_INTERVAL`" shape, default `12h`, `<= 0` disables it — see the Config bullet above), that re-scrapes every eligible item (`db.ListItemsForTargetPriceScan`: not done, has a `url`, `alert_on_price_drop = 1`, `target_price` set — deliberately *not* requiring an existing `price`, unlike `ListItemsForPriceScan`, since a freshly threshold-tagged item may not have one yet and this scan's job is exactly to go find it) via the existing `internal/scraper.FetchProductInfo`. A fixed `targetPriceScanDelay` (5s, via a cancelable `sleepOrCanceled` helper rather than a raw `time.Sleep`, so a slow multi-item scan still responds to shutdown promptly) is paused between each item's fetch — deliberate rate limiting so a house tracking many items can't burst-hammer a merchant's site (Amazon in particular) from this server's single IP; not yet exposed as its own env var, per the request that asked for this. Applying a newly-found price goes through a new DB method, `db.UpdateItemPriceFromScan`, rather than `UpdateItemPriceIfMissing` (which only ever fires once, on a still-`NULL` price) or a plain `UpdateItem` (which would blindly clobber whatever's there): it's a compare-and-swap, guarded on `WHERE ... AND url = ? AND price IS ?` using SQLite's NULL-safe `IS` operator to handle both "was unset" and "was some float" with one statement — the price/url the scan read at the start of that item's check must still match, or the update is silently skipped (a lost race, a deleted item, or a since-changed url are all "nothing to do", the same contract every other scraper-driven write in this codebase already follows). When the compare-and-swap does apply, the worker reuses `checkPriceDropAlert`/`priceAlertCondition` exactly as every other price-mutating path does — `wasActive` is computed from the item's pre-scan state, so this is a false→true transition, not a level check, and firing sends the same push notification a manual edit would; there's simply no HTTP response for this path to attach an in-app toast to. Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#price-drop-alerts) and [docs/DEPLOYMENT.md](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md). Verified: `internal/db/target_price_scan_test.go` (`TestListItemsForTargetPriceScan`'s eligibility filter — url/threshold/opt-in/done/custom-list exclusions — and `TestUpdateItemPriceFromScan`'s compare-and-swap: applies from both a nil and a non-nil old price, and is correctly rejected when the price or the url has changed since it was read, or the item was deleted) — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean across the whole repo. **Not yet done**: no live/browser verification — the worker's own network fetch goes through `internal/scraper`'s SSRF-guarded `http.Client`, which (like every other scraper/webpush test in this codebase) correctly refuses to dial a local `httptest` server's loopback address, so an end-to-end integration test isn't feasible in this sandbox; coverage instead leans on the two new DB-layer tests plus the already-verified `priceAlertCondition`/`checkPriceDropAlert`/`scraper.FetchProductInfo` pieces it composes. Before relying on it: tag a real item with a `target_price` above its currently-listed real-world price, wait for (or manually trigger, e.g. by temporarily shortening `SCRAPE_INTERVAL`) a scan pass, and confirm the item's `price` updates and a push notification arrives with no request/toast involved; also confirm the 5s per-item delay is actually being honored (e.g. via timestamps in the `slog` output) on a house with several tracked items.

- **Header cleanup: Theme and Language moved into the "Paramètres" modal, plus a server-side per-account language preference**: the header previously carried two standalone dropdowns — a ☀️/🌙 theme button (`#theme-button`/`#theme-menu`) and an FR/EN language button (`#lang-button`/`#lang-menu`) — crowding an already-dense mobile header. Both are removed outright; `#user-settings-modal` (`static/index.html`) now opens with two new sections above the existing "keep last page" form, "Apparence" (`#user-settings-theme`, a `<select>` with the same light/dark/auto options the old menu offered) and "Langue" (`#user-settings-language`, `<select>` with fr/en), both wired in `static/js/settings.js` rather than in `theme.js`/`i18n.js` themselves — those two files now expose only `window.TrakkaTheme`/`window.TrakkaI18n`'s get/set API (their own open/close/click-outside/Escape menu-wiring code was deleted entirely, a genuine simplification, not just a relocation) for `settings.js` to call, the same way it already called into `push.js`'s toggle. `openUserSettingsModal()` re-syncs both selects' displayed value every time the modal opens (mirroring the existing keep-last-page/push-toggle pattern), and each select's own `change` listener applies the choice immediately — `TrakkaTheme.set()` is purely local (`localStorage`, no server round-trip); the language `<select>` additionally best-effort `PATCH`es `/api/v1/me` after applying the language client-side, so switching languages still works instantly even if that request fails (e.g. offline) — see below for why the response is only adopted when it looks like a genuine user object, guarding against the pre-existing `queueOfflineWrite` quirk where an offline-queued `PATCH /api/v1/me` comes back as a bare `{queued: true}` placeholder rather than the real profile.

  Backend: `users.language` (`TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, migration `internal/db/migrations/0017_user_language_preference.sql`) is a new column on `models.User`, selected by both `GetUser` and `getUserWithCredentials` (`internal/db/users.go`) and settable via the new `UpdateUserLanguage` DB method. An empty column value means "no explicit preference" and is *not* baked in at `CreateUser` time — instead, `internal/handlers.resolveUserLanguage` fills in the instance-wide `DEFAULT_APP_LANGUAGE` env var (`internal/config`, default `en`, validated against the new `internal/validate.SupportedLanguages`/`Language()` helper — shared by both the env-var parsing and the handler's own request validation) on every response that carries a `User`, so a brand-new account, *and* any pre-existing account that never touched the Language section, both resolve to whatever that env var is currently set to — raising or changing it takes effect retroactively, exactly as requested, rather than only for accounts created after the change. `PATCH /api/v1/me` (`handleMeUpdate`, `internal/handlers/auth.go`) gained a second optional field, `language` (`"fr"`/`"en"` only, `400` otherwise), applied alongside the pre-existing `keep_last_page` — both follow the same "absent = untouched" convention and either, both, or neither may be present in one request. `static/js/app.js`'s `init()` reconciles the account's own server-side language against whatever `i18n.js`'s pre-auth guess (`navigator.language`/`localStorage`) already applied, once `/me` resolves — the same "server is authoritative once available" pattern `isKeepLastPageEnabled()` already uses for `keep_last_page` — which is what actually makes a brand-new account land in the instance's configured default language rather than the browser's own.

  Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#get-apiv1me) and [docs/DEPLOYMENT.md](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md). Verified: `internal/db/users_test.go`'s `TestUserLanguagePreference` (defaults to empty for a new user, `UpdateUserLanguage` round-trips through both `GetUser` and `GetUserByEmail`, `ErrNotFound` for a nonexistent user), `internal/handlers/me_test.go`'s `TestHandleMeResolvesDefaultLanguage`/`TestHandleMeUpdateSetsLanguage` (the default-resolution fallback, a valid `PATCH` persisting and being echoed back, and an invalid language rejected with `400` without touching the previously-persisted value), `internal/config/config_test.go`'s `TestDefaultAppLanguage` (unset/valid/invalid/case-insensitive env var handling) — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean across the whole repo — plus a JSON key-parity/validity check on both locale files after removing the now-dead `header.themeSwitcher`/`header.langSwitcher` aria-label keys and adding the four new `modals.userSettings.appearance*`/`language*` keys (still 290/290, zero missing either direction). **Not yet done**: no browser verification of any of this — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: open "Paramètres", confirm the Apparence/Langue selects reflect the current theme/language and that changing either applies instantly with no page reload; confirm the header no longer shows the old theme/language buttons at all, on both mobile and desktop widths; register a brand-new account with `DEFAULT_APP_LANGUAGE` set to `fr` (or left at its `en` default) and confirm the dashboard renders in that language on first load; and confirm a language choice made from the modal survives a reload (both via `localStorage` and, once `/me` resolves, the server-persisted value).

- **Mobile item-creation redesign: sticky bottom quick-add bar, replacing the old top form + FAB/sheet**: on mobile, item creation previously competed for two separate pieces of screen real estate — an always-inline quick-add form sitting at the *top* of the item list (pushing every item down, worst on a long list) and a floating blue `[+]` FAB hovering over the list's bottom-right corner that opened a near-duplicate copy of the same form as a bottom sheet. Both are gone. `#create-item-form-anchor` (`static/index.html`) is now a single, permanently-visible bar pinned to the bottom of the viewport below Tailwind's `md` breakpoint (`fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0` reverting to `md:static` — its original in-flow spot above the item list — at `md:` and up, so desktop is visually unchanged) holding the same text input, `[⚙️]` advanced-options toggle (quantity/price/target price/target month/recurrence/urgent — unchanged), and `[+]` submit button as before; the input's placeholder became "Ajouter un article ou une tâche…" (`items.titlePlaceholder`, both locales). No relocation logic is needed any more — previously `openAddItemSheet`/`closeAddItemSheet` (`static/js/list_view.js`) moved the one real `<form>` node between its inline slot and `#add-item-sheet`'s body via `appendChild`; both that sheet and the FAB button are deleted outright (`#add-item-fab`, `#add-item-sheet`, their `listEls` entries, the two now-dead i18n keys `items.addFabAriaLabel`/`addSheetTitle`), since a single element whose CSS `position` changes at a breakpoint needs nothing to relocate. `static/js/reorder.js` (which hid the FAB while reorder mode is active) had its two `listEls.addItemFab.hidden = ...` lines dropped — hiding `#create-item-form-anchor` itself already covers the bar on every viewport width. `static/css/base.css` gained the bar's `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` padding (`padding-bottom: max(12px, env(safe-area-inset-bottom))`, replacing the old `#add-item-fab` position rule, scoped inside the same `@media (max-width: 768px)` block the bar's own `md:static` switches at) and a matching `#items-section { padding-bottom: 90px }` in the same block, so the item list's last row can scroll clear above the now-permanently-fixed bar — needed because a `position: fixed` element, unlike the old in-flow top form, no longer pushes any content to make room for itself. `static/js/gestures.js`'s edge-swipe-back zone gained `#create-item-form-anchor` to its exclusion list (alongside `.swipe-item`/`.reorder-row`/`#dashboard-tabs`) so a tap landing on the bar's own input/buttons near the screen's left edge can't be hijacked into a back-navigation gesture. UX: submitting (tapping `[+]` or pressing Enter — the latter already native `<form>` behavior, no JS needed) resets the text field and collapses the advanced panel back to its default collapsed state, but — this was already true before, just simplified now that there's no sheet-close branch to choose between — keeps focus in the (now-empty) title input, so the mobile keyboard never dismisses and several items can be added back-to-back. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v49` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`base.css`/`list_view.js`/`reorder.js`/`gestures.js`/both locale files. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a throwaway Go program using `golang.org/x/net/html` confirming `index.html` still parses with no duplicate ids and that every removed id (`add-item-fab`, `add-item-sheet`, `add-item-sheet-body`, `close-add-item-sheet-button`, `add-item-sheet-title`) is genuinely gone, brace/paren/bracket balance checks on every edited JS file, and the same JSON-validity/key-parity/placeholder-consistency check this log's other i18n entries used (288/288 keys, zero mismatches either direction) — no `node`/`python` was available in the sandbox this was built in, same limitation noted elsewhere in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser or real-device verification at all — confirm on an actual phone (or Chrome DevTools' mobile emulation) that the bar stays pinned above the keyboard/gesture area when the input is focused, that the last item in a long list scrolls fully clear of the bar, that rapid back-to-back adds via Enter never drop the keyboard, and that the desktop (`md:` and up) layout is pixel-unchanged from before this session.

- **Fix: a genuinely offline device still surfaced a blocking "hors ligne" error banner on several views, despite the existing `isNetworkError`-based suppression architecture**: reported as "an offline toast/error still appears and interrupts the flow" — should never happen per the Offline-First design already described throughout this log. Root cause: `apiRequest` (`static/js/app.js`) only ever tags an `Error` `isNetworkError = true` when `fetch()` itself *throws* (no connectivity at all, or the synchronous `!navigator.onLine` short-circuit for a `GET`) — but `sw.js`'s `offlineReadFallback` answers a `GET` it has no explicit IndexedDB mirror for (`/api/v1/me`, `/api/v1/admin/settings`, `/api/v1/price-alerts`, `/api/v1/push/vapid-public-key`, a house's `/members`, a list's/category's `/share` roster, and the `/share/pin` toggle) with a genuine, *resolved* HTTP `503 {"error":"hors ligne"}` response instead — that never throws, so it skips `apiRequest`'s `catch` block (and its `isNetworkError` tag) entirely, silently defeating every one of the `if (!isNetworkError(err)) showError(...)` guards already sprinkled across `app.js`/`list_view.js`/`urgent.js`/`planning.js`/`spaces.js`/`shares.js` (several of which — `loadMembers`, `loadShareRoster` — already *had* this exact guard, and it simply never fired for this specific offline path). Separately, roughly two dozen other call sites (mostly optimistic-mutation `catch` blocks — `changeQuantity`/`toggleUrgent`/`toggleDone`/`removeItem`/the create/edit-item forms in `list_view.js`; `admin.js`'s settings load/save; `settings.js`'s keep-last-page/language `PATCH`es; `push.js`'s toggle; `notifications.js`'s `resolveAlert`; `reorder.js`'s `commitReorder`; several in `shares.js`/`spaces.js`/`urgent.js`/`planning.js`) called `showError(err.message)` completely unconditionally, with no `isNetworkError` guard at all — most of these are normally unreachable while offline since their underlying write is itself queued by `sw.js` and resolves as an ordinary `2xx`/`202`, but were still a real gap for the narrow edge case of a `fetch()`-level failure before a service worker is installed/controlling (e.g. the very first page load).

  Fixed with two changes. (1) `apiRequest`'s `!response.ok` branch now also checks `response.headers.get('X-Trakka-Offline') === 'true'` and tags the thrown `Error` `isNetworkError = true` when present — reusing the header `offlineReadFallback` already sets on *every* response it returns (mirroring the same trusted, SW-only, never-set-by-the-Go-backend pattern `X-Trakka-Queued` already uses for the write side, confirmed by grepping `internal/` for both header names). This makes every pre-existing `isNetworkError(err)` guard across the app actually work for this whole class of endpoint, with no per-call-site change needed. It deliberately does *not* fire for the unrelated, deliberate "this action requires connectivity" `503`s `queueOfflineWrite` returns for houses/admin-settings/shares/invitations (see those features' own bullets above) — those responses don't carry `X-Trakka-Offline`, so they still correctly surface as real, actionable errors telling the user the action needs a network connection, rather than being silently swallowed. (2) Every one of the ~24 previously-unconditional `showError(err.message)` call sites listed above was wrapped in the same established `if (!isNetworkError(err)) showError(err.message);` guard, for consistency and as defense-in-depth against the pre-SW-registration edge case. The header's own `503`/`404` responses for a list not found in the offline mirror (`offlineReadFallback`'s "liste introuvable (hors ligne)") are also covered by the same header, so opening a link to an uncached list while offline now silently falls back to the dashboard instead of flashing an error banner too — consistent with the same "only the header network badge communicates offline state" requirement. The header's `updateNetworkStatus`/`#network-dot`/`#network-label` badge and the sync-status indicator (`setSyncIndicatorState`, the ☁️⏳/🔄/☁️✓ cycle — see "Offline sync visual indicators" above) were already the sole ambient network-state UI and needed no changes. `list_view.js`'s three remaining unconditional `showError` calls (`items.linkCopyFailed`/`linkShareFailed` from the Clipboard/Web Share APIs, and the two client-side price/target-price validation messages) were deliberately left as-is — none of them originate from `apiRequest` or a network condition, so none of them are in scope for this fix and all three should keep showing. No `static/js/api.js` exists in this codebase (`apiRequest` lives in `app.js` and is shared, unmodularized, with every other frontend file — see "Frontend (PWA, `static/`)" in .claude/frontend-pwa.md) — this fix was applied there instead.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` all clean (no Go files touched — this is a pure frontend fix), a full re-grep confirming every `showError(err.message)`/`showError(message)` call site in every `static/js/*.js` file is now either guarded by `isNetworkError` or is one of the three deliberately-unguarded non-network cases in `list_view.js` just named, and a brace/paren/bracket balance check on every edited file (`app.js`, `list_view.js`, `urgent.js`, `planning.js`, `spaces.js`, `shares.js`, `push.js`, `notifications.js`, `admin.js`, `settings.js`, `reorder.js`) — no `node`/`python3` was available in the sandbox this was built in to run a real syntax check, same limitation noted elsewhere in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: go offline (DevTools' Network → Offline, or airplane mode) with the service worker already controlling the page, then exercise every one of the previously-broken paths — open "Membres de la Maison", the "Paramètres du Système" admin modal (as an admin), a Space/List's share modal, and toggle the push notification switch in "Paramètres" — and confirm each one fails silently (no blocking banner) rather than flashing "hors ligne"/"Erreur 503"; separately confirm a *genuine* functional error (e.g. a validation `400`, or a `403` from a read-only share) while online still shows its message as before.

- **Fix: dashboard/Espaces list-card price-total badge used a neutral gray style instead of the app's green money-positive accent**: reported live from a screenshot of the dashboard's "Achats" grid — the estimated-total pill on a card (e.g. "259,80 €") rendered in a flat gray (`bg-slate-200/60`/`text-[color:var(--tk-money-total)]`) while every other money-positive indicator in the app (the auto-detected-price sparkle icon, the all-items-done todo-progress badge, "Déjà dépensé" in a list's own finance summary) is green, so the card felt visually inconsistent at a glance. `static/js/app.js`'s `badge()` helper had a dedicated `neutral` palette entry used by exactly one call site — `urlBadges`'s price-total badge, shared by the dashboard grids, the Espaces tab, and the "Partagé avec moi" tab since all three render cards through the same `buildListCard`/`urlBadges` pair (confirmed by grep — no other file duplicates this rendering). Fixed by dropping the now-dead `neutral` palette entry outright and switching that one call site to the already-existing `emerald` palette (`bg-emerald-500/10 text-emerald-600 dark:text-emerald-300` — the same classes the auto-price sparkle icon and the all-done todo badge already use), with `font-semibold` added explicitly on top of `badge()`'s shared `font-medium` so the figure stays easy to spot at a glance, per the report's own ask. This is a deliberate, narrow exception to `--tk-money-total` (`static/css/tokens.css`), not a reversal of it: that token (and its `--tk-money-spent`/`--tk-money-remaining` siblings) still exclusively backs every *in-list* money figure — the finance summary, item rows, the budget-planning totals — where "Total estimé" specifically has to stay visually distinct from "Déjà dépensé"/"Reste à dépenser" within the same view; a dashboard/Espaces card only ever shows one number, so there's no such distinction to preserve there, and tokens.css's own doc comment was updated to say so. No new CSS variables were introduced and no dark-mode-specific rule was needed — Tailwind's `dark:` variant on the `emerald` classes already resolves against the same `[data-theme="dark"]` selector this app's whole theme system uses (see `static/js/tailwind-config.js`), the identical mechanism every other `badge()` palette already relies on. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v57` for the content-only change to already-cached `app.js`/`tokens.css`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a brace/paren balance check on the edited file, and a grep confirming no remaining reference to the removed `neutral` key anywhere in `static/js/*.js`/`static/css/*.css` — no `node`/`python` was available in the sandbox this was built in to run a real syntax check, same limitation noted elsewhere in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: open the dashboard (and the Espaces and "Partagé avec moi" tabs) in both Light and Dark mode and confirm every priced shopping/groceries/recurring-shopping card's total badge now renders as a green pastel pill with bold text, matching the color of the auto-price sparkle icon and the all-done todo badge elsewhere on screen.

- **Dashboard/Espaces card total moved server-side (`total_amount`), and made explicit that it's the list's full cumulative cost, not "reste à dépenser"**: requested to make sure the green price badge on a dashboard/Espaces/"Partagé avec moi" card reflects every priced item's `price * quantity`, done or not, rather than only what's left to buy — the client-side `urlBadges` calculation already summed every item unconditionally (no `done` filter was ever applied there), so this session's actual change is a refactor, not a behavior fix: the sum is now computed once in SQL rather than re-derived in JS from a per-card fetch of embedded items, so it can never drift from a slightly different client-side formula, and the field's own doc comments now say explicitly that it's the *total*, distinct from the list detail view's "Reste à dépenser" (which genuinely does exclude done items — see `updateFinanceSummary`/`lineTotal` in `list_view.js`, unchanged by this session). `internal/db/lists.go`'s `listSelect` (shared by `GetList`, `ListListsForUser`, and — via `scanListRow` — `ListPinnedHouseSpaceLists`) gained a correlated subquery, `total_amount`: `SUM(items.price * CASE WHEN items.quantity > 0 THEN items.quantity ELSE 1 END) FROM items WHERE items.list_id = lists.id AND items.price IS NOT NULL` — the same quantity-floor-at-1 defensiveness `static/js/list_view.js`'s `lineTotal` already uses, even though `internal/handlers/items.go` never actually persists a quantity ≤ 0. `SUM()` over zero matching rows returns SQL `NULL` for free, which is exactly the "no priced items at all" signal the new `models.List.TotalAmount *float64` (`json:"total_amount,omitempty"`) field's nil case relies on to tell the frontend whether to show the badge at all — present (including a genuine `0`) whenever at least one item has a price. `db.ListSharedListsForUser` (the hand-rolled query backing `?shared_with_me=true`) was deliberately *not* touched — it only ever returns list *stubs* used to know which ids to fetch full detail for (`GET /lists/{id}`, which does go through `listSelect`), so every one of the four card-rendering call sites (`static/js/app.js`'s `renderGrid`, `spaces.js`'s two grids, `shares.js`'s "Partagé avec moi" list) already gets `total_amount` "for free" via that per-list detail fetch, with no changes needed there. Frontend: `urlBadges`/`progressBadge` (`static/js/app.js`) now take the whole `list` object instead of just its `items` array (so `urlBadges` can read `list.total_amount` while still deriving its domain badges from `list.items`) — `noBadges` was already parameter-agnostic — and all four call sites (`renderGrid` in `app.js`, two in `spaces.js`, one in `shares.js`) were updated to pass `list` instead of `list.items || []`. Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md) (`GET /api/v1/lists` and `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}`); no `docs/DATABASE.md` change needed since this adds no column/migration, only a computed response field. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v59` for the content-only change to already-cached `app.js`/`spaces.js`/`shares.js`. Verified: a new `internal/db/lists_test.go` (`TestListTotalAmountIncludesDoneItems` — a list with no items has a nil `TotalAmount`; an unpriced item never contributes; a priced item with `quantity > 1` contributes `price * quantity`; and, the core regression case, marking that item `done` via `UpdateItem` leaves it still counted — checked through both `GetList` and `ListListsForUser`), `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean, and `node --check` on every edited JS file. **Not yet done**: no browser verification that the badge still renders correctly end to end (the domain badges alongside the total, the "no priced items → no badge at all" case, a repeat visit after checking an item off not changing the shown total) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. **Superseded by the very next entry below**: a follow-up request reversed this bullet's own "full cumulative cost, done or not" design in favor of the opposite — see "Dashboard/Espaces card total_amount now excludes done items" immediately below.

- **Dashboard/Espaces card `total_amount` now excludes done items ("reste à dépenser", reversing the previous session's own design)**: a direct follow-up request asked for the opposite of what the bullet above deliberately built — the dashboard/Espaces/"Partagé avec moi" card's green badge should show only what's left to spend (unchecked items), not the list's full lifetime cost. `internal/db/lists.go`'s `listSelect` subquery gained an `AND items.done = 0` condition: `SUM(items.price * CASE WHEN items.quantity > 0 THEN items.quantity ELSE 1 END) FROM items WHERE items.list_id = lists.id AND items.price IS NOT NULL AND items.done = 0`. This makes `total_amount` exactly the same figure the list detail view's own finance summary already shows as "Reste à dépenser" (`updateFinanceSummary`/`lineTotal` in `static/js/list_view.js`, unchanged — it was already correct, filtering to unchecked items itself) — just computed once in SQL for the card instead of re-derived per list from a full item fetch, so the two can never drift apart. `SUM()` over zero matching (unchecked, priced) rows still returns SQL `NULL`, so `models.List.TotalAmount`'s nil-means-"nothing to show" contract is unchanged; only which rows feed the sum changed. No frontend *code* change was needed — `static/js/app.js`'s `urlBadges` already just renders whatever `list.total_amount` the server sends — only its doc comments (and the one above `badge()`'s emerald-palette exception, since this card's figure is now literally "Reste à dépenser" rather than a distinct "Total estimé"-like figure) were updated to match, along with `models.List.TotalAmount`'s own doc comment and both `total_amount` descriptions in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md). `internal/db/lists_test.go`'s test was rewritten (`TestListTotalAmountIncludesDoneItems` → `TestListTotalAmountExcludesDoneItems`): the same three-item setup (an active priced item, a quantity-2 priced item, an unpriced item) now asserts marking the quantity-2 item done *excludes* it from the total (`want` dropped from `10 + 5*2` to just `10`), plus a new final step marking the one remaining priced item done too and asserting `TotalAmount` goes back to `nil` once every priced item in the list is done — the "SUM over zero matching rows → NULL" edge case specifically. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v60` for the comment-only content change to already-cached `app.js`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean (including the rewritten test), `node --check` on `app.js`. **Not yet done**: same as the superseded entry above — no browser verification that a dashboard/Espaces card's badge actually drops/shrinks the moment an item is checked off, and that a list with everything checked off shows no badge at all rather than a stale or zero one; no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in.

- **Offline consistency hardening: `Cache-Control: no-store` on every API JSON response, plus an explicit `cache: 'no-store'` on the frontend's own fetch call**: reported as a Dashboard-vs-list-detail-view inconsistency while offline (an item checked off would show correctly on the Dashboard but the list detail view would still show the pre-toggle, stale state). Investigated from scratch rather than assumed, since this app already carries an unusually deep, previously-built machinery for exactly this class of bug — worth recording what was actually found before describing the fix. `static/sw.js`'s `handleApiRead` never uses the Cache Storage API for `/api/v1/...` at all (`RUNTIME_CACHE` is declared but genuinely unused for API responses — only `handleShellRequest`, gated on non-API paths, ever calls `caches.match()`/`caches.put()`); it's strictly network-first, falling back to reading straight from the IndexedDB mirror (`offlineReadFallback`) only when the real `fetch()` fails or the backend answers with a 502/503/504. `static/js/list_view.js`'s `selectList`/`refreshCurrentList` already pre-paint from that exact same IndexedDB read (`cachedListDetail` → `db.js`'s `getListWithItems`) before the network call even starts, and fall back to it again on failure — and `app.js`'s `cachedDashboardLists` (backing the Dashboard) composes lists via that identical `getListWithItems` function against the identical `lists`/`items` IndexedDB stores, so the two views are structurally reading the same data, not two independently-stale copies. `list_view.js`'s `updateFinanceSummary`/`lineTotal` already recompute the budget summary (Total/Déjà dépensé/Reste à dépenser) live from `state.currentList.items` on every `renderItems()` call rather than trusting any cached/stale aggregate field, exactly matching what a fix for this class of bug would otherwise need to add. In short: nearly everything asked for was already built, in a prior session this log's own entries document at length (see "Loading skeletons & stale-while-revalidate view loaders" and the several "Fix: dashboard/tab counters stuck stale..." entries above).

  The one genuine, previously-unclosed gap: no code path anywhere in `internal/` ever set a `Cache-Control` header on a JSON API response. Without one, a `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}` carries no explicit freshness information at all — which browsers are free to interpret with their own heuristic HTTP caching, entirely independent of (and underneath) the service worker's own careful network-first design; the service worker's `fetch(request)` call inside `handleApiRead` is itself a normal `fetch()` subject to the browser's HTTP disk cache unless told otherwise. That's a real, if narrow, way for a `GET` to resolve to something other than a genuine network round trip or the IndexedDB-backed offline fallback, regardless of how well everything built on top of it behaves. Fixed at the single choke point every JSON response already funnels through, `writeJSON` (`internal/handlers/json.go`), which now sets `Cache-Control: no-store` unconditionally — see the "Response caching" bullet in .claude/backend.md's Security rules section, which future contributors should treat as a standing invariant the same way "Response encoding" already is. Paired with a frontend-side belt-and-suspenders change: `apiRequest` (`static/js/app.js`) now passes `cache: 'no-store'` on its own `fetch()` call, so the browser's HTTP cache is bypassed from the request side too, independent of what any past response's headers said. `static/sw.js` gained a short comment block reinforcing (not changing the behavior of) the pre-existing "no Cache Storage API for `/api/v1/...`" design, tying it to this new header guarantee for future readers; `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped for the content-only change to already-cached `app.js`/`sw.js`. `docs/API.md`'s "Security headers" bullet documents the new header.

  Note for the next session: at the time of this fix, `git status` already showed a substantial *uncommitted* work-in-progress on `static/js/{app,db,shares,spaces}.js`/`static/sw.js`/`docs/PWA.md` — a separate, unrelated feature (per-account IndexedDB mirror ownership/isolation for a shared/kiosk browser, plus dedicated stub mirrors for the `?shared_with_me=true`/`?pinned_house_spaces=true` queries) that predates this session and was left alone here (reviewed for correctness as part of this investigation, found structurally sound, but out of scope for this fix and not yet committed or verified end-to-end by whoever started it). Don't mistake it for part of this entry's own change.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean, `node --check` on every edited JS file (`node`/`python3` were both available in this sandbox, unlike most other frontend-only entries in this log). **Not yet done**: no browser verification that this closes the originally-reported symptom for good — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, and the root cause (browser HTTP-cache heuristics) is inherently hard to reproduce deterministically outside a real browser. If the exact symptom (Dashboard correct, list detail view stale, while genuinely offline) recurs after this fix, the next thing to check is whether the reporter's browser had already cached a stale `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}` response *before* this fix shipped (a pre-existing disk-cache entry `no-store` on future responses can't retroactively evict) — a hard reload (or clearing site data) would rule that out — and, failing that, to capture the actual `Response` object's `type`/headers in DevTools' Network panel for the exact failing request to confirm whether it's resolving from `(disk cache)`/`(memory cache)` at all before assuming this fix didn't address it.

- **Offline sync engine: fixed a "ghost item" bug (a permanently-rejected queued mutation silently discarded, leaving its IndexedDB mirror row orphaned forever) and a header-badge lag when editing an item still awaiting its own offline create**: reported as two symptoms — editing an item made while offline seemed to "erase" its pending-sync indicator, and items left in the local cache without ever reaching the server became invisible to the sync engine, never retried again. The report's own framing (a `static/js/sync.js` file, `_pending`/`client_id` fields, an `offline_sync_queue` store) doesn't match this codebase's actual architecture — there is no `sync.js`; the offline write queue and its replay logic live in `static/sw.js` (`queueOfflineWrite`/`flushQueue`/`resolveAgainstPendingCreate`), backed by `static/js/db.js`'s `STORE_QUEUE`, using `temp-list-*`/`temp-item-*` ids rather than a separate `_pending`/`client_id` pair — but investigating the actual code surfaced two real, confirmed bugs matching the reported symptoms, both in `static/sw.js`.

  **Bug 1 (the header badge, not the data itself)**: `resolveAgainstPendingCreate` — the function that folds an edit to a still-unsynced `temp-item-*`/`temp-list-*` object into its existing queued "create" entry rather than issuing a contradictory request against an id the server has never heard of — correctly preserved the entry's pending status (the DELETE branch already called `broadcastQueueState`, and every response it returns already carries the `X-Trakka-Queued: true` header `apiRequest`'s own per-call `refreshPendingChangeIndicators()` reacts to, so the per-item "⏳ Non synchronisé" dot was never actually wrong), but its non-DELETE (edit) branch never called `broadcastQueueState()` — so the header's own pending-count badge (`#pending-badge`, driven by the service worker's `postMessage`) could lag behind reality after editing a pending item, until some unrelated queue mutation happened to fire it. Fixed by adding the same `broadcastQueueState(false)` call the DELETE branch already had.

  **Bug 2 (the real "ghost" data-loss bug)**: `flushQueue`'s replay loop treated *any* resolved non-401 response as "done" — a genuine `2xx` success and a `404`/`400`/`403`/even a `5xx` server error were all unconditionally `dequeue()`d and counted as synced, with no distinction and no cleanup. For a queued **create** (a `temp-item-*`/`temp-list-*` entry) that the server permanently rejected — e.g. its parent list itself failed to sync first, or a validation error — this silently dropped the one queue entry that would ever have retried it, while leaving the object sitting in the IndexedDB mirror forever: present locally, absent on the server, and never touched again — exactly the "ghost" the report described. Worse, a transient `5xx` (a momentary backend hiccup) was treated identically to success, discarding a perfectly good pending edit that should have been retried. Fixed with `isDefinitiveClientError(status)` (true for 400-499 except 401/408/429 — statuses no amount of identical retrying can ever turn into a success) and `discardRejectedEntry(entry, status, queue)`: a `5xx`/unexpected status now stops the loop and leaves the entry (and everything queued after it) queued for the next attempt, exactly like a network failure already did; a definitive rejection of a still-pending **create** removes the orphaned local object (and, for a list, cascades to cancel any queued item-creates that were waiting on it and deletes their own local rows too — mirroring the cancellation logic `resolveAgainstPendingCreate`'s own DELETE branch already used for an explicit user cancellation); a `404` on an edit/delete of an **already-synced** resource deletes its now-nonexistent local mirror row; and any other definitive rejection of an edit (400/403/409) drops the queued mutation and re-fetches its parent list (`reconcileListFromServer`, reusing the existing `mirrorReadResponse`) to pull the mirror back in line with whatever the server's true state actually is, rather than leaving a change the server never applied silently stuck in the local cache.

  Also added `syncOnStartup()` (`static/js/app.js`, called from `init()` right after `hydrateFromCache()`, fire-and-forget) — waits on `navigator.serviceWorker.ready` and, if online, immediately posts `{ type: 'flush-queue' }` to the active worker, forcing an eager retry of anything still queued from a previous session rather than relying on an incidental fetch or the `online` event to eventually trigger one. This doesn't scan IndexedDB itself (the existing `flushQueue`/`getQueue` already is that scan — there's exactly one queue store, not scattered `_pending` flags to hunt across every item/list row) — it just guarantees that scan-and-retry actually runs promptly at boot instead of only opportunistically.

  `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v69` for the content changes to `sw.js`/`app.js`. Verified: `node --check` on every edited file, `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched — this is a pure frontend fix). **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: create an item offline, edit it (confirm the header badge and the item's own dot both stay showing "unsynced"), go back online and confirm it syncs and gets remapped to a real id; separately, queue an edit to a real item, have the server reject it with a definitive `4xx` (e.g. temporarily reject it server-side, or edit a list you then get removed from) and confirm the queue entry is dropped and the mirror reconciles rather than the edit being retried forever or the app going silent about it; and confirm a `5xx` (temporarily stop the backend mid-queue) leaves the entry queued and retries once the backend comes back, rather than being discarded.

- **Item labels + Compact/Detailed view mode**: an item can now carry a freeform, user-managed `labels` array (e.g. `"Bio"`, `"Promo"`) and each list detail view has a per-list-`type` Compact/Detailed display toggle. Backend: `items.labels` (`TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'`, a JSON-encoded string array — migration `internal/db/migrations/0018_item_labels.sql`), `models.Item.Labels` (never nil in a response — see its own doc comment), `internal/validate.Labels` (trims, strips control characters via the existing `Text` helper, drops empties, dedups case-insensitively keeping first-seen casing, caps at 20 labels of ≤30 characters each, `400` otherwise). Deliberately **not** folded into `CreateItem`/`UpdateItem`'s already-16-parameter signatures — `internal/db.SetItemLabels(ctx, id, labels)` is a separate method (the same "per-item concern doesn't need to ride along with every other field write" reasoning `ReorderItems` already established), called from `internal/handlers/items.go`'s `handleItemsCreate` (only when non-empty, since a fresh item already defaults to `[]`), `handleItemsUpdate`/PUT (always, full-replace semantics — an omitted `labels` resets it, matching `icon`/`custom_category_id`), and `handleItemsPatch`/PATCH (only when the field is present in the request, `*[]string` so absent is distinguishable from an explicit `[]`). `scanItem` and the two other `SELECT`-then-`scanItem` call sites outside `internal/db/items.go` (`ListItemsForPriceScan` in `price_alerts.go`, `ListItemsForTargetPriceScan` in `target_price_scan.go`) all had `labels` added to their column list, in step with `scanItem`'s own updated `Scan()` call. Documented in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#labels) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md).
