  Frontend, entirely in `static/js/list_view.js`/`static/index.html`: a new density-icon button (`#view-mode-toggle-button`, in the list detail header next to the share/reorder/edit buttons) toggles `localStorage['trakka:viewMode:<type>']` between `detailed` (default — today's existing two-row card, now also showing a full row of small pastel label chips under the title via `buildLabelChipsRow`, wrapped in a new `topGroup` div only when there's a chip row to show, so an item with no labels renders byte-for-byte as before) and `compact` (a forced single line — `buildItemRow`'s `compact` derivation is now `compactView || (!showQuantity && !showPrice)`, so a `shopping`/`groceries`/`recurring_shopping` list can be collapsed the same way `todo`/`custom` already structurally are; the quantity stepper/price pill are additionally gated on `!compact` so they don't leak into a `compactView`-forced single line the way they never could before when compactness was purely structural). Compact view shows `buildLabelMiniBadge` instead — up to three small colored dots (a stable hash-based palette, `labelChipClasses`/`labelDotClass`, shared between the chip and dot renderings so a given label is always the same color everywhere) plus a `+N` overflow count, with the full label list as a title/aria-label tooltip. Both quantity and price stay reachable via the edit modal/actions sheet regardless of view mode, per the feature's own "Le clic sur la carte ou le menu ⋮ permet d'éditer" requirement.

  Label management is a new bottom sheet (`#label-manage-sheet`, `openLabelManageSheet`), reachable from a new "🏷️ Gérer les labels" entry in `#item-actions-sheet` and from a matching button + read-only chip preview in the edit-item modal (both close their own modal/sheet first — the same sequential close/open pattern `itemActionsEditButton` already used for the edit modal itself, so at most one modal/sheet is ever open at once) — a search/create text input plus a chip list combining every label already used elsewhere in the currently open list (`collectListLabelSuggestions`, deduped case-insensitively) with the item's own current labels, each chip toggling on a single tap via `commitItemLabels`: an optimistic, coalesced-in-flight PATCH (`{requestId, committedLabels}` per item, the identical pattern `changeQuantity`/`toggleUrgent` already use, so a slower stale response can never clobber a value the user has since moved past with further taps) rather than a separate "save" step. Typing text with no matching suggestion offers a "+ Créer «…»" chip; pressing Enter on text that *does* match an existing suggestion reuses that suggestion's own casing rather than creating a differently-cased near-duplicate. No `sw.js` changes were needed — `labels` is a plain field already covered by the service worker's generic `{...existing, ...body}` optimistic-edit spread and by `db.js`'s opaque per-item IndexedDB rows, the same "no schema-aware mirror to update" story `is_urgent`/`icon` already had. `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v74` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`list_view.js`/both locale files (new `items.viewModeToggleAriaLabelTo{Detailed,Compact}`/`labelsBadgeAriaLabel`, `modals.itemActions.manageLabels`, `modals.editItem.{labelsLabel,manageLabelsButton,noLabels}`, and a new `modals.labelManage.*` section).

  Verified: `internal/validate/labels_test.go` (trim/dedup/casing/length/count-ceiling cases), `internal/db/items_test.go`'s `TestItemLabelsPersistence` (empty-by-default, `SetItemLabels` round-tripping through `GetItem`, clearing back to empty, `UpdateItem` never clobbering a previously-set label set, `ErrNotFound` for a nonexistent item), and `internal/handlers/item_labels_test.go` (POST cleaning/validation, PUT's full-replace-resets-on-omit semantics, PATCH's absent-vs-present-vs-empty-array three states) — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean across the whole repo. Frontend: `node --check` on every edited JS file, a Go-`x/net/html` parse confirming `index.html` is still well-formed with no duplicate ids, and the same JSON-validity/key-parity/`{placeholder}`-consistency check this log's other i18n entries used (303/303 keys, zero mismatches either direction). **Not yet done**: no browser verification of any of the frontend (the view-mode toggle actually collapsing/expanding a card, the chip row vs. mini-badge rendering in each mode, the label-management sheet's search/create/toggle flow, or that Compact mode stays perfectly aligned on a real narrow phone) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: open a `shopping` list, add a few labels to an item via the actions-sheet/edit-modal "🏷️ Gérer les labels" entry points, confirm the chips render under the title in Détaillé and collapse to colored dots in Compact, toggle the view-mode button and confirm the switch is instant with no layout shift/misalignment on a narrow viewport, and confirm the per-`type` `localStorage` persistence (switching a `shopping` list to Compact doesn't affect an already-open `todo` list).

- **Admin Console restructuring**: the admin surface was restructured for clearer navigation and expanded from a single instance-settings panel into a full "Console d'Administration". The header's standalone ⚙️ admin button (`#admin-settings-button`) is gone entirely; a new "🛡️ Console d'Administration" button (`#admin-console-button`, admin-only, `hidden` by default) opens the console from inside the ordinary "Paramètres" modal (`#user-settings-modal`) instead — it lives immediately below the modal's title row, before the Apparence/Langue selects, so an admin sees and can reach it the instant the modal opens rather than having to scroll past every ordinary preference first (a same-session follow-up moved it here from its original spot at the very bottom, after the install-help button — the original placement is kept only as history in this paragraph). It keeps a `mb-4` (16px) gap below it and a distinct sky-tinted card style (`border-sky-300`/`bg-sky-50` in light, `dark:border-sky-700`/`dark:bg-sky-950/40` in dark, `font-semibold` text) so it visually separates itself from and stands out among the ordinary slate-toned preference cards beneath it, while otherwise following the same shield-icon/card-button shape every other button in this modal uses; opening the console itself still follows the exact same "modal stacked on modal" pattern `install-help.js` already established — only the trigger button's position and styling changed, not how it's opened/closed. Clicking it opens `#admin-console-modal` — the old `#admin-settings-modal` renamed and widened (`max-w-2xl`) — now holding four internal tabs (`static/js/admin.js`, fully rewritten, `adminConsoleEls`/`setAdminConsoleTab`): "Paramètres" (the pre-existing instance name/registration/OIDC form, unchanged internally, just relocated into a tab panel), "Utilisateurs", "Espaces", and "Logs" — the latter three are new.

  Backend, all newly gated behind the pre-existing `authorizeAdmin`: `GET /api/v1/admin/users` (`internal/db.ListAllUsers`) and `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/v1/admin/users/{id}` (`internal/handlers/admin_users.go`, `internal/db.SetUserAdmin`/`DeleteUser`/`CountAdmins`) — promoting/demoting/deleting any account, with two safeguards: an admin can't delete their own account from this panel, and neither demoting nor deleting the *last* remaining admin is allowed (per `db.CreateUser`'s own doc comment, there's no seeding mechanism/CLI to grant the role back once every admin is gone — this would be a permanent lockout). `DeleteUser` is a single `DELETE FROM users` relying on the schema's existing `ON DELETE CASCADE` chain (sessions, house_members, custom_categories, list_shares/space_shares as recipient, space_house_pins, push_subscriptions, pending_invitations as inviter) to clean up everything the account owned or was granted — no new migration needed. `GET /api/v1/admin/spaces` (`internal/db.ListAllCustomCategoriesWithOwners`, a new `AdminCustomCategoryRow` type embedding `CustomCategory` plus `owner_email`/`owner_display_name`/`list_count`) and `DELETE /api/v1/admin/spaces/{id}` (`internal/db.DeleteCustomCategory`, the non-owner-scoped admin override of the existing owner-scoped `DeleteCustomCategoryForUser`) — list/delete any user's Space instance-wide, with the same `ON DELETE SET NULL` behavior on referencing lists as the owner-initiated delete. `GET /api/v1/admin/logs` (`internal/handlers/admin_logs.go`, `?limit=`, default 200/max 1000) is backed by a brand-new package, `internal/logbuffer`: a fixed-capacity ring-buffer `slog.Handler` (`NewHandler(next, capacity)`) that wraps the application's real handler — every record still reaches stdout exactly as before, and is also captured into an in-memory ring (shared across `WithAttrs`/`WithGroup`-derived loggers via a pointer, so `logger.With(...)` calls still land in the same buffer) — `Entries()` returns a most-recent-first snapshot. `cmd/server/main.go` wires it in with capacity 500 (`logbuffer.NewHandler(slog.NewJSONHandler(os.Stdout, nil), 500)`) and passes it to `Application.LogBuffer` (a new field, nil-safe in the handler — a test-constructed `Application{}` with no `LogBuffer` set just returns an empty list rather than panicking). This is deliberately in-memory-only with no persistence across a restart, matching the project's stdlib-first/no-new-infra ethos — see `internal/logbuffer`'s own package doc for the reasoning.

  Frontend: the Utilisateurs tab lists every account (email/display name, an "Admin" badge, a promote/demote button, and a delete button) via `loadAdminUsers`/`buildAdminUserRow`; both the demote and delete actions are client-side pre-emptively disabled (with a tooltip) when they'd hit either safeguard, mirroring the server-side checks. The Espaces tab lists every Space instance-wide with its owner and list count (`loadAdminSpaces`/`buildAdminSpaceRow`) and a delete action. Both delete actions use a new shared `buildConfirmableDeleteButton` helper — a "click again to confirm" inline pattern (the button turns into a "Confirmer la suppression ?" pill for 4s, reverting if not clicked again) rather than a native `confirm()` popup, since this codebase uses none anywhere else. The Logs tab (`loadAdminLogs`/`buildAdminLogRow`, plus a manual "Rafraîchir" button — no auto-polling) shows each entry's timestamp, a color-coded level, the message, and any structured attrs as inline JSON. `sw.js`'s `queueOfflineWrite` was extended to require connectivity (immediate `503`, not queued) for `PATCH`/`DELETE` on `/api/v1/admin/users/{id}` and `/api/v1/admin/spaces/{id}`, the same reasoning already applied to `/api/v1/admin/settings` — these are rare, high-stakes mutations (an admin-count safeguard evaluated server-side, a whole account's cascade-delete) where a stale queued action silently reapplying later would be worse than an immediate, visible failure; the new `GET` endpoints need no such change since they have no offline mirror and already fall through `offlineReadFallback`'s generic `503`. `admin.js`'s script tag was moved to load after `settings.js`/`push.js`/`install-help.js` (consistent with its button now living inside `#user-settings-modal`, the same "loads after the modal it stacks on" convention `install-help.js` already follows) — this is a load-*order* change only; every cross-file reference is still made lazily from inside a function body, never at another script's top-level parse time, the same safety property that already let `install-help.js` reference `settings.js`'s `userSettingsEls`. The now-dead `header.adminSettings` i18n key (no header button left to use it) was removed from both locale files; a new `modals.adminConsole.*` section (title, four tab labels, users/spaces/logs sub-sections) and `modals.userSettings.adminConsoleButton` were added, key parity confirmed via a throwaway Go program (no `node`/`python` available in the sandbox — same limitation noted elsewhere in this log).

  Verified: new Go tests for every new DB method (`internal/db/users_test.go`'s `TestListAllUsers`/`TestSetUserAdminAndCountAdmins`/`TestDeleteUser`, `internal/db/custom_categories_test.go`'s `TestListAllCustomCategoriesWithOwners`/`TestDeleteCustomCategoryAdmin`) and every new handler (`internal/handlers/admin_users_test.go`, `internal/handlers/admin_spaces_test.go`, `internal/handlers/admin_logs_test.go` — including the last-admin-demote/delete safeguards, the nil-`LogBuffer` no-panic case, and the ring buffer's capacity/eviction/most-recent-first ordering in `internal/logbuffer/logbuffer_test.go`) — `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean across the whole repo. Frontend: a throwaway Go program using `golang.org/x/net/html` confirmed `index.html` still parses with no duplicate ids, that every id `admin.js` references via `getElementById` actually exists, and that the removed header button's id is genuinely gone; brace-balance checks on every edited JS file and on `sw.js`; the locale-file JSON-validity/key-parity/placeholder-consistency check. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of any of it (the header button's removal, opening the console from Settings, tab switching, the Utilisateurs/Espaces/Logs panels' rendering and actions, the confirm-delete interaction, or the offline `503` behavior on the new admin write endpoints) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: as an admin, open Paramètres and confirm the Console d'Administration button appears immediately below the modal's title (no scrolling needed, with its 16px gap and sky-tinted styling clearly setting it apart from the Apparence/Langue cards beneath it) and the header no longer has a gear icon; open the console, confirm the Paramètres tab still saves instance settings correctly; switch to Utilisateurs and confirm promote/demote/delete work and are correctly disabled for your own account and the last remaining admin; switch to Espaces and confirm listing/deleting another user's Space works and that a list referencing a deleted Space loses its tag rather than being deleted itself; switch to Logs and confirm recent activity shows up and "Rafraîchir" pulls in new entries; as a non-admin, confirm the console button never appears. Also confirm a fresh non-admin account can't reach any of the six new endpoints directly (already covered by the handler tests, but worth a live sanity check).

- **Fix: `i18n.js` could overwrite a good HTML fallback with a raw, untranslated key** (reported as the new `modals.userSettings.adminConsoleButton` button showing its literal key instead of "Console d'Administration"). Root-caused, not just patched blind: this app has no i18next and no `translateDOM()`/`updateContent()` re-run hook — `static/js/i18n.js`'s own `applyTranslations()` is the only thing that ever writes a `data-i18n*` element's text/attribute, and it already ran correctly on page load (`loadLang()` `await`s the real `/locales/{lang}.json` fetch *before* ever calling `applyTranslations()`, so the bootstrap fallback object at the top of the file — itself incomplete by design, see its own doc comment — was never actually in play for static markup). The real mechanism: `static/sw.js`'s `handleShellRequest` serves every non-API/non-`/auth/` `GET`, including `/locales/*.json`, cache-first (by design — locale files are part of `APP_SHELL` specifically so the UI still translates while offline), and a service worker sits *in front of* the page's own `fetch()` — so `i18n.js`'s `fetch(..., { cache: 'no-store' })` only ever bypasses the **browser's** native HTTP cache, not the SW's Cache Storage lookup, which never inspects that init option at all. A client whose service worker hadn't yet picked up this session's own `SHELL_CACHE` bump (SW updates are opt-in via the "Mettre à jour" banner, never automatic) kept being served whatever `/locales/fr.json`/`en.json` snapshot was cached *before* `adminConsoleButton` (or any other newly-added key) existed — and the old `t()`/`applyTranslations()` unconditionally did `el.textContent = t(key)`, where `t()` falls back to returning the bare key string itself when a lookup misses, stomping the element's perfectly good hardcoded HTML fallback with that raw key.

  Fixed at the single choke point every `data-i18n`/`data-i18n-placeholder`/`data-i18n-aria-label` element goes through, not with a one-off patch on this one button: a new `hasTranslation(key)` helper, and `applyTranslations()`'s three loops now skip writing anything at all when it returns `false`, leaving whatever's already in the DOM — the real, already-correct fallback text every such element in `index.html` already carries — untouched instead of replacing it with a broken key. This generalizes the fix to every current and future `data-i18n*` element in the app against the identical failure mode (a stale SW-cached locale file, a genuine key typo, anything), not just the one button reported. `t()` itself is unchanged (a *dynamic* string built in JS via `TrakkaI18n.t(...)`, which has no HTML fallback to preserve, still needs its own raw-key-on-miss behavior to stay visibly broken rather than silently blank — see its updated doc comment). This does not touch — and deliberately does not need to touch — `sw.js`'s cache-first strategy for `/locales/*.json`, which is working as designed for offline support; nor does it add any `i18next`/`translateDOM()`-shaped API, since neither exists in this codebase. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v79` for the content change to already-cached `i18n.js` — which, per the very mechanism this fix addresses, only actually reaches an existing installed client once they click the "Mettre à jour" banner or do a fresh load with no previous SW at all.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched — pure frontend fix), a brace-balance check on the edited file, and a full re-read confirming every `data-i18n*` element in `index.html` already carries real fallback text (so the "leave it untouched" behavior never regresses to blank/missing content anywhere). **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: simulate the actual failure mode by manually removing a key from a served `/locales/fr.json` (or forcing an old SW to stay in control past a content change) and confirming the affected element keeps showing its hardcoded fallback rather than the raw key; separately confirm ordinary language switching (FR ↔ EN via the Paramètres modal) still updates every string correctly now that a *found* key still overwrites as before.

- **Dynamic item sort in the list detail view**: a new "⇅" header button (`#sort-items-button`, in `static/js/list_view.js`/`static/index.html`, next to the Compact/Detailed toggle) opens `#sort-items-sheet` — the same bottom-sheet idiom every other menu in this app uses (`#item-actions-sheet`, `#list-card-actions-sheet`, ...) rather than a dropdown, since the app has none — offering six mutually-exclusive orders: **Ordre par défaut** (today's original behavior: server position, with unfinished urgent items floated to the top), **Nom (A → Z / Z → A)**, **Prix (croissant / décroissant)**, and **Label**. Entirely client-side: no request to the server, no change to any item's stored `position`, so switching modes is instant and never touches the offline sync/reorder machinery. Persisted **per list id** (`localStorage['trakka:sortMode:<listId>']`, via `getSortMode`/`setSortMode`) rather than per list type like the Compact/Detailed toggle, since two lists of the same type may reasonably want a different order — matching the "pour chaque liste" requirement this was built against. `applySortMode(items, mode)` is the one comparator function every non-default mode goes through, applied independently to `renderItems()`'s existing active/done split (both are computed first, *then* sorted) — so checking/unchecking an item still moves it between the two sections exactly as before, and only the order *within* each section changes; `Array.prototype.sort`'s stability means any tie (equal names/prices, both unlabeled) keeps the items' existing relative order instead of reshuffling them, the same guarantee the pre-existing urgent-first sort already relied on. Price sort puts an unpriced item last regardless of direction (`a.price ?? Infinity` / `b.price ?? -Infinity`) — "trier par prix" on an item with no price at all is meaningless, so it consistently drops to the bottom rather than jumping to the top the moment "décroissant" is picked. Label sort groups items by their full label set (`labelSortKey`: every label sorted alphabetically and joined, so two items sharing the same set — added in any order — sort adjacently), with an unlabeled item likewise always sorting after every labeled one. The two "Prix" rows in the sheet carry the same `data-item-field="price"` attribute every other price-only control in this app already uses, so `applyListTypeVisibility` (unchanged) automatically hides them on a list type with no price at all (`todo`/`custom`), exactly like the price input on the create/edit-item forms. The header button itself highlights (the same `bg-sky-500/10`/`text-sky-600` treatment `#quick-add-toggle` already gives itself while expanded) whenever a non-default sort is active for the open list, and the sheet shows a ✓ next to the currently selected option (`updateSortOptionChecks`, run right before the sheet opens). `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v85` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`list_view.js`/both locale files (new `items.sortItemsAriaLabel` and a new `modals.sortItems.*` section, six keys). Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a brace/paren/bracket balance check on the edited JS files, a Go/`golang.org/x/net/html` parse confirming `index.html` is still well-formed with no duplicate ids and that every new id exists exactly once, and the same JSON-validity/key-parity/`{placeholder}`-consistency check this log's other i18n entries use (329/329 keys, zero mismatches either direction). **Not yet done**: no browser verification of any of it (opening the sheet, picking each of the five non-default orders and confirming the active/done split and the urgent-item styling survive it, the ✓/highlight state, the price rows correctly hiding on a `todo`/`custom` list, and the per-list persistence surviving a reload or a switch to a different list) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log.

- **Fix: the edit-item modal (`#edit-item-modal`) could overflow the viewport on a short/narrow screen with no way to scroll to the clipped fields**: reported as the modal's top and bottom getting truncated on small screens, with no scroll available to reach the rest of its form — with all of name/URL/price/target-price/target-month/recurrence/urgent/labels visible at once, the edit form is one of the tallest pieces of UI in the app, and its dialog (`static/index.html`) had no height cap or internal scroll region at all, so on a short viewport it simply grew past both edges of the screen. Root-caused against the actual markup rather than the generic `.modal-backdrop`/`.modal-content`/`.modal-body` class names the report assumed — this codebase has no such classes; every modal is a one-off `id`-addressed `<div>` styled with inline Tailwind utility classes (see the "Frontend (PWA, `static/`)" conventions section below) — and fixed by mirroring the identical, already-shipped fix for the exact same bug class on `#admin-console-modal` (see its own dedicated CSS comment block in `static/css/base.css`) rather than inventing a second approach: the dialog (`[role="dialog"]` inside `#edit-item-modal`) gained `flex max-h-[90vh] flex-col overflow-hidden`, its header div gained `id="edit-item-modal-header"` plus `shrink-0` (so the flexbox shrink algorithm can never push it out of view), and `#edit-item-form` — the one remaining child, so it doubles as the entire scrollable body — gained `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto` (`min-h-0` is what overrides the flex-item default `min-height: auto`, which would otherwise floor the form at its own full unscrolled height and defeat the scroll region entirely). This keeps the modal centered identically (via the backdrop's pre-existing `items-center`/`justify-center`, `items-end` for the mobile bottom-sheet look) whether or not a given item's fields actually need to scroll, since the dialog's box never grows past `max-h-[90vh]` either way. `static/css/base.css` gained the matching CSS: `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on both the header and the form (plus `overflow-y: hidden` on the header, needed for the property to take effect at all there) so a native, non-overlay scrollbar appearing on the form alone can't throw it out of horizontal alignment with the header — the identical fix already explained at length for `#admin-console-header`/`-tabs`/`-body` — and a slim, discreet scrollbar scoped to `#edit-item-form` specifically (`scrollbar-width: thin` for Firefox, `::-webkit-scrollbar*` for Chrome/Safari/Edge, both colored from the existing theme-aware `--tk-border-strong`/`--tk-text-subtle` tokens rather than a hardcoded rgba pair that would wash out in Dark mode). `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v86` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`base.css`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a brace-balance check on the edited CSS, and a Go/`golang.org/x/net/html` parse confirming `index.html` is still well-formed with no duplicate ids and that the new `edit-item-modal-header` id exists exactly once. **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, same caveat as most other frontend-only work in this log. Before relying on it: on a short/narrow viewport (or Chrome DevTools' responsive mode set to a small height), open the edit-item modal for an item whose list type shows every optional field, confirm the modal never exceeds ~90% of the viewport height, that its header (title + close button) stays fixed and visible while the form scrolls internally, that the scrollbar is slim and doesn't shift the form's fields out of alignment with the header, and that the modal stays centered on both a screen that needs to scroll and one that doesn't.

- **Fix: dashboard list order and a card's "N restant(s)" count could disagree between the offline (cached) and online (network) dashboard renders**: reported live with a concrete repro — the "Achats & Sourcing" grid showed Homelab/Courses/Bébé while offline but Bébé/Courses/Homelab (the exact reverse) once back online, and the Homelab card's remaining-item count read 4 offline vs 3 online. Two independent, unrelated root causes, both in the IndexedDB mirror layer (`static/js/db.js`/`static/sw.js`), not in the sort/rendering code itself (`renderGrid`/`buildListCard`/`urlBadges` in `app.js` were already correct and untouched by this fix).

  **Cause 1 (the order flip)**: `internal/db.ListListsForUser` orders lists `ORDER BY lists.created_at DESC` (newest first) — the order every online `GET /api/v1/lists` response, and therefore every online dashboard render, already uses. The offline/cache path (`app.js`'s `cachedDashboardLists` → `db.js`'s `getListsByHouse` → `getLists()` → `getAll(STORE_LISTS)`) applied no sort of its own at all: IndexedDB's `getAll()` returns records in ascending primary-key order with no way to request anything else, which is oldest-created-first — the exact reverse of the server's newest-first order whenever list ids and creation order line up, matching the reported symptom byte for byte. Fixed by sorting once, centrally, in `db.js`'s `getLists()` (`compareListsNewestFirst`, a plain ISO-8601 string comparison on `created_at` mirroring the server's own ordering, with a deterministic `id`-descending tie-break for two lists created in the same millisecond) — both of `getLists()`'s only two callers (`getListsByHouse`, used by `app.js`'s cache-paint/offline-fallback path, and `sw.js`'s own `offlineReadFallback` for a flaky-but-`navigator.onLine`-true `GET /api/v1/lists`) inherit the fix automatically rather than needing two separate, driftable sort implementations.

  **Cause 2 (the stale count)**: `sw.js`'s `mirrorReadResponse` mirrors a `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}` response's `items` array into IndexedDB purely by upserting (`putItems`) — nothing ever pruned a locally-mirrored item that the server no longer returns for that list (deleted from a different session/device, moved to another list, etc.), so a stale item left over from an earlier sync could sit in the mirror indefinitely, inflating that list's offline "N restant(s)" count relative to what a fresh online fetch reports — exactly the Homelab card's 4-vs-3 mismatch. Fixed with a new `db.js` method, `pruneRemovedListItems(listId, currentIds)`: after the existing `freshItems`/`putItems` upsert, it deletes any item mirrored under `listId` whose id isn't in the just-received response's own id list — deliberately built from the *raw* response array, not the staleness-filtered one, so an out-of-order GET that `freshItems` already declined to overwrite is never mistaken for a deletion. A still-queued offline-created item (a `temp-item-*` id, which can never legitimately appear in a server response since the server doesn't know about it yet) is explicitly exempted from pruning via a new `isTempId` helper, so this can never discard an unsynced local creation. Wired into both `mirrorReadResponse` branches that receive a full, list-scoped item set: `GET /api/v1/lists/{id}` (the one that actually matters here — it's what both the list detail view and the dashboard's per-list detail fan-out in `loadDashboard` use) and, for completeness/symmetry even though no frontend code currently calls it that way, `GET /api/v1/items?list_id=` (guarded to only prune when the request was actually list-scoped — an unscoped items fetch says nothing about what a given list should still contain, so pruning there would be unsafe).

  `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v87` for the content changes to already-cached `db.js`/`sw.js`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched — pure frontend fix), and brace/paren balance checks on both edited files (no `node`/`python3` was available in this sandbox to run a real syntax check, unlike a couple of other entries in this log that had one — same limitation as most frontend-only work here). **Not yet done**: no browser verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: create a few lists, go offline, confirm the dashboard's card order matches what a subsequent online reload shows (newest first, in both states); separately, with a list open on two different sessions/devices (or by directly deleting an item via curl while a browser tab has that list's detail cached), delete an item from one session, let the other session go offline before it ever re-fetches that list, then bring it back online and confirm the stale item's count corrects itself and the item disappears from the mirror rather than lingering indefinitely.

- **Mobile UX overhaul of the list detail header: a single "Options" sheet replacing the row of individual action icons, plus a new client-side Filters sheet**: the list detail header's second row (`static/index.html`'s `#items-section`) used to be five separate square icon buttons — Partager/Trier/Vue compacte-détaillée/Réordonner/Modifier — squeezed in next to the (already-truncating) list title, worst on a narrow phone with a long name. Redesigned into two rows: row 1 is just back + the full title/sync-badge/remaining-count + one "⋮" **Options** button; row 2 holds two pill buttons, **Trier** and a brand-new **Filtres**, since those are what's actually used while browsing a list. `#list-options-sheet` is a new bottom sheet (the same pattern as `#item-actions-sheet`) consolidating Partager/Vue compacte-détaillée/Réordonner/Modifier la liste into text-labeled rows — these are literally the *same* elements the old header used (`#share-list-button`/`#view-mode-toggle-button`/`#reorder-list-button`/`#edit-list-button`), just relocated and restyled, so `list_view.js`'s/`reorder.js`'s existing click handlers/`.hidden` toggling on them kept working unchanged; each one now closes the sheet first before acting (`closeListOptionsSheet()`), the same sequential close-then-open convention `#item-actions-sheet`'s own rows already use — `updateViewModeToggleButton` was adjusted to write its icon/label into two child spans (`#view-mode-toggle-icon`/`-label`) instead of overwriting the whole button's `innerHTML`, since the row now needs a visible text label alongside the icon.

  **Filters are new** (no prior feature to build on): `#filter-items-sheet`, opened by the new "Filtres" pill (badge-dotted via `#filter-active-badge` whenever any filter is non-default), offers a Statut segmented control (Tout/Restant/Acheté), a multi-select of the list's own labels (built from the existing `collectListLabelSuggestions()`, the section hidden outright when the list has no labels at all), and a price range (two number inputs, gated behind the same `data-item-field="price"` convention already used everywhere else price-only UI hides itself on a `todo`/`custom` list). Entirely client-side and per-list-*id* (`getFilterState`/`setFilterState`, `localStorage['trakka:filters:<id>']`, mirroring `getSortMode`/`setSortMode`'s own per-list-id persistence) — no request to the server, no offline-queue interaction. `renderItems()` (`list_view.js`) now runs `applyItemFilters` (labels + price range) before the active/done split, and folds the Statut choice into that split itself (`status !== 'done'`/`!== 'active'` gates on the `active`/`done` filter predicates) rather than filtering it separately — so "Restant" simply forces the done section empty and "Acheté" forces the active section empty, with a dedicated `items.filterShowingDoneOnly` message in the (now-empty-by-design) active slot so it doesn't misleadingly read as "Tout est terminé", and the done `<details>` auto-opens itself when Statut = Acheté finds something to show. A distinct `items.noFilterMatches` message covers the "labels/price filters matched nothing" case, kept separate from "allDone" so the two don't get confused. The Trier sheet also gained a new, explicit **Urgence** sort option (`applySortMode`'s new `'urgent'` case — urgent items first, alphabetical within each group) alongside its existing five modes, since the previous "Ordre par défaut" only put urgent items first implicitly.

  New i18n keys added to both `static/locales/{fr,en}.json`: `items.{listOptionsAriaLabel,sortButtonLabel,filterButtonLabel,filterItemsAriaLabel,noFilterMatches,filterShowingDoneOnly}`, `modals.sortItems.urgent`, and two new sections, `modals.listOptions`/`modals.filterItems`. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v88` for the content-only changes to already-cached `index.html`/`list_view.js`/`reorder.js`/both locale files (no new cached paths). Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), brace/paren/bracket balance checks on every edited JS file, a Go/`golang.org/x/net/html` parse confirming `index.html` is still well-formed with no duplicate ids and that every new id is present exactly once, and the locale files' usual JSON-validity/key-parity/`{placeholder}`-consistency check (349/349 keys, zero mismatches either direction) — no `node`/`python3` was available in this sandbox, same limitation as most frontend-only work in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of any of it — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: on a narrow viewport, confirm the header no longer truncates the list name behind a row of icons and that the "⋮" Options sheet's four rows (Partager/Vue/Réordonner/Modifier) all still work exactly as their old header-button equivalents did; open the Filtres sheet, confirm Statut/label/price-range filtering actually narrows the item list and that the badge dot appears/disappears correctly; confirm "Réinitialiser les filtres" clears everything and the badge/highlight go away; and confirm the new "Urgence" sort option in the Trier sheet groups urgent items first, alphabetically.

- **Fix: mobile touch-tap hardening — the new `#list-options-button` ("⋮" Options button, see the "Mobile UX overhaul" bullet above) was reported as opening fine with a desktop click but doing nothing at all on a touchscreen tap**: investigated the actual wiring rather than assuming the report's own suggested code, which named elements (`#listOptionsMenuBtn`, `.list-options-dropdown`, a `.show` class toggle) that don't exist anywhere in this codebase — this app has no such ids/classes; every sheet/modal follows the single established `hidden`-attribute + backdrop-click-closes convention documented in the "Frontend (PWA, `static/`)" conventions section below, and `#list-options-button`'s own wiring (`listEls.listOptionsButton.addEventListener('click', openListOptionsSheet)` in `static/js/list_view.js`) already matches that convention exactly, with no duplicate id, no stray `disabled`/`pointer-events: none`, and a hit area already at the 44×44px minimum. Also re-checked for interference from the touch-gesture layer (`static/js/gestures.js`): its edge-swipe-back tracking only ever activates for a touch starting within 30px of the screen's *left* edge (the options button sits at the header's far right), and every listener there is `{ passive: true }` with no `preventDefault` call anywhere that could suppress the browser's synthesized `click`, so it cannot be the cause either. No functional bug was found in the reviewed event-wiring/CSS-cascade/z-index (`[hidden] { display: none !important }` already exists in `static/css/base.css` specifically to defeat the classic Tailwind-`.flex`-beats-`[hidden]` gotcha; every sheet, this one included, shares one `z-40`, well above the `z-30` sticky header and below nothing that would intercept a tap). Since this exact "Options" redesign has never been exercised on a real touchscreen at all (see the "Mobile UX overhaul" bullet's own "Not yet done" note), and no browser/touch-emulation tooling was available in this sandbox to reproduce the symptom directly, the fix applied is defensive hardening rather than a targeted one-line correction: a new global rule in `static/css/base.css` sets `touch-action: manipulation` and `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on every `<button>` in the app (every icon/kebab/pill/sheet-opening control here is a plain `<button>`, so one type-selector rule covers all of them, present and future) — `touch-action: manipulation` tells the browser up front that a button is a discrete tap target rather than a candidate for its own double-tap-to-zoom/pinch gesture recognition, which on some Android WebViews and older iOS Safari versions can occasionally swallow a fast tap near another interactive element instead of just delaying it; `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` replaces iOS Safari's default gray tap flash with this app's own `:hover`/`:active` Tailwind classes. A companion `.swipe-item button { touch-action: pan-y }` rule (added right next to `.reorder-handle`'s pre-existing `touch-action: none`, the same "gesture-related touch-action" section) restores the vertical-only panning `attachItemSwipeGestures` (`gestures.js`) already sets on an item row's foreground for any button nested inside it (the checkbox, quantity stepper, edit/delete/kebab controls) — without it, the new blanket `manipulation` rule would have won on exactly those elements (a class selector always beats a bare type selector regardless of file order), letting the browser's native horizontal-pan handling compete with the item-swipe-to-delete/done gesture for a touch starting on one of them. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v89` for the content-only change to already-cached `base.css`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), a brace-balance check on the edited CSS (67/67). **Not yet done**: no browser or real-device verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. If the "⋮" Options button (or any other button) still doesn't respond to a tap after this change, the next thing to capture is *what specifically happens* on the device: does the sheet's `hidden` attribute actually flip in a remote DevTools inspector (proving the `click` handler did fire, pointing at a purely visual/CSS-stacking issue instead) or does nothing change at all (pointing at the tap never reaching the button, e.g. a stale service-worker-cached page still serving pre-redesign markup — reload with "Update on reload"/clear site data first to rule that out) — either answer narrows this down far more precisely than static code review alone can.

- **Fix: the header's Online/Offline status could get stuck on "Hors-ligne" after the server came back, and the service-worker update banner could go missing on a long-lived mobile PWA session**: both symptoms shared one root cause — every existing trigger for `updateNetworkStatus()`/the service-worker-update check was purely *event-driven* (the browser's own `online`/`offline` events, and `visibilitychange` for network status; nothing at all for update checks beyond whatever the browser happens to decide to re-check `sw.js` on its own, typically only around a real navigation), so a session that never received one of those specific events — a tab left open and focused in the foreground the whole time the backend happened to restart, or, more commonly on a phone, a PWA installed to the home screen and kept alive by the OS for a long stretch without ever being fully reloaded — had no way to notice either a network recovery or a deployed update short of the user restarting the app outright, exactly as reported. Fixed with two independent, complementary additions to `static/js/app.js`, neither replacing what already existed: (1) a `window.addEventListener('focus', ...)` alongside the pre-existing `visibilitychange` listener — deliberately both, not one instead of the other, since a desktop tab can regain focus without ever having been hidden (alt-tabbing between windows) and a mobile browser's backgrounding/resuming is `visibilitychange`'s own domain, so neither event alone fires in every real scenario; both now call `updateNetworkStatus()` (the pre-existing `/healthz` ping, unchanged) and a new `checkForServiceWorkerUpdate()`. (2) Two `setInterval` backstops for the case where a session sits in the foreground the entire time and neither `online`/`visibilitychange`/`focus` ever fires at all: `updateNetworkStatus` every 30 seconds (`NETWORK_STATUS_POLL_MS`), and `checkForServiceWorkerUpdate` every 15 minutes (`SW_UPDATE_POLL_MS`, deliberately far less frequent than the health ping, since re-fetching and byte-comparing `sw.js` is heavier than a bare health check and doesn't need to be caught within seconds the way a network recovery does).

  `checkForServiceWorkerUpdate()` itself is new — a thin wrapper around `registration.update()` (the standard Fetch-and-compare-`sw.js` API, previously never called anywhere in this codebase) on a module-level `swRegistration` reference now captured in `registerServiceWorker()`'s `.then()` callback — with its own errors swallowed (offline, or the browser declining to check right now, just means the next trigger tries again). It deliberately reuses the *existing* `watchForServiceWorkerUpdate()`/`showUpdateBanner()` machinery for actually surfacing the result — `registration.update()` just forces the same `updatefound` → `installing` → `'installed'` `statechange` path a browser-initiated check would also take, so nothing about how the banner appears changed, only how often the check for one now happens. One deliberate omission, called out explicitly in a code comment so a future session doesn't "fix" it by mistake: this fix does **not** add an automatic `reload()` on `controllerchange`, even though that's a common pattern (and was what the bug report's own suggested code included) — `watchForServiceWorkerUpdate`'s own pre-existing doc comment already documents why the banner is deliberately "dismissible-by-inaction" rather than auto-reloading: `sw.js`'s `self.skipWaiting()`/`self.clients.claim()` mean `controllerchange` fires automatically moments after *any* install/activate, not only once a user clicks the banner's own reload button, so wiring an automatic reload to it would force every single deployed update to reload every open tab out from under whatever the user was doing, update banner or not — reintroducing exactly the "could wipe out an in-progress form" risk that design already exists to avoid. The reload button (`#update-reload-button`) is untouched and remains the only thing that ever calls `window.location.reload()`.

  `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v90` for the content-only change to already-cached `app.js`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched — pure frontend fix), and a brace/paren/bracket balance check on the edited file (914/914 parens, 30/30 brackets, 304/304 braces) — no `node`/`python3` was available in this sandbox to run a real syntax check, same limitation as most frontend-only work in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser or real-device verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: leave the app open and online, stop the backend, wait, then restart it and confirm the header's badge flips back to "En ligne" within 30 seconds with no interaction needed (and separately confirm alt-tabbing away and back, or backgrounding/resuming on a phone, triggers an immediate re-check rather than waiting for the interval); and, for the update banner, deploy a change (bump `sw.js`'s cache version again), leave an already-open tab/PWA session alone without reloading it, and confirm the banner appears on its own within 15 minutes (or immediately upon backgrounding/resuming or refocusing the window) rather than only after a manual reload.

- **Fix: `#list-options-button` ("⋮" Options button) still didn't respond on mobile after the touch-action hardening above — found the actual root cause this time, a missing scroll-position reset, not a touch-event problem at all**: a third report of the same symptom, again with the report's own suggested code (`#listOptionsMenuBtn`, `.list-options-dropdown`, a `.show` class, `onclick`/`stopPropagation`) not matching anything in this codebase — ignored in favor of investigating the real wiring again. Re-confirmed everything the previous fix (see the "mobile touch-tap hardening" bullet above) had already ruled out still holds: no duplicate `id="list-options-button"` (checked via a fresh `golang.org/x/net/html` parse), no ancestor `pointer-events`/`preventDefault`/global `document` click-outside handler, no z-index/stacking overlap, `touch-action: manipulation` already in place. This time the investigation went one level further: `<main>` (`static/index.html`) carries no `overflow-y` of its own, which makes `window`/`document` — not some inner scroll container — the actual scrolling element for the entire app, and grepping every frontend file for `scrollTo`/`scrollIntoView`/`scrollTop` turned up **zero** matches anywhere in `static/js/`. Neither `selectList()` (opening a list from the dashboard) nor `showDashboard()`/`setActiveTab()` (leaving a list, or switching dashboard tabs) ever reset scroll position — so opening a list card from partway down a tall, scrolled dashboard grid (routine on a phone, where the "Achats"/"Tâches"/"Espaces" grids are frequently taller than one screen) swaps in the list detail view while the page stays scrolled exactly where it was a moment before. The header's "⋮" Options button visually *should* sit at the top of the newly-shown view, but the stale scroll position puts something else on-screen at that exact spot instead — a tap where the user expects the button to be lands on nothing (or on an unrelated item row), which reads indistinguishably from "the button doesn't react" even though the button, its click handler, and every touch-event concern the previous fix addressed were never actually the problem. This also explains why it looked mobile-specific despite the button and its handler being identical at every breakpoint (this redesign has no `md:` split at all — see the "Mobile UX overhaul" bullet above): a desktop dashboard more often shows every card without scrolling at all, so the same missing reset rarely has anything to hide.

  Fixed with a new `resetScrollPosition()` helper (`static/js/list_view.js`, a one-line `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` with a doc comment recording this root cause for the next session), called from `selectList()` right after `listEls.itemsSection.hidden = false` (entering a list), from `showDashboard()` right after `els.listsSection.hidden = false` (leaving a list), and from `setActiveTab()` (`static/js/planning.js`, called lazily — safe regardless of script load order, the same convention every other cross-file call in this app already follows) so every one of the five dashboard tabs gets the identical fix, not just the list detail view the report specifically named. `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v91` for the content-only changes to already-cached `list_view.js`/`planning.js`. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .` clean (no Go files touched), and brace/paren balance checks on both edited JS files plus `sw.js` — no `node`/`python3` was available in this sandbox to run a real syntax check, same limitation as most frontend-only work in this log. **Not yet done**: no browser or real-device verification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: on a phone (or Chrome DevTools' device emulation with a tall dashboard grid), scroll down on the dashboard, tap a list card near the bottom of the screen, and confirm the list detail view opens already scrolled to its own top with the "⋮" Options button immediately tappable — no manual scroll-up needed; separately, open a list, scroll down through its items, tap back to the dashboard, and confirm the dashboard is likewise back at its own top rather than wherever the list view happened to be scrolled; and confirm switching between the five dashboard tabs (Listes/Budget/Urgent/Espaces/Partagé) while scrolled down on one of them lands each new tab scrolled to its own top too. If the button still doesn't respond after this, the next thing to capture is exactly what the earlier fix's own note already asks for: whether `#list-options-sheet`'s `hidden` attribute actually flips in a remote DevTools inspector when tapped.

- **Manual "check for updates" control in Settings, plus a real (not just suspected) root-cause fix for the update popup never appearing on some mobile sessions**: two related asks. First, a "Mises à jour" block in `#user-settings-modal` (`static/index.html`, wired in `static/js/settings.js`) shows the currently-running version and a "Vérifier les mises à jour" button — a manual escape hatch alongside the existing automatic visibility/focus/interval-driven checks (see the two bullets directly above), for a user who wants to force a check right now rather than wait. The displayed version is read directly off the Cache Storage API (`app.js`'s new `getAppVersion()`: `caches.keys()`, find the entry starting with `trakka-shell-`, strip the prefix) rather than a second, separately-maintained constant — deliberately, since `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` staying inlined literal strings in `sw.js`'s own source (not imported from a shared file) is exactly what guarantees a version bump changes `sw.js`'s own bytes, which is what `registration.update()`'s byte comparison actually keys off; a version constant factored out into its own file would stop changing `sw.js` at all and silently defeat that mechanism, so this reads the real, already-active cache name back out instead of introducing one. The button calls a new `manualCheckForUpdate()` (`app.js`): it attaches a one-shot `updatefound` listener to `swRegistration`, calls `.update()`, and after a 1s grace window (since `update()`'s own promise resolution says nothing about whether anything new was found — `updatefound` firing is the only reliable signal, and it fires synchronously as part of `update()`'s own algorithm) resolves to `'updated'` / `'up-to-date'` / `'error'` / `'unsupported'`. `settings.js` closes the settings modal on `'updated'` (so the already-existing `#update-banner` — shown by `watchForServiceWorkerUpdate`'s pre-existing listener once the new worker reaches `'installed'`, unchanged — is actually visible instead of sitting behind the modal's overlay) and shows a `TrakkaToast.success(...)` for the other three outcomes (new `modals.userSettings.updateCheck{UpToDate,Unsupported,Error}` keys, both locales).

  Second, and the actual mobile-specific fix: investigated why the existing visibility/focus/interval triggers (see the two bullets above) could still fail to ever surface an update on some mobile sessions despite calling `registration.update()` correctly, rather than assuming the report's own suggested rewrite (a from-scratch `sw-register.js` using a `postMessage({action:'skipWaiting'})` handshake) was the right fix — it isn't: this app's `sw.js` already calls `self.skipWaiting()`/`self.clients.claim()` unconditionally on install/activate (a deliberate, documented invariant — see the new "PWA lifecycle & mobile layout rules" section above), which the suggested handshake would have made redundant at best and, if it ever gated `skipWaiting()` behind the message instead, would have broken outright. The real, previously-unaddressed gap: `/sw.js` was served by the plain `http.FileServer` (`internal/handlers/app.go`) with no explicit `Cache-Control` header at all — every one of this app's update-detection mechanisms ultimately depends on `registration.update()` actually fetching a *fresh* copy of `sw.js` to byte-compare against the installed one, and without an explicit header, a browser's own heuristic HTTP caching (mobile Safari and mobile carrier/proxy caching are both historically more aggressive here than desktop Chrome) can silently serve back an already-cached copy instead — the check runs, finds "no difference" against its own stale copy, and reports nothing to update, indefinitely, regardless of how often or how promptly it's triggered. This is a well-documented, industry-standard gotcha (browsers are only *supposed* to always bypass the cache for the top-level SW script, but real-world engines have a history of not fully honoring that without an explicit header) and fits the reported PC-vs-mobile asymmetry far better than a lifecycle/event-wiring bug would, since the trigger code itself (`checkForServiceWorkerUpdate`) is identical on every platform. Fixed with a new, more-specific route registered directly on the app's `http.ServeMux` — `mux.HandleFunc("GET /sw.js", ...)`, which Go 1.22+'s mux resolves ahead of the catch-all `mux.Handle("/", ...)` regardless of registration order — that sets `Cache-Control: no-cache` (verified live: `curl -sD - http://localhost/sw.js` now shows the header, `200`, correct body) before delegating to the same `staticFileSystem`-wrapped file server every other static asset still uses; `no-cache` (not `no-store`) still lets the browser keep a cached copy but forces revalidation before ever using it, which is all an update check needs. Belt-and-suspenders on the client side: `registerServiceWorker()` (`app.js`) now registers with `{ updateViaCache: 'none' }`, which additionally bypasses the HTTP cache for `db.js` (loaded via `sw.js`'s own `importScripts`) on every update check — otherwise a stale cached copy of an *imported* script could keep running under a worker the browser still considers current, since the byte comparison that actually detects an update only ever looks at `sw.js`'s own top-level bytes, never its imports. Also added a defensive `if (registration.waiting && navigator.serviceWorker.controller) showUpdateBanner();` check right after registration resolves, covering the narrow race of a worker that reached `'installed'`/`'waiting'` before this page's own `updatefound` listener existed to catch it.

  `static/sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v94` for the content changes to already-cached `index.html`/`app.js`/`settings.js`/both locale files. Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test ./...` all clean, a live `curl` confirming the new header and correct `sw.js` body, a throwaway Go program using `golang.org/x/net/html` confirming `index.html` is still well-formed with every new id present exactly once, and the locale files' usual JSON-validity/key-parity/`{placeholder}`-consistency check (356/356 keys, zero mismatches either direction) — no `node`/`python3` was available in this sandbox, same limitation as most frontend-only work in this log. **Not yet done**: no real-device verification that this specific root cause was actually *the* cause of the originally-reported mobile symptom (only the mechanism and the fix's correctness were confirmed, via `curl` and the existing update-banner machinery, not the original failure reproduced first) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in. Before relying on it: on a real phone (particularly iOS Safari, historically the most aggressive at HTTP heuristic caching among the platforms this app targets), deploy a change, background/resume or refocus the installed PWA (or wait for the 15-minute interval, or use the new manual "Vérifier les mises à jour" button), and confirm the update banner now appears where it previously didn't; also exercise the manual button's three other outcomes (already up to date, offline/error, unsupported browser) and confirm the version line in Settings reads a real `vNN` rather than "Version inconnue".

- **Configurable, Google-Tasks-style task due-date reminders — generalizing the existing recurring-only reminder into one mechanism for any item with a due date, plus the frontend's first-ever due-date picker**: requested as a new feature ("ajouter un système de notifications push configurables pour les tâches, similaire à Google Tasks"), but most of the Web Push infrastructure it needed already existed (see the "Web Push notifications" bullet above) — this was mainly about generalizing the existing recurring-only reminder scan and adding the UI pieces that were still missing, not building push delivery from scratch. Full design in the "Use case 2" paragraph of [backend.md](backend.md) and the "Task due dates & reminders" paragraph of [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md); this entry just records what changed and how it was verified.

  New migration `0019_task_reminders.sql`: `users.reminder_default_offset_days`/`reminder_default_time` (a personal default, settable via `PATCH /api/v1/me`) and `items.reminder_enabled`/`reminder_offset_days`/`reminder_time` (the per-item counterpart), replacing the old `recurrence_lead_minutes`-driven, recurring-items-only model (left in the schema/API for backward compatibility, no longer read by any scan). New `internal/validate/time_of_day.go` (`TimeOfDay`, mirroring `Date`'s style). `internal/db/recurring_notify.go` renamed to `due_reminders.go` (`ListItemsForRecurringNotifyScan`/`MarkRecurringReminderSent`/`RecurringDueCandidate` → `ListItemsForDueReminderScan`/`MarkDueReminderSent`/`DueReminderCandidate`, dropping the `recurrence_rule IS NOT NULL` filter); `internal/db/items.go` gained `SetItemReminder` (a dedicated method, not another `CreateItem`/`UpdateItem` parameter — the same reasoning `SetItemLabels`, migration 18, already established for an already-too-long positional-parameter list) plus the 3 new columns threaded through `scanItem`/every full-row `SELECT` (including `price_alerts.go`/`target_price_scan.go`'s own inline selects). `internal/handlers.RunRecurringDueScan`/`checkItemForRecurringDue` renamed to `RunDueReminderScan`/`checkItemForDueReminder`, now computing a reminder instant (`due_date` minus `reminder_offset_days`, at `reminder_time`) instead of a lead-time-before-due-date comparison; `cmd/server/main.go`'s `runRecurringNotifyScanLoop` renamed to `runDueReminderScanLoop`. `internal/handlers.resolveReminderDefaults` (`items.go`) is the key new piece: it substitutes the *acting* user's own current `reminder_default_offset_days`/`reminder_default_time` into an item's reminder **once, at write time** (POST/PUT/PATCH `/api/v1/items` all call it before `SetItemReminder`) rather than leaving a live reference to be re-derived later — deliberate, since a shared list's item can be visible to several users with different personal defaults, so "notify every recipient at their own preferred time" has no single well-defined answer; resolving once keeps the existing single-moment, single-`sendToUsers`-fan-out design (used by both this and the "item added/checked off" notification) intact. `handleMeUpdate` (`auth.go`) gained the paired `reminder_default_offset_days`/`reminder_default_time` fields (rejecting one without the other, since a half-updated pair is ambiguous). Config: `NOTIF_RECURRING_TASK_LEAD_TIME` removed outright (superseded, never wired to any frontend control anyway); `NOTIF_RECURRING_SCAN_INTERVAL_MINUTES` renamed to `NOTIF_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MINUTES`; new `APP_TIMEZONE` (default `Europe/Paris`) — there was no notion of a time zone anywhere in this codebase before this feature, and a reminder's `HH:MM` is meaningless without one; resolved once at startup into a new `handlers.Application.Location` field, falling back to UTC with a startup warning on an unrecognized zone (the same graceful-degrade posture a broken OIDC discovery already gets), documented in `.env.example`/`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`.

  Frontend: the create-item form and edit-item modal gained a due-date `<input type="date">` and a reminder `<select>` ("Par défaut"/"Le jour même"/"La veille"/"Personnalisé"/"Désactivé") for `todo`/`recurring_shopping` lists (`FIELD_VISIBILITY_BY_TYPE`'s new `dueDate` flag) — there was previously **no frontend UI at all** to set a due date on any item, so this is the feature's first UI for that, not just the reminder layered on top. `reminderSelectionToPayload`/`reminderItemToSelection` (`static/js/list_view.js`) convert between the select (plus, for "Personnalisé", its own inline offset/time inputs) and the 3 API fields; "Par défaut" deliberately sends `reminder_offset_days: null, reminder_time: ""` rather than omitting them, since that's the one representation that means "use my current default" correctly under both a POST/PUT full payload and a PATCH's absent/null/value convention. The "Paramètres" modal gained a parallel preset select (`static/js/settings.js`) for the personal global default, wired to the same `PATCH /api/v1/me`. The existing push-permission toggle and the service worker's `push`/`notificationclick` handling needed **no changes** — both were already fully generic. `static/locales/{fr,en}.json` gained matching keys for all of the above; `sw.js`'s `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to `v96`.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` all clean, including a new `TestListItemsForDueReminderScan` (covering a plain non-recurring item alongside a recurring one — the generalization itself — plus the disabled/done/no-due-date/never-resolved exclusion cases and the re-arm-on-due-date-change behavior) and a new `internal/validate.TimeOfDay` test. Beyond unit tests, the full pipeline was exercised end-to-end via a local smoke test against a real running server: registered an account, set a personal reminder default via `PATCH /api/v1/me`, created items exercising "use my default" (confirmed it resolved to the account's current default), an explicit custom override, and a disabled reminder; `PATCH`ed a reminder back to "use my default" via explicit `null`/`""` after changing the account's default and confirmed it re-resolved to the *new* value (not the one at creation time); confirmed negative-offset/bad-time-format/half-given-pair validation errors; and confirmed the periodic scan (`NOTIF_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MINUTES=1` for a fast test loop) actually fired for a due-now item and wrote `due_reminder_sent_for`, by inspecting the SQLite file directly. **Not yet done**: no browser verification of the new frontend controls (the due-date/reminder inputs' visibility toggling, the create/edit forms' payloads, the Paramètres modal's preset select) — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, and no `node`/`python3` either (JSON validity was checked with a throwaway Go program instead, same substitute this log's other i18n entries used). Before relying on it: open the app, add a `todo` item with a due date and each of the 4 reminder options, confirm the edit modal round-trips correctly, set a personal default in Paramètres and confirm a new "Par défaut" task actually resolves to it, and — as with the original Web Push feature — confirm delivery end-to-end on a real device with VAPID configured.

- **Encrypted WebDAV backups (2026-09-29)**: full design in [backend.md](backend.md#encrypted-webdav-backups); operator docs (setup, what lives where, both disaster-recovery procedures) in [docs/DEPLOYMENT.md](../docs/DEPLOYMENT.md#encrypted-webdav-backups); endpoints in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#admin-backups); `backup_runs` in [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md#backup_runs); frontend specifics in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md#admin-console-encrypted-backups-tab). In short: a hot `VACUUM INTO` snapshot on a separate read-only connection → chunked AES-256-GCM (per-file HKDF subkey, STREAM-style nonces) streamed straight into a WebDAV `PUT` → retention pruning by file name; key in `/data/backup.key` (never in the DB), exportable from the console, with a warning until it has been; WebDAV password sealed in `system_settings`; minute-tick scheduler (12h/nightly/weekly in `APP_TIMEZONE`, 3 attempts per slot, 30 min apart); alerts as a dot/⚠️ plus inline messages only; restore = decrypt to staging → integrity/schema checks + migrate the staged copy → plaintext safety copy → SQLite online-backup API over the single shared connection, no restart, key adopted if different, run history carried over, OIDC client reloaded.

  Design decisions worth knowing before touching it: **(1)** `backup_runs` rows are only written once a run finishes (the in-progress state is in memory) because the snapshot is taken from the same database — see the migration's own comment. **(2)** WebDAV-side failures in the test/listing endpoints are `200` + `ok:false`/`error`, never `502`/`503`/`504`, which `sw.js`/`apiRequest` read as "Trakka is down". **(3)** `static/sw.js` passes every non-`GET` `/api/v1/admin/backups...` request straight to the network (a multipart restore must never be cloned into memory or queued offline). **(4)** The SSRF guard is public-only by default like the scraper/Web Push, with `BACKUP_WEBDAV_ALLOW_PRIVATE=true` as the operator's opt-in for a LAN/Tailscale Nextcloud; link-local/metadata stays blocked regardless, and redirects are never followed (they'd resend Basic credentials). **(5)** `ParseKey` requires the canonical base32 encoding — found by a flaky handler test: the key's last character carries only 2 data bits and Go's decoder ignores the rest, so some "typos" there decoded to the right key.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt` (on tracked + new files), `go test ./...` all clean; `golangci-lint` v2.13.2 and `gosec` v2.29.0 (CI's pinned versions, installed to a scratch dir) report 0 issues — gosec's two findings on the first draft (G703 path-from-request in the restore handler, G118 `context.Background()` in a goroutine) were fixed, not silenced (see [ci-security.md](ci-security.md)). Test coverage: `internal/backup` (stream format at chunk-boundary sizes, tampering/truncation/trailing data/wrong key, key parsing incl. every last-character variant, key file perms/replace, DST-aware scheduling, dial-guard IP policy, and service tests against an in-test WebDAV server: end-to-end backup→restore, disaster recovery onto a fresh instance with key adoption and a working restored password, retention leaving unrelated files alone, scheduler anchor/retry/cap and the alert lifecycle, config validation), `internal/db` (snapshot + live restore keeping WAL, older-schema migration of the staged copy, the three refusal cases, run queries), `internal/handlers` (403 for a non-admin on all seven routes, validation, write-only password, key export, multipart restore with the file part before the key, error codes). **Browser-verified** in headless Firefox via Selenium against a live server + a `golang.org/x/net/webdav` server (scratch program, not committed): 19 scripted steps — bad/good connection test with translated messages, schedule UI/hints, write-only password placeholder, key alert → download → cleared, manual backup with polling, history + remote list, restore from the remote list with the confirm-twice button (data actually rolled back), restore by uploading `.tkb` + `.key`, malformed key refused, and a 390 px layout check; screenshots reviewed in light/dark, desktop/mobile. (That layout check only measured the panel's own `scrollWidth` and missed a real sideways-panning bug on phones — see the "Fix: sideways panning on phones" entry below.) **Container-verified**: built the Dockerfile image and ran it with `compose.yml`'s hardening (read-only root, 16 MB `/tmp`, UID 10001, no capabilities) — backup, restore via API, and the documented `-decrypt-backup` recovery recipe into a fresh volume followed by signing in on a new container with the restored account and a working restored WebDAV password; all test containers/volumes/images removed afterward. `go test -race` could **not** run locally (no C compiler on this machine) — CI runs it.

- **Fix: sideways panning on phones when opening the admin console's "Sauvegardes" tab (2026-09-29)**: reported as the backups tab letting the whole page slide horizontally on mobile. **Root cause, found by reproducing under Chromium mobile emulation (Playwright, `is_mobile`), not in the backups tab at all**: the dashboard's `#house-toolbar` row ("Maison" label + `#house-select` + rename + "Membres") was 427px wide on any phone narrower than that, because `#house-select` — a flex item — lacked `min-w-0` and so couldn't shrink below its widest option ("+ Créer une Maison"). The mobile browser then widened the *layout viewport* to 427px (`window.innerWidth` 427 on a 393px Pixel 5, even on the plain dashboard), and the admin console, `position: fixed; inset: 0`, spanned that wider viewport — so it could be panned sideways; the existing `body { overflow-x: hidden }` did not prevent this. Inside the backups tab itself, nothing overflowed. Fixes: **(1)** `min-w-0` on `#house-select`, `shrink-0` on the label and "Membres" (`whitespace-nowrap` too), and the "Maison" label visually hidden (`max-[379px]:sr-only`, still announced) under 380px so the house name keeps room on a 320px screen. **(2)** A global guard in `base.css`: `overflow-x: clip` on both `html` and `body` (`hidden` kept as body's fallback) — tested against the alternatives with a deliberately 600px-wide element: body-only `hidden` (before) let the viewport grow to 616px; `html { overflow-x: hidden }` held it at 393px but broke the sticky header (it scrolled off); `clip` on both held 393px *and* kept the header sticky. **(3)** The hardening asked for in the report, all defensive on top of (1): the console dialog `w-full min-w-0 max-w-full sm:max-w-2xl`, its overlay and body `overflow-x-hidden` (body also `min-w-0 max-w-full`), the tab strip `flex-wrap` instead of a sideways-scrolling strip (every tab — and the backups alert dot — now visible without a swipe; one row on desktop, 2–3 on phones), the status grid `grid-cols-[auto_minmax(0,1fr)]`, and a zero-specificity `:where(#admin-panel-backups) …` block in `base.css` (`max-width: 100%`, `min-width: 0`, `box-sizing: border-box` on every control/text block; `overflow-wrap: anywhere` so long URLs/paths/file names in error texts wrap). `:where()` is deliberate so these defaults never override a utility on the element itself — e.g. the retention field keeps its `sm:max-w-[10rem]`. Buttons were capped at the panel width rather than all forced to `w-full`, which would have stretched the small inline ones ("Rafraîchir", "Restaurer…", "Utiliser un fichier à la place"). `sw.js` cache bumped to v99.

  Verified with Chromium mobile emulation at 320, 360, 375, 390, 393 and 412px: on the dashboard and on every console tab (Paramètres/Utilisateurs/Espaces/Logs/Sauvegardes), including the backups tab stuffed with worst-case content injected through its own render functions (three alerts with a 150-character unbroken WebDAV URL, long run errors, a remote backup selected for restore, a long test-result error) and after scrolling to the restore section: `innerWidth` = `clientWidth` = device width, no visible element past the screen edge outside a scroll container, the console body can't be scrolled sideways, the tab strip has no hidden overflow, and the "Sauvegardes" tab is on screen. Before the fix the same check fails (`innerWidth` 427 on 393). Desktop (1280px) unchanged: dialog 672px, tabs on one row, header still sticky, label visible. Not checked on a physical iPhone/Android device. The generalizable lesson is now rule 3 of "PWA lifecycle & mobile layout rules" in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md).

- **Fix: the backups tab's time field spilled past the modal's right edge on phones (2026-09-29)** — the tab strip's `flex-wrap` layout from the previous fix was confirmed good and kept. `#admin-backups-time` now carries `appearance-none` (plus `-webkit-appearance: none`, a left-aligned `::-webkit-date-and-time-value` and a fixed 50px height in `base.css`): with its native appearance, WebKit/iOS Safari gives a time input an intrinsic minimum width and ignores `width: 100%`. Every field of the backups form — URL, username, password, frequency/weekday selects, time, retention — now shares one class structure (`block w-full min-w-0 max-w-full box-border … px-4 py-3 text-base`, 50px tall); the retention field lost its desktop-only `sm:max-w-[10rem]` in the process. Each field's wrapper is `tk-field w-full max-w-full min-w-0 overflow-hidden`, refined in `base.css` to `overflow: clip; overflow-clip-margin: 4px` so the focus ring (drawn outside the field) isn't cut off. `sw.js` cache → v100. Verified in Chromium mobile emulation at 320/390/393/700px: all six fields share the same left/right edges and 50px height, with no internal overflow, and the focused time field keeps its ring and native picker. **Not reproduced beforehand**: Chromium didn't show the overflow, and Playwright's WebKit build can't run on this Fedora machine (missing system libraries), so the iOS Safari side of this fix still needs a check on a real iPhone.

- **Multi-select / bulk actions in the list detail view (2026-09-29)** — new `static/js/selection.js`, frontend-only (no Go/API change). Entered from `#list-options-sheet`'s "Sélectionner des articles" row (`#select-items-button`, plus its `#select-items-button-desktop` twin in `#list-desktop-actions`) or by a long press anywhere on an item card; see the "Multi-select mode" section of [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md) for the design. `#bulk-actions-bar` (fixed, safe-area padded) replaces the quick-add bar: "Annuler", "N sélectionnés", Σ(price × quantity) of the selection on `shopping`/`groceries`/`recurring_shopping` lists (hidden when no selected item has a price), "Tout sélectionner/Tout désélectionner", and the actions Cocher/Décocher (one undo toast), Labels (`#label-manage-sheet`, now generalized to 1..N items with a partial "Bio · 2/5" chip state that merges on tap), Mois (`#bulk-target-month-sheet`, `shopping` only, like the forms' own target-month field) and Supprimer (one undo toast). There is no bulk endpoint: each item still gets its own `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/v1/items/{id}`, at most `BULK_REQUEST_CONCURRENCY` (4) at a time, after an optimistic update of `state.currentList.items` — which is what keeps offline queueing/mirroring/temp-id resolution in `sw.js` working unchanged. **Behavior change**: a long press on an item's *title* used to open `#item-actions-sheet` focused on its link group; it now enters multi-select mode like the rest of the card (a long press on the 🔗 icon itself still opens the link actions, and a tap on the title still opens the sheet). `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to v101.

  Verified: `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt -l .`/`go test ./...` clean; `node --check` on every `static/js/*.js` and `sw.js`; FR/EN locale key and placeholder parity, and every key the new code references resolves. A 70-check Playwright scenario (headless Chromium, 360px mobile emulation with touch, plus 1280px desktop, against a throwaway DB) passed in both FR and EN: entering via the sheet, the desktop button and a synthetic long press; tap/checkbox selection without double toggles; the live total (priced/unpriced/none); select-all with the done section collapsed vs expanded; bulk month apply + clear, bulk labels add/partial-merge/remove, and the single-item label sheet still working; bulk check/uncheck and delete, each with undo and with commit after the 5s grace period; the same bulk month PATCHes queued by the service worker while offline and replayed once back online; `todo` lists hiding the total and month action; no horizontal overflow at 320/360/412px; zero console errors. **Not verified**: a real touchscreen/iOS (the long press was a synthetic `TouchEvent`, and the swipe-vs-long-press guard in `gestures.js` was not exercised), and screen-reader behavior. Noticed while measuring, not caused by this change and not fixed: at 320px the quantity stepper is squeezed to ~58px in normal mode too, clipping its −/+ buttons.

- **Budget summary follows the active filters (2026-09-29)** — frontend-only. While any filter is active, `#finance-summary`'s Total estimé / Déjà dépensé / Reste à dépenser are computed from the items `renderItems` actually shows (after the status filter too) instead of the whole list; with no filter, nothing changes. The 📊 icon is swapped for a sky funnel pill (`#finance-filtered-badge`) and the expanded body gains a note, "Calculé uniquement sur les N articles filtrés sur M" (`#finance-filtered-note`). See "Item filters & the budget summary" in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md). New i18n keys `items.financeFilteredBadge` and `items.financeFilteredNoteOne/Other` in both locales. `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to v102.
