  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server` and `go vet ./...` clean; `node --check` on `list_view.js`/`sw.js`. A 107-check Playwright scenario (headless Chromium, mobile emulation, throwaway DB) passed in FR and EN: amounts with no filter; a label filter; label + status; each status on its own; a price range; a filter that matches nothing; reset; the filter persisting across a reload; one vs several filtered items (singular/plural note); the note re-rendering after a language switch; no console errors. At 320 and 360 px the summary row, open or collapsed, is no wider than without a filter.

- **Emoji picker for list icons (2026-09-29)** — design notes in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md#emoji-picker-list-icon). The new/edit list modal's five fixed preset buttons are replaced by a "Choisir un émoji" button opening `#emoji-picker-sheet` (keyword search in the UI language, 9 category tabs + recents, skin-tone choice, arrow-key navigation), a clear button, and the existing free-text `#list-icon` field kept for pasting anything. The data is `static/emoji/{fr,en}.json` (1,914 emojis each, ~128/112 KB raw, ~39 KB gzipped), generated by the build-ignored `tools/genemoji` from emojibase-data 17.0.0 (MIT, CLDR annotations) and precached by `sw.js` (`v103`). No dependency, no CDN, no CSP change. Backend: no change needed. `validate.Text` keeps ZWJ/VS16/skin tones/tag sequences and 32 code points fits the longest sequences. The one real blocker was the input's own `maxlength="8"` (UTF-16 units), now 32. New tests: `internal/validate/text_test.go`, `internal/handlers/lists_icon_test.go` (create + update round trip of a two-tone ZWJ kiss and the England tag-sequence flag).

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l`, `go test ./...` clean; `node --check` on every `static/js/*.js` and `sw.js`; FR/EN locale key parity and all 27 `emojiPicker.*` keys resolve. A 95-check Playwright scenario (headless Chromium 1243 with Noto Color Emoji, throwaway DB) passed at 320×568 and Pixel 7 (mobile emulation, FR) and 1280×800 (EN, dark): no layout-viewport widening, panel inside the screen, tab/scroll-spy sync, accent/ligature-insensitive search ("coeur" → hearts, "courses" → 🛒), ranking, Escape order, focus return, tone rules 1 and 2, recents, free input of a 15-unit sequence saved and shown on the dashboard card, roving tabindex, language switch rebuild, and a cold offline reload loading the data from the SW cache. The font probe correctly hid the 8 Emoji 17 entries this Noto build can't draw.

- **Long press on mobile: list cards open their actions sheet, no more native text selection on items (2026-09-29)** — frontend-only; design in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md#long-press-touch). (1) A long press on any list card (dashboard, Espaces, "Partagé avec moi") used to select its text; `buildListCard` now attaches `attachLongPress` → `openListCardActionsSheet`, and the sheet gained Modifier/Partager/Supprimer entries for the caller's own lists (pin stays the only entry for shared ones). New `modals.listActions.{edit,share,delete}` keys. (2) A long press on an item entered selection mode but still selected text: `enterSelectionMode` re-renders the rows while the finger is down, and selection-mode rows were built without `long-press-target`. It is now added to every row on touch devices, before the selection-mode early return. `base.css` also resets `user-select: text` on inputs/textareas inside a target. `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to v104.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean; `node --check` on the two edited JS files; locale JSON valid. A 15-check CDP script (chrome-headless-shell, 390px mobile emulation with touch, throwaway DB) passed: long press on a list card opens the sheet with the right entries and does not open the list, no text selection, "Modifier" opens the list modal, a short tap still opens the list; long press on an item enters selection mode and every re-rendered row has `user-select: none`, while the quick-add input stays selectable. **Not verified**: a real iOS/Android device — headless Chromium doesn't run the OS's own long-press text selection, so the selection fix is checked through computed styles, not by reproducing the highlight.

- **Fix: the list-card actions sheet closed as soon as the finger lifted after a long press (2026-09-29)** — frontend-only; design in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md#long-press-touch). The `click` a mobile browser emits on release after a long press landed on the backdrop of the sheet that had just opened under the finger, closing it; the same click deselected the item a long press had just selected in multi-select mode. `attachLongPress` now arms a one-shot capture-phase `click` guard on `window` (`armLongPressClickGuard`) when the long press fires. It's disarmed by the swallowed click, the next `touchstart`, or 400 ms after the lift. The early-lift (ordinary tap) and scroll (`touchmove` past 10 px) cancellations were already in place and are unchanged. `SHELL_CACHE`/`RUNTIME_CACHE` bumped to v105.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...` clean; `node --check list_view.js`. A 13-check CDP script (headless Chromium, 390px touch emulation) reproduced the bug before the fix (sheet closed, item deselected) by dispatching the release click at the finger's position 0/30/150 ms after `touchend` — headless Chromium doesn't emit that click itself — and passed after it: sheet stays open and the list doesn't open, a later backdrop tap still closes it, an early lift still opens the list, the pressed item stays selected and a later tap selects another, a `touchmove` cancels the press. The earlier 15-check long-press script also passes. **Not verified** on a real Android/iOS device.

- **Fix: Android system back closed the installed PWA instead of the open modal or list (2026-09-30)** — frontend-only; design in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md#system-back-history-entries). Trakka never navigates, so an installed PWA had no history entry of its own: Android's back action (gesture-navigation edge swipe or back button) found nothing to pop and closed the app, even with a modal, sheet or list open. Section 3 of `static/js/gestures.js` now gives each open level (the list view, then each overlay) its own same-URL history entry with a `trakkaDepth` state, kept in sync with the DOM by a `MutationObserver` on the overlays' and `#items-section`'s `hidden` attribute. The startup entry is anchored at depth 0. `popstate` closes levels down to the depth it landed on. Manual closes drop extra entries with `history.go(-n)`. Nothing is ever pushed in response to a back, and pushes wait for an honored user activation. `goBack()` now closes only the most recently opened overlay, and the in-app edge swipe goes through the same history step (`requestBack()`). `handleDeepLinkOrRestore` (app.js) keeps `history.state` when it strips `?list=`. `sw.js` `v107`.

  The first version (`v106`, same day) kept a single guard entry re-pushed from `popstate`. It passed a Playwright test but still closed the app on the second back on a real phone (Vanadium, GrapheneOS). Chrome's history manipulation intervention marks every entry of a document skippable when it pushes without an honored activation, and a back stops honoring earlier activation. Playwright's `page.goBack()` bypasses that rule.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .` clean; `node --check`. A 20-check Playwright script (headless Chromium, Pixel 7 touch emulation, throwaway DB, service worker blocked and enabled) models the intervention in an init script: it wraps `pushState` and tracks honored activation and skippable entries per Chromium's doc, and a modeled "system back" exits when everything is skippable. It reproduces the bug on `v106` (the second back from list + sheet exits) and passes on the fix. Scenarios: list + sheet, back ×3 (third exits at the root); emoji picker over the list modal; ✕ close then back; sheet→edit-modal handoff; header back; in-app edge swipe; reload with the list restored. **Not yet done**: confirm on the real phone (installed PWA, gesture navigation) that back closes the open sheet/modal, then the list, and only then exits. Known limit: right after launch, a list reopened by `restoreLastView` has no entry until the first tap, so a back before touching anything still exits.

- **Fix: multi-select mode stayed on with "Aucune sélection" after the last item was deselected (2026-09-30)** — frontend-only, `static/js/selection.js`; design in [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md#multi-select-mode-bulk-actions). Deselecting the last selected row (card tap or its checkbox), "Tout désélectionner", or a render that prunes the last selected row away (deleted/filtered out/sync) now calls `exitSelectionMode()`: the bar is hidden, the rows go back to their normal shape (done checkboxes, swipe, actions), the quick-add bar and the list's own totals come back. Entering through the "Sélectionner" button still starts at 0 selected and stays in the mode. `hideSelectionChrome` also clears the bar's count and total. No CSS change was needed: the bar is hidden only through `[hidden]`, which base.css already forces to `display: none !important`. `sw.js` `v108`.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .` clean; `node --check`. An 11-check Playwright script (headless Chromium, 1280px desktop, throwaway DB, service worker blocked) covers: button entry with 0 selected stays in the mode; select one then deselect it (card tap, then via the checkbox with two selected) exits; "Tout sélectionner" then "Tout désélectionner" exits; a render hiding the only selected item exits; after exit `elementFromPoint` at the bar's old position no longer hits the bar; bulk delete still exits as before. **Not verified** on a real phone (touch entry by long press, then deselecting the pressed item).

- **Recurring tasks, due times & task notifications (2026-09-30)** — design in [backend.md](backend.md#recurring-items) ("Recurring items", "Web Push notifications" use case 2) and [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md) ("Scheduling UI"); API in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#recurring-items). Migration `0021_task_scheduling.sql`: `items.due_time`/`next_due_date`/`notification_sent_at`/`reminder_at_due_time`, `users.reminder_default_at_due_time`, partial index `idx_items_next_due_date`.
  - **Lifecycle change**: checking off a recurring item no longer un-checks it with an advanced date. It stays done ("Terminés") with `next_due_date` = the first occurrence strictly after today (steps from its due date; overdue periods are skipped), and `RunNextOccurrenceScan` (new one-minute `runNextOccurrenceLoop` in `cmd/server/main.go`, runs without VAPID too, and first thing in each reminder scan) brings it back at local midnight of that date, or at its reminder moment if earlier. Un-checking by hand cancels it. `applyRecurrenceCompletion`/`nextDueDate` became `applyRecurrenceLifecycle`/`nextOccurrence`/`stepDueDate`; `sw.js`'s offline port and `planning.js`/`list_view.js` follow (planning projects a done item from `next_due_date`).
  - **Reminders**: optional `due_time`; fourth mode `reminder_at_due_time` (per item and as the account default, "À l'heure exacte de l'échéance", offset/time kept as the no-time fallback); dedup key now includes the due time; `notification_sent_at` exposed read-only; per-item push `tag`; readable French body (`describeDueFR`). `NOTIF_DUE_SCAN_INTERVAL_MINUTES` default 30 → 1 (the scan's per-tick log moved to debug).
  - **Recurrence**: new `EVERY_X_MONTHS:<n>` rule; custom intervals capped (days ≤ 9999, months ≤ 999). UI "Personnalisée…" = every *n* days/weeks/months (weeks sent as days).
  - **UI**: date + time + "Aujourd'hui/Demain/Dans une semaine" chips in the quick-add panel and the edit modal (due date now first); a 📅 pill in the quick-add bar; "🔁 Échéance suivante : dim. 11 oct. à 18:00" under a recurring item's title (active or done, never on a non-recurring item; opens the edit modal on the due date); actions-sheet rows for due date, next occurrence, reminder sent; "🔔 Notifications de tâches" section in Paramètres grouping the push toggle and the reminder default.
  - **Bug fixed on the way**: `PATCH` with `recurrence_rule: ""` also cleared `due_date`, and the edit modal always sends `recurrence_rule` for `todo` lists — so saving any non-recurring task from the modal wiped its due date. Now only `recurrence_end_date` is cleared. Also: server item responses omit empty fields, so `Object.assign` kept stale values; `mergeServerItem` now nulls the schedule fields first (used by `toggleDone`, the edit modal, `bulkToggleDone`).
  - `sw.js` `v109`.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .` clean; `CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` green, with new tests for the lifecycle (`TestApplyRecurrenceLifecycle`, `TestNextOccurrence`, `TestRecurringTaskLifecycle` through the PATCH handler and the scan), `stepDueDate`, `reminderMoment` (Europe/Paris), `occurrenceStart`, `describeDueFR`, `resolveReminderDefaults`, PATCH due-time/recurrence rules, create with at-due-time, `PATCH /me`, DB `TestDueReminderKeyIncludesDueTime`/`TestActivateNextOccurrence`, `EVERY_X_MONTHS` validation (`TestPrepareRestoreFileMigratesOlderSchema` now also undoes migration 21). Playwright (headless Chromium, 390px and 1280px, FR and EN, throwaway DB): quick-add via the pill with tomorrow 18:00, every 2 weeks and "à l'heure de l'échéance" round-trips (`EVERY_X_DAYS:14`, `reminder_at_due_time`); the badge reads "Échéance suivante : demain à 18:00", no badge on a non-recurring task; checking it off shows it in "Terminés" with "jeu. 15 oct. à 18:00" both optimistically and after the commit (server `next_due_date` matches); the badge opens the edit modal focused on the date with the custom recurrence restored; un-checking clears `next_due_date`; a non-recurring task keeps its due date through an edit-modal save (the regression above); settings save the at-due-time preset; offline (service worker active, network off) a check-off gets the same `next_due_date` locally as the server after sync; a recurring-shopping row lays out correctly at both widths. On a real server with VAPID keys: a DAILY task with a passed "veille 00:01" reminder, checked off, came back 45 s later due tomorrow and its reminder was marked sent in the same pass. **Not yet done**: delivery to a real push service/device (no subscription possible here), and a pass on a real phone (native date/time pickers, `showPicker()` from the pill).

- **Custom recurrence: weekdays, interval, start date (RRULE) (2026-09-30)** — design in [backend.md](backend.md#recurring-items) and [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md) ("Custom recurrence sheet"); API in [docs/API.md](../docs/API.md#recurring-items).
  - **Format**: `recurrence_rule` is now a canonical iCalendar RRULE subset — `FREQ=DAILY|WEEKLY|MONTHLY|YEARLY`, `INTERVAL` 1–999, weekly-only `BYDAY`. New stdlib-only package `internal/recurrence` (`Parse`/`String`/`After`); `validate.Recurrence` normalizes every accepted spelling (legacy forms included) to it. Data-only migration `0022_rrule_recurrence.sql` rewrote stored legacy values. `validate.EveryXDaysInterval`/`EveryXMonthsInterval` are gone.
  - **Next occurrence**: BYDAY takes the next listed day later in the same Monday–Sunday week, else the first listed day `INTERVAL` weeks on (Mon/Wed/Fri completed Friday → Monday). Months/years now clamp to the month end (January 31 → February 28) instead of `time.AddDate`'s roll-over (→ March 3) — a behavior change for monthly rules on the 29th–31st. No stored DTSTART: the current `due_date` anchors the series.
  - **One JS port**: `static/js/recurrence.js` (`TrakkaRecurrence`), importScripts-safe, used by `sw.js`, `planning.js` and `list_view.js` — it replaces the three per-file ports from the previous session.
  - **UI**: "Personnalisée…" opens `#recurrence-editor-sheet` (`static/js/recurrence-editor.js`): frequency pills, interval stepper, round L M M J V S D toggles (multi-select), "Date de début", live summary with the first due date. Applying sets the form's due date to the first occurrence on/after the start. A summary button under the select reopens it; cancel/✕/Escape/backdrop/system back restore the select. It replaces the inline "Tous les [n] [jours|semaines|mois]" fields added in the previous session. Badges read "Chaque semaine le lun., mer., ven.", "Du lundi au vendredi", "Tous les 2 ans"…; new `items.weekday*` and `recurrenceEditor.*` i18n keys (FR/EN). `sw.js` `v110`.

  Verified: `go build ./cmd/server`, `go vet ./...`, `gofmt -l .` clean; `CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` green, with new `internal/recurrence` tests (normalization, rejections, `After` incl. BYDAY/intervals/month-end clamp/leap years/DST), handler tests (the Friday→Monday example, overdue BYDAY catch-up, PATCH normalization and `400` for BYDAY on a monthly rule) and `TestMigrationRewritesLegacyRecurrenceRules`; a Node parity script runs the Go `After`/normalization/rejection tables through `recurrence.js`. Playwright (headless Chromium, 390px/1280px, light/dark, FR/EN, touch): legacy `EVERY_X_DAYS:14` comes back `FREQ=WEEKLY;INTERVAL=2`; quick-add → Personnalisée → Mon/Wed/Fri from Friday 2 Oct stores `FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO,WE,FR` due 2 Oct, and checking it off gives `next_due_date` 5 Oct (optimistic badge and server agree); no day selected disables OK; the badge → edit modal shows the summary, reopening restores the pressed days, Escape closes only the sheet; interval 2 and switching to Mensuelle (days hidden, "Tous les 2 mois") behave; cancel restores the previous select value; with the service worker active the shell cache is `v110` and holds both new modules, and an offline Mon/Wed/Fri check-off computes the same `next_due_date` as the server after sync; a history back closes the sheet and leaves the edit modal open. **Not yet done**: a pass on a real phone (native date input inside the sheet, gesture back).

- **Refactor: recurring tasks & reminders cleanup (2026-09-30)** — a strict no-functional-change pass over the two recurrence sessions above: no behavior, UI or API contract change; the one schema change is an index. Design docs updated in [backend.md](backend.md#recurring-items), [frontend-pwa.md](frontend-pwa.md), [architecture.md](architecture.md) and [docs/DATABASE.md](../docs/DATABASE.md).
  - **Go**: the catch-up loop moved into `internal/recurrence` as `Rule.Next`; `handlers.nextOccurrence` is now a thin `YYYY-MM-DD` adapter that parses the rule once (it re-parsed it on each of up to 3660 steps). `stepDueDate` is gone: its legacy-spelling cases moved to `TestAfter`, its error case to `TestNextOccurrence`, and a new `TestNext` covers the method directly. `reminderMoment` moved to `push.go` next to `parseTimeOfDay`; a `dateLayout` constant replaces the `"2006-01-02"` literals in `internal/handlers`; `validateDueTime` holds the due-time check that `handleItemsCreate` and `handleItemsUpdate` both repeated. In `internal/db`, `nullStringPtr`/`nullIntPtr` replace the repeated nullable-column blocks (`scanItem`, `ListItemsAwaitingNextOccurrence`), and `userSelectColumns`/`userCredentialColumns`/`scanUser` replace four copies of the users column list and three copies of its scan code (the SQL is still concatenated from constants only, and gosec stays clean).
  - **Queries**: `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` showed `ListItemsForDueReminderScan`, which runs every minute since the previous session, doing a full `SCAN items`. Migration `0023_due_reminder_index.sql` adds the partial index `idx_items_pending_reminder` (`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, so re-running migrations from an earlier `user_version`, as `TestMigrationRewritesLegacyRecurrenceRules` does, stays harmless), and the plan is now `SEARCH ... USING INDEX`. `TestPrepareRestoreFileMigratesOlderSchema` drops it too. Two things were reviewed and deliberately left as they are. `ActivateNextOccurrence` stays one autocommit `UPDATE` per item: a shared transaction would let one failure affect the rest of the pass, and a tick activates a handful of items at most. The reminder scan's per-item `GetList`/recipient lookups only run for reminders that actually fire, while joining `lists` into the scan query would cost more on every tick.
  - **JS**: `TrakkaRecurrence.localDateISO`/`addDaysISO` replace the copies in `list_view.js` and `sw.js` (`localDateOffline`). `intlLocale()` replaces three copies of the locale expression. `formatDueLabel` reuses cached `Intl.DateTimeFormat`s instead of building one per badge per render. `setItemDoneOptimistic` is shared by `toggleDone` and `bulkToggleDone`. `setTextIfChanged` stops the quick-add pill and recurrence summary, which are refreshed on every `renderItems`, from rewriting identical text. `planning.js` parses a rule once per projection instead of once per step, and its `nextOccurrenceDate` wrapper is gone. `recurrenceBadgeLabel` accepts an already-parsed rule, so the recurrence sheet no longer formats a rule only to parse it again. No debug logging or dead code had been left by the two sessions (`sw.js`'s `[trakka-sync]` `console.debug` lines predate them and are intentional). `sw.js` `v111`.

  Verified: `gofmt -l .` empty, `go vet ./...` clean, `go test ./...` and `CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race ./...` green, `golangci-lint run` (v2.13.2, the CI pin) 0 issues, `gosec ./...` clean, `go mod verify`, `go build ./cmd/server`. A Node script checked that the shared date helpers return the same values as the removed copies on 70,128 cases, in both Europe/Paris and America/Los_Angeles. A Playwright before/after comparison ran the same scenario against a `git archive` of the pre-refactor commit and against the refactored tree, each on a fresh database (headless Chromium, 390px, EN UI, service worker active). The scenario covered the quick-add pill and "tomorrow" chip, the custom recurrence sheet (weekly, a day, interval, apply), create, a single toggle (optimistic, committed, and the server's `next_due_date`), undo, a bulk toggle, badge → edit modal → sheet → Escape, the actions sheet, the planning projection, the settings preset, and an offline `PATCH` through the service worker. Both runs produced byte-identical output across 27 captured states, with no console errors. Not re-checked on a real device: nothing device-specific changed.

### What's left / open points

- **The emoji picker was only exercised in emulated Chromium** — worth a pass on a real iPhone (Safari + home-screen PWA) and Android: the on-screen keyboard over the bottom sheet once the search field is tapped, the canvas font probe against Apple Color Emoji / Samsung's font (which versions it hides), and scroll inertia inside the grid. The space (category) modal still has its fixed preset buttons; wiring it to `TrakkaEmojiPicker.open` is a few lines if wanted.
- **The long-press fixes (list-card actions sheet, release-click guard, no text selection on items) were only exercised in emulated Chromium** — on a real iPhone and Android phone, check that holding a list card opens the sheet, that it stays open once the finger lifts, with no text highlight or callout, that holding an item enters selection mode with no blue highlight, and that fields in the sheets/modals still accept typing.
- **Multi-select mode was only exercised in emulated Chromium** — try it on a real phone (Android Chrome and iOS Safari): long press on a card to enter (and that iOS shows no text-selection callout), long press then drag must not swipe-complete/delete the card, the bar clearing the home indicator in standalone PWA mode, and the undo toasts sitting above the bar.
- **Encrypted WebDAV backups were only exercised against `golang.org/x/net/webdav` (and an in-test fake), never a real Nextcloud/ownCloud/Synology** — worth one real run: an app password, a folder URL with a URL-encoded path segment (spaces), a backup large enough to take several seconds (Nextcloud's own upload/chunking limits), and retention against the server's real `PROPFIND` output. Also not yet seen: a real scheduled run at its wall-clock slot (the scheduler logic is unit-tested with a fake clock), a restore through a reverse proxy (body-size/timeout limits — see DEPLOYMENT.md), and the UI on an actual phone. Known nit: after an offline `-decrypt-backup` recovery, the console shows "key not saved" until the key is exported once from the new instance (the backup predates the export being recorded).
- **The `/sw.js` `Cache-Control: no-cache` fix (and the manual "Vérifier les mises à jour" button) above have never been confirmed against a real mobile device** — see the dedicated bullet under "What's done" for the full reasoning and the exact scenario to verify: deploy a change, then on a real phone (iOS Safari especially) confirm the update banner now appears via background/resume, refocus, the 15-minute interval, or the new manual button — and confirm the manual button's three other outcomes (up to date / offline-error / unsupported) and the Settings version line all behave correctly.
- **The scroll-position-reset fix above (the actual, confirmed root cause of the "⋮" Options button not responding on mobile) has never been exercised on a real device** — see its own bullet under "What's done" for the exact scenario to verify: scroll down on the dashboard before tapping a list card, confirm the list detail view opens already scrolled to its own top; confirm the reverse (leaving a scrolled list back to the dashboard); confirm the same for switching between dashboard tabs. If the button still doesn't respond after this, capture whether `#list-options-sheet`'s `hidden` attribute actually flips in a remote DevTools session when tapped — that isolates "click handler didn't fire" from "sheet opened but isn't visible."
- **The header status/service-worker-update fix above has never been exercised in a browser or on a real device** — see its own bullet under "What's done" for the exact two scenarios to verify (a stopped-then-restarted backend recovering the header's status within 30s or immediately on refocus/resume, and a deployed update surfacing the banner within 15 minutes or immediately on refocus/resume, without a manual reload in either case).
- **The mobile touch-tap hardening fix above needs real-device confirmation, and the root cause is still unconfirmed** — see its own bullet under "What's done": no functional JS/CSS bug was found in the `#list-options-button`/`#list-options-sheet` wiring via static review, so the applied fix (`touch-action: manipulation` on every button, with a `.swipe-item button { touch-action: pan-y }` carve-out for item-row buttons) is defensive hardening, not a confirmed root-cause correction. On the next report of this (or any button not responding to a tap), get a remote-DevTools/console session on the actual device first — specifically whether the sheet's `hidden` attribute flips when tapped (proves the click handler fired) — before assuming this fix didn't address it.
- **The mobile list-header redesign (Options sheet + new Filters sheet) above has never been exercised in a browser** — see its own bullet above under "What's done" for the exact scenarios to verify (the Options sheet's four relocated rows, Statut/label/price-range filtering actually narrowing the list, the filter badge dot, "Réinitialiser les filtres", and the new "Urgence" sort option).

- **The dashboard order/count consistency fix above has never been exercised in a browser** — see its own bullet above under "What's done" for the exact two-part scenario to verify (list order matching between offline and online renders, and a stale item mirrored on one device correcting itself once that device syncs after another device/session deleted it).

- **The edit-item modal's overflow/scroll fix above has never been exercised in a browser** — see its own bullet above under "What's done" for the exact scenario to verify (a short/narrow viewport, the header staying fixed while the form scrolls, scrollbar alignment, and centering in both the scrolling and non-scrolling case).

- **Dynamic item sort has never been exercised in a browser** — see the dedicated bullet above under "What's done" for the exact scenarios to verify (each of the five non-default orders, the active/done split, the ✓/highlight state, the price rows hiding on a `todo`/`custom` list, and per-list persistence across a reload).

- **The Admin Console restructuring has never been exercised in a browser** — see the dedicated bullet above under "What's done" for the exact scenarios to verify (the header button's removal, opening the console from Settings, all four tabs' rendering and actions including the confirm-delete interaction, and the offline `503` behavior on the new admin write endpoints).
- **Item labels + Compact/Detailed view mode has never been exercised in a browser** — see the dedicated bullet above under "What's done" for the exact scenarios to verify (the view-mode toggle's collapse/expand, chip-row-vs-mini-badge rendering, the label-management sheet's search/create/toggle flow, and mobile alignment in Compact mode).

- **The server-side `total_amount` field (dashboard/Espaces/"Partagé avec moi" card total, now "reste à dépenser") has never been exercised in a browser** — see the dedicated "Dashboard/Espaces card `total_amount` now excludes done items..." bullet above: confirm a card's green badge amount drops (or the badge disappears entirely) the moment an item on that list is checked off, matches the list detail view's own "Reste à dépenser" figure exactly, and that a list with every priced item checked off shows no badge at all rather than a stale or zero one.
- **The dashboard/Espaces price-badge color fix has never been exercised in a browser** — see the dedicated "Fix: dashboard/Espaces list-card price-total badge used a neutral gray style..." bullet above for the exact scenario to verify: open the dashboard/Espaces/"Partagé avec moi" tabs in both Light and Dark mode and confirm every priced list card's total badge renders green (not gray).
- **The offline-error-banner fix (`X-Trakka-Offline` header tagging + the ~24 newly-guarded `showError` call sites) has never been exercised in a browser** — see the dedicated "Fix: a genuinely offline device still surfaced a blocking 'hors ligne' error banner..." bullet above for the full root cause and the exact scenario to verify: go offline with the service worker already controlling the page, open Membres/admin settings/a share modal/the push toggle, and confirm none of them flash an error banner, while a genuine online functional error (400/403) still does.
- **The mobile item-creation redesign (sticky bottom quick-add bar) has never been exercised on a real device or in a browser** — see the dedicated "Mobile item-creation redesign" bullet above under "What's done" for the exact scenarios to verify: the bar staying pinned above the on-screen keyboard/gesture area, the last item in a long list scrolling fully clear of it, back-to-back Enter-driven adds never dismissing the keyboard, and the `md:` desktop layout being pixel-unchanged from before this session.

- **Web Push notifications have never been exercised against a real browser or a real push service** — see the dedicated "Web Push notifications" bullets in the Architecture and "What's done" sections above for the full design and a concrete verification recipe (generate keys, enable the toggle, trigger a shared-list change from a second account, and a recurring item's due-date reminder). The `internal/webpush` encryption/signing implementation itself is verified against RFC 8291's own published test vector (see `TestEncryptPayloadMatchesRFC8291Example`), which is a strong signal it's spec-correct, but that's still not the same as a real browser actually receiving and displaying a notification — no browser automation tooling was available in the sandbox this was built in, and the async delivery path can't be integration-tested even with tooling since the SSRF guard correctly refuses to dial a local test server. Also still open: `items.recurrence_lead_minutes` (the per-item lead-time override) has no frontend input anywhere yet — it's reachable only via a direct API call, the same gap `EVERY_X_DAYS:<n>` already had before this session.

- **OIDC was never tested against a real identity provider.** The hand-rolled discovery/PKCE/JWKS/RS256-verification code builds and follows the spec as implemented, but no OIDC provider (Authelia, Keycloak, Google, ...) was available to actually exercise `/auth/oidc/login` → `/auth/oidc/callback` end-to-end in the sandbox that built it. Treat this path as unverified until it's been run against a real provider at least once — set up a local Authelia/Keycloak via Docker (or a Google OAuth test client) and walk through a full login before relying on it.
- **The Docker image build itself was not verified** for the same reason (no Docker daemon available) — neither the original `templates/` `COPY` addition nor the later distroless/`read_only` hardening has actually been built and run. Before deploying: `docker compose up -d --build` (or `podman-compose up -d --build`) and confirm the container starts cleanly, `/healthz` passes, and an item create/read round-trip works with `read_only: true` in effect — that's the setting most likely to surface a real problem (e.g. anything unexpectedly trying to write outside `/data`/`/tmp`), more so than the base-image swap itself.
- **No UI to rename/delete a house, or to leave a house you're a member of** — the dashboard exposes creating a house and managing membership (invite/remove), but `PUT`/`DELETE /api/v1/houses/{id}` and "leave house" (self-`DELETE` on `/members/{userId}`) exist and are documented but aren't wired to any dashboard button yet (they were already true of house rename/delete before this feature; "leave house" is new).
- **No account deletion/management UI or endpoint** — a user can't currently delete their own account, change their password, or change their email from within the app.
- **Nothing has been committed yet** beyond the initial commit (`1a74e2d Initial commit`): every project file shows up as untracked/modified in `git status`. Need to decide how to split the commits (one big commit per feature area, or several smaller logical commits) before pushing anything.
- **No test suite for most of the app**: `internal/scraper` and `internal/db/items_test.go` now have real Go unit tests (see the automatic-price-lookup entry above), but everything else still has none, and there are no JS tests (though [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md) explains how to test `sw.js`/`db.js` outside a browser via `fake-indexeddb` + `vm` — that approach hasn't actually been set up in the repo yet). Given how security-sensitive the OIDC/session code is, that's a stronger candidate for coverage than most of the remaining app.
- **The automatic price & image lookup was never exercised against a real product page** — only against `example.com` (no price/image present, so only the "nothing found" path ran) and a rejected `127.0.0.1` (confirming the SSRF guard). Point it at a real Shopify/Amazon/Fnac-style listing at least once to confirm the OpenGraph/JSON-LD/microdata/Twitter-Card parsing actually matches something in the wild, not just the synthetic HTML fixtures in `scraper_test.go` — and check the thumbnail/lightbox UI (`buildItemThumbnail`, `#image-preview-modal` in `static/js/list_view.js`) in an actual browser, which also hasn't happened yet.
- **CalDAV/Radicale integration doesn't exist yet**: `compose.yml` can start a Radicale container (`calendar` profile), but Trakka doesn't talk to it — no `.ics`/CalDAV export of `todo` lists. `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` explicitly describes this as "a separate, not-yet-implemented piece of work".
- **No TLS reverse proxy provided**: `compose.yml` serves plain HTTP by design (PWA/offline only work on `localhost` or behind TLS, see [docs/PWA.md](../docs/PWA.md)) — putting a Caddy/Traefik/nginx in front is left to the final deployment, not included in this repo. This now matters more than before: `SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE` should stay `true` (the default) in any real deployment, which requires HTTPS in front for the session cookie to actually get sent.
- **The new CI/CD pipeline has never actually run on GitHub's runners** — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` was written, and every Go-side tool it calls (`golangci-lint`, `gosec`, `govulncheck`, `go test -race -cover`) was run locally against the current tree and confirmed clean, plus the YAML's structure (jobs/steps/permissions) was validated with a small standalone parser, but no push or PR was made in the sandbox that built this, so the Docker build, both Trivy scans, the SARIF uploads to the Security tab, and the build-provenance attestation are all unverified beyond local reasoning. Push a commit or open a PR and watch the Actions tab before trusting it fully — see .claude/ci-security.md's "CI/CD & Sécurité" section for the full pipeline design.
- **`templates/login.html` is still French-only** — it's server-rendered via `html/template` and never loads `i18n.js`, so the FR/EN toggle added this session doesn't reach the login/register page at all.
- **No Go test coverage for `target_month`**: `internal/validate.Month` and the item handlers'/`internal/db`'s handling of it were only verified live (curl + two headless-Chrome sessions), not with `go test`. `internal/validate` has no tests at all yet (not even for the pre-existing `URL` validator), so adding a `validate_test.go` would be a good place to start.
- **The planning view always fetches every shopping list's items on open/house-switch/sync** (the same `?house_id=` + per-list fan-out `loadDashboard` already uses) rather than a dedicated server-side query — fine at the scale this app targets, but if a house ever has many shopping lists this is the first place to optimize (e.g. a `GET /api/v1/items?house_id=&from=&to=` endpoint).
- **A non-recurring task's due date shows only in the actions sheet and the edit modal, not on its row** — the "Recurring tasks, due times & task notifications" session's spec asked for the row badge on recurring tasks only. A plain due-date pill on every due-dated row would be a small follow-up if wanted.
- **Urgent items' frontend was never exercised in a browser** — no browser automation tooling (Playwright or similar) was available in the sandbox that built it. Only the backend (Go tests + a live curl walkthrough) was verified. Check the checkbox in both the create form and edit modal, the 🚨 badge, the sort-to-top of unfinished urgent items within a list, and the "Urgent" dashboard tab's cross-list fan-out/mark-done/click-through-to-list before relying on any of it.
- **Price alerts / deal notifications were never verified live** — no curl walkthrough (no product page with an actual, triggerable price drop was available in the sandbox this was built in) and no browser verification of the 🔔 bell/badge/drawer. Before relying on it: (1) manually insert a `price_alerts` row (or point an item's `url` at a page you control and lower the listed price) and confirm `GET /api/v1/price-alerts?house_id=&status=pending` returns it, (2) accept one and confirm the item's `price`/`price_auto` update, reject another and confirm the item is untouched, (3) exercise the bell/badge/drawer in an actual browser, including the optimistic-with-rollback behavior on a forced request failure. Also consider trying `POST /api/v1/items/{id}/price-check` against a real listing to confirm the scraper's price-comparison path works end-to-end the same way the original automatic-price-lookup feature's "never tested against a real site" caveat above still does.
